Before a server is switched off or renumbered: every DNS record, CNAME chain, SPF mechanism, MX / NS host, HTTPS address hint and imported-zone record that still reaches its address. DOM-free; DNS through the injected client (`dns.query(name, type, { signal })`, `dns.resolveHost(name, { signal })`: a DohClient, whose cache makes a repeated check cheap). Nothing is sent by importing it; the addresses are only compared here.
```js
export const RETIRE_MAX_ADDRESSES = 256, RETIRE_MIN_PREFIX = { 4: 24, 6: 120 }, RETIRE_MAX_DOMAINS = 25, RETIRE_MAX_HOSTS = 1000 /* every domain together */,
  RETIRE_MAX_MX = 10, RETIRE_MAX_NS = 13, RETIRE_MAX_ZONE_REFS = 1000 /* zone records verified live */, PASSIVE_MAX_ADDRESSES = 8, PASSIVE_SOURCES = ['hackertarget', 'thc'],
  FAILURE_KINDS = ['name', 'mx', 'ns', 'spf', 'https']
export const SEVERITIES = ['mail', 'ns', 'live', 'origin', 'chain', 'file', 'stale', 'unknown'], VERIFIED_STATES = ['live', 'file', 'hidden', 'internal', 'unverified', 'unknown'],
  CHANGE_ACTIONS = ['remove', 'repoint', 'narrow', 'follow', 'glue', 'provider', 'origin', 'keep', 'check'], UNKNOWN_REASONS = ['macro', 'ptr', 'lookup-failed', 'skipped', 'include-failed', 'multiple'],
  HOST_SOURCES = ['scan', 'zone', 'passive', 'discovered'], TARGET_ISSUES = ['invalid', 'too-large', 'host-bits', 'over-cap', 'private', 'nothing'],
  RETIRE_CSV_COLUMNS = ['group', 'severity', 'name', 'type', 'value', 'address', 'action', 'verified', 'via', 'sources', 'line']
export function parseRetireTargets(text, { max = 256 }) -> { kind: 'empty'|'addresses', blocks: RetireBlock[], addresses /* every single address */, total, issues: [{ code, severity, params }], ok, label }
  // addresses (brackets, a mapped IPv4 → IPv4) and networks up to a /24 (IPv6 /120; wider: too-large, error); host bits masked (host-bits, info); a block inside
  // another counts once; over `max` distinct addresses: over-cap (error); junk: invalid (warn); private space kept with a `private` note (public resolvers
  // do not see split-horizon records); '#' comments. RetireBlock = { input, cidr, label, version, network, prefix, size, first, last, single }
export function parseDomainList(text, { max = 25 }) -> { domains, invalid, truncated }   // host names (URL, trailing dot, '*.' cleaned); addresses and single labels invalid
export function retireTokens(text) ; rangeOf(address, prefix?) ; blockOf(address, blocks)
export function rangeRelation(range, block) -> 'equal'|'contains'|'within'|null   // CIDR blocks nest or do not touch; never across families
export function effectiveQualifier(chain /* [{ kind: 'include'|'redirect', qualifier }] outermost first */, qualifier) -> '+'|'-'|'~'|'?'|null
  // what a term gives the checked domain (RFC 7208 §5.2): through an include only a pass counts, then the include's qualifier; a redirect passes it on; null = no match
export function spfCoverage(tree /* spfLookupCount().tree, §5.15 extension */, blocks, { mxAddresses /* host → { addresses, error } */ }) -> { matches: SpfMatch[], unknown: SpfUnknown[] }
  // ip4 / ip6: the range against each block; a / mx: each address of the named host (MX hosts from mxAddresses) widened by the term's CIDR length (a/24);
  // include / redirect followed with the path. "Cannot tell", never "not covered": a macro that needs the sender (%{i}, %{s}, %{l} …; %{d} / %{o} are
  // expanded by health.js), ptr, a failed or skipped lookup, an include whose policy could not be read. exists without such a macro does not depend on the address.
  // Term order (the first match wins): within one policy, the first ip4 / ip6 / a / mx term that covers a whole block decides for it — a later match gets
  // `shadowedBy` ({ term, qualifier }), and a later pass term joins the earlier match's `shields` (an include in between, or a term that cannot be told, is not weighed).
  // SpfMatch = { path, holder, record, term, mechanism, qualifier, effective, relation, block, range, via: { host, address }|null, shadowedBy, shields };
  // SpfUnknown = { path, holder, record, term, mechanism, reason, target }
export function spfMxHosts(tree) -> string[]   // lib/health.js spfMxHosts (§5.15), re-exported
export async function checkDomain(domain, { dns, blocks, hosts /* [{ name, source }] */, signal, onLookup(done, total) }) -> DomainCheck
  // its own name, the known hosts, MX (≤ 10) and NS (≤ 13) hosts, each resolved; its SPF tree (spfLookupCount) and the addresses of every host an mx
  // mechanism names; the HTTPS record's ipv4hint / ipv6hint. DomainCheck = { domain, names: [{ name, status, cnames, ipv4, ipv6, error, errorKind,
  // roles: 'apex'|'host'|'mx'|'ns'|'spf', sources }], mx, ns, spf: { status: 'ok'|'none'|'failed'|'multiple', record, lookups, matches, unknown }, https, failures,
  // missing /* its own name NXDOMAIN without a CNAME (a dangling alias exists) and no name server: the domain does not exist, a typo in the list most likely */ }.
  // A failed lookup is a failure (never "nothing points here"); a domain SPF record that could not be read is also an `unknown` entry
  // (mechanism 'record', reason lookup-failed), so it is a "cannot tell" row, never "no SPF"; only an abort rejects.
export function zoneCandidates(records /* §5.22 referenceRecords */, blocks) -> ZoneRef[]   // A / AAAA with the address, CNAMEs whose in-zone chain ends at one,
  // MX / NS / SRV / HTTPS / SVCB whose in-zone target holds one (a DNS-only CNAME into a proxied name takes its proxy status), HTTPS / SVCB hints, SPF ip4 / ip6;
  // `targetInternal`: the target or a name on its in-zone chain looks internal
export async function verifyZoneRefs(refs, { dns, signal, max = 1000, labelFn = randomLabel }) -> ZoneRef[] with `live`: true / false / null (failed)
  // does public DNS still serve each? A wildcard owner `*.x` is asked at a random name under it (`<label>.x`, one per owner, kept as `probe`), like the
  // Zone File live check, and is a record like any other (live / file / unverified — never "zone file only" without an answer that says so). A proxied
  // record's origin and an internal-looking name are never asked (`live` undefined); a served record whose target looks internal is live without its
  // target being asked; past `max` records to ask, the rest keep `live` undefined with `capped: true`
export function knownHostsFor(domain, { scan, zone, passive, discovered }) -> [{ name, source }]   // names equal to / under the domain, first source wins
export async function runRetireCheck({ blocks, domains, hosts, zoneRefs, dns, signal, concurrency = 2, onEvent }) -> { checks, errors, zone, aborted }
  // events: start, progress, domain (a check, or its error), zone; an abort keeps what finished
export function retireGaps({ domains, checks, errors, zone, aborted, counts }) -> { failed, failures: { name, mx, ns, spf, https, domain, zone }, unknown, notChecked, missing, stopped, settled }
  // what the check could not settle: failed lookups (a domain's, a domain that could not be checked, a zone record's live lookup), "cannot tell" rows,
  // the domains a stop left unchecked, the checked domains that do not exist (`missing`). Only a settled check may say that nothing points at the address
export function inventoryOwners(blocks, servers) -> [{ name, addresses, others }]
export function buildChanges({ blocks, checks, zone: { origin, refs }|null, passive: [{ address, names }]|null }) -> { changes: Change[], groups,
  counts: { total, breaking, passive /* rows of the passive group */, bySeverity, byVerified }, gone }
export function breakingChanges(changes) ; passiveNewNames(passive, { checked, resolved }) -> { names, domains }
export function retireExportRows(changes) ; retireExportJson({ … }) -> { schema: 'domainscope.ip-retire/1', app, version, addresses, domains, missingDomains, zone, startedAt, finishedAt, aborted, counts, owners, changes, passiveGone, failures }
```
- **One row per record** (`Change`, key owner|type|value) with the retiring addresses it reaches, its evidence merged (`sources`: dns, spf, zone — the file holds the record —, passive and where a known host came from: scan, zone-name, discovered; `roles`; `foundFor`: the checked domains that led to it): the A / AAAA record that holds the address (the end of a CNAME chain), the CNAME that leads there, the MX / NS record whose host is on it, the HTTPS hint, each SPF term that covers it, each SPF term that cannot be told, the zone's records, the unchecked passive names.
- **Severity**, worst first: `mail` (an SPF term whose effective qualifier is a pass — renumbered, mail from the new address fails; switched off, whoever gets the address next can send as the domain — an MX record on it, the A record of an MX host) > `ns` (a name server's record and its A) > `live` (an A / AAAA or HTTPS hint that answers it) > `origin` (a proxied record's origin, from the zone file) > `chain` (a CNAME) > `file` (the zone has it, public DNS does not) > `stale` (an SPF term that does not authorize it — also a pass term behind an earlier term of its policy that does not pass, which decides for the address first —, a passive hit nobody checked) > `unknown`. `breaking` counts mail to chain.
- **What to change** (`action`): remove (or put the new address in), repoint (MX, NS, SRV, an HTTPS target: a host that stays, or that host's new address first), narrow (an SPF range wider than the retired addresses; an a / mx term whose CIDR length widens its host's address over them — `a/24`, `mx/24` —, whose text names the retiring address, the host and its address, and says whether moving the host is enough), follow (an SPF a / mx term whose host's own address is on the block and whose range is not wider than it, or a CNAME: change the record it names), glue (an in-bailiwick name server: its glue at the registrar too), provider (a term in the SPF policy of a domain that is neither under a checked domain nor the zone's origin — the text says to change it there if that domain is the user's, and that a provider's needs nothing but the include; a central `_spf` policy under any checked domain is the user's to narrow or remove), origin (a proxied origin: the proxy's origin setting, else 521 / 522), keep (an SPF term that does not authorize the address and either is a carve-out in front of a later pass term of its policy — `-ip4:192.0.2.10 ip4:192.0.2.0/24`: removed, the range would authorize the address — or covers more than the retired addresses), check (cannot tell, a passive hit). A term behind an earlier one that does not pass says it is not in effect today, and to remove or narrow it all the same: left in place, it authorizes the address as soon as the earlier one goes.
- **Verification**: live (seen over DoH; a wildcard zone record: at the random name under it, which its evidence names), file (zone only: public DNS answered without it), hidden (behind the proxy), internal (never asked), unverified (a passive hit, a zone record over the cap or left by a stop — with its type's severity), unknown (failed or depends on the sender).
- **Groups**: the checked domain the owner is under (the zone's origin as its own group when it was not in the list), `other` (a record in a zone nobody checked: a CNAME target, a provider's SPF include — each row names the domain that led to it), `passive`. `gone`: passive names whose lookup settled (an answer or NXDOMAIN) and that no longer point at the address; a passive name whose lookup failed stays in the passive group as `unknown` (lookup-failed), never gone.
- Tests: `tests/js/retire.test.js` (CIDR math, SPF coverage through includes, macros and failed lookups, one domain over a fake DohClient, a failed SPF record lookup, what a check could not settle, a domain that does not exist, a/24 and mx/24 terms, the first match of a policy (a carve-out in front of a wider range, in the live policy and the zone file), the zone's candidates and their verification — internal targets, a wildcard through a random name, the cap —, the merged list, SPF policies under checked domains, an abort, owners, passive hits — a SERVFAIL one —, exports), `tests/js/retire-view.test.js`, `tests/e2e/retire.e2e.mjs` (offline).

### 5.46 `lib/passport.js` — the Domain overview, one page per domain
For a migration or a customer takeover: the registration, DNS hosting, mail, web, certificates, SaaS verifications and health of one domain, the facts otherwise gathered from five or six tools. DOM-free: lookups through an injected DohClient and `fetchImpl` with the caller's `signal` (only an abort rejects), card data as codes the view translates.
```js
export const PASSPORT_CARDS = ['registration', 'dns', 'mail', 'web', 'certs', 'saas', 'health']
export const PASSPORT_LOOKUPS = ['rdap', 'ns', 'soa', 'ds', 'dnskey', 'mx', 'txt', 'dmarc', 'apex', 'www', 'https', 'wwwHttps', 'caa', 'health']
export const CARD_LOOKUPS   // card → its lookups (health: ['health', 'rdap'], its score includes the registration checks); a card's Retry asks only its failed ones
export const HEALTH_LOOKUPS = ['ns', 'soa', 'ds', 'dnskey', 'mx', 'txt', 'dmarc', 'apex', 'https', 'caa']   // the lookups whose questions the health run asks too
export const LOOKUP_SOURCES  // lookup → 'rdap' | 'doh'
export function passportDomain(input) -> { domain, host } | null   // the registrable domain of a host, URL or *.name; host set when reduced; null for IPs, suffixes, junk
export const DNS_PROVIDERS   // NS host → provider by suffix or pattern: Cloudflare (incl. Foundation DNS), Route 53, Azure DNS, Google Cloud DNS, NS1, Akamai, UltraDNS, Dyn,
                             // Oracle, Microsoft 365, DigitalOcean, Hetzner, OVHcloud, GoDaddy, Namecheap, IONOS, STRATO, Gandi, Hostinger, Natro, Turhost, İsimtescil, Wix,
                             // Squarespace, Vercel, Linode, Vultr, Porkbun, ClouDNS, DNS Made Easy, Hurricane Electric, deSEC, Yandex, Alibaba, DNSPod, Network Solutions, WordPress.com
export function dnsProviderOf(nsHost, { domain }) -> { id, name } | { id: 'self' } | null ; export function dnsHosting(nsHosts, { domain }) -> { providers: [{ id, name, hosts }], self, other }
export const MAIL_PLATFORMS  // { id, name, kind: 'mailbox'|'gateway'|'forwarding'|'sending', mx (suffixes), mxPatterns, spf (include suffixes) }: Microsoft 365, Google Workspace,
                             // Zoho, Yandex 360, Yaani, Türk Telekom, Mail.ru, Proton, iCloud, Fastmail, …, Mimecast, Proofpoint, Barracuda, Cisco, …, SendGrid, Mailgun, Amazon SES, …
export function mailPlatformOf(exchange) -> { id, name, kind } | null ; export function spfSenders(spf) -> { senders: [{ id, name, kind }], other: string[] }   // include: / redirect=, macro includes by suffix
export const TXT_VENDORS     // { id, name, key, re }: ~100 verification-token prefixes, anchored at the record's start (google-site-verification, MS=, facebook-, apple-, atlassian-,
                             // docusign=, stripe-, ZOOM_verify_, adobe-idp-, hubspot-, openai-, anthropic-, …); `key` is the fixed prefix, the only part ever shown
export function txtVendorOf(record) -> { id, name, key } | null ; export function saasFingerprints(records) -> { vendors: [{ id, name, key, count }], other, policy, total }   // never a token
export const REGISTRY_WHOIS = { tr: TRABİS, de: DENIC, jp: JPRS, ch: SWITCH } ; export function registryWhois(tld, domain) -> { name, url, iana } | null   // else the TLD's IANA page
export function rdapStatusFlags(statuses) -> [{ code, kind: 'lock'|'hold'|'pending'|'ok'|'other' }]   // `code` as the registry spelled it (RFC 8056 or EPP camelCase), matched case-free
export function serialDate(serial) -> 'YYYY-MM-DD' | null   // YYYYMMDDnn only
export function lookupStatus(result, { now }) -> SourceStatus | null   // §5.36: a DoH failure or error rcode, a failed RDAP lookup or host resolution, a lookup that threw
export function registrationCard / dnsCard / mailCard / webCard / certsCard / saasCard / healthCard(raw, { now }) -> Card ; export function passportCards(raw, { now }) -> { [card]: Card }
Card = { id, state: 'pending'|'ready', pending: string[], failures: (SourceStatus & { lookup })[], retry: string[], …the card's own fields }
  // registration: outcome 'ok'|'unsupported' (whois)|'not-found'|'invalid'|'failed', registrar, ianaId, created, expires, daysLeft, expiry ('expired'|'error' <30 d|'warn' <60 d|'ok'),
  //   flags, transferLock (true | false | null when the registry reports no status), dnssec, nameservers
  // dns: exists, nameservers, hosting, delegation (the registry's NS set when it differs from the zone's), soa { mname, email, serial, serialDate, provider },
  //   dnssec 'validated'|'signed'|'failing' (DS, the DNSKEY lookup failed)|'unsigned'|null
  // mail: mx { state 'some'|'none'|'null', hosts, platforms, other }, spf { state 'ok'|'none'|'many'|'invalid', all, redirect, senders, other }, dmarc { state, policy, pct, reports }
  // web: exists, hosts [apex, www] { state 'ok'|'nxdomain'|'nodata'|'dangling'|'failed', cnames, ipv4, ipv6, classification (netinfo), aliasOfApex, sameAsApex }, https { apex, www }
  // certs: exists, caa { state, issue, issuewild, wildcardOnly (an issuewild property exists), foundAt, iodef, criticalTags }, ct (after lookupCtIssuers) { state, issuers with health.checkCaaAllows' verdict, notAllowed }
  //   caa.state as checkCaaAllows reads the set (RFC 8659 §4): 'none' (no CAA) | 'critical' (an unknown tag marked critical: no CA may issue) | 'unrestricted' (no issue
  //   property — iodef / issuewild / other tags only: any CA for the name, issuewild still rules wildcards) | 'deny-all' (no issue or issuewild value authorises anybody) |
  //   'present' (issue names CAs; `issue` empty when only issuewild does: wildcard certificates only)
  // saas: the saasFingerprints of the apex TXT; health: the report with applyRdap, score, light, summary, the worst three problems (+ moreProblems); pending until RDAP landed too
export function cardsOfLookup(lookup) -> string[]   // the cards to redraw when it lands: its own, the DNS card for RDAP, every card that says NXDOMAIN for NS / SOA
export function passportDns(dns, { signal, noCache }) -> client   // one question once per build; the Domain Health run shares it; resolveHost / detectWildcard go to the DohClient's own
export async function runLookup(id, domain, { dns, fetchImpl, signal, now }) -> result   // a thrown lookup comes back as { failed: true, error, errorKind }
export async function buildPassport(domain, { dns, fetchImpl, signal, now, lookups = PASSPORT_LOOKUPS, noCache, onLookup }) -> raw   // all at once, onLookup(id, result) as each lands
export function certspotterIssuersUrl(domain) ; export function crtshIssuersUrl(domain)
export function issuersFromCertspotter(rows, { now }) / issuersFromCrtsh(rows, { now }) -> { issuers: [{ name, dn, intermediates, caaDomains, count, newest }], certificates }
export function issuerName(dn, friendly) -> string   // the known CA (health.CAA_ISSUERS), else the DN's O, else its CN — the name an issuer is grouped under
export function dnPart(dn, key) -> string|null        // one attribute of a DN string (`,` or `/` separated); both also used by the headless runner's `ct` (§9)
export async function lookupCtIssuers(domain, { fetchImpl, signal, now, cooldown = ctcert.ctCooldown }) -> { status: 'ok'|'failed', provider, issuers, certificates, requests, failures }
export function passportSummaryFacts(cards, { domain, at, host }) -> facts   // §5.32 domainSummary: per card { pending, failed, exists, … } and the failed lookup by name
                                                                             // (dns.nsFailed, mail.mxFailed / spfFailed / dmarcFailed, web.httpsFailed: host names, certs.caaFailed / ctFailed);
                                                                             // certs: caa (the state), cas (issue), wildcard (issuewild exists) with wildCas, criticalTags
```
- **The health run:** `domainHealth(domain, { rdap: false })` on the build's `passportDns` client, so it asks no question twice; the card applies the build's RDAP result (`health.applyRdap`) and so shows exactly Domain Health's score. After a Retry of one of `HEALTH_LOOKUPS` the view runs it again: the retried answers are in the resolver cache (`noCache` stores them), and so is the rest. A Retry pressed while the build still runs (a SERVFAIL lands in milliseconds, RDAP takes seconds) runs on its own controller; if the build's health run has not landed yet it read the failed answer too, so it runs again once it lands.
- **CT:** one Cert Spotter request (the single-host allowance: `expand=issuer&expand=issuer.caa_domains`, its first page of current issuances); crt.sh's identity search only when Cert Spotter fails or is cooling down after a 429 (`ctcert.noteCertspotterLimit`, the page session's cool-down). The view asks only on a click.
- **Tables:** checked against live answers on 2026-09-28 (NS of the hosting companies, MX of the mail providers, every SPF include publishing SPF, TXT tokens of large domains); the TXT prefixes were cross-checked with nuclei-templates `dns/txt-service-detect.yaml` (MIT). Tests: `tests/js/passport.test.js` (fake DoH + fake fetch), `tests/e2e/domain.e2e.mjs` (offline).

### 5.47 `lib/pkcs12.js`, `lib/ciphers.js`, `lib/sha.js` — PKCS#12 (.pfx / .p12) in the browser
IIS, Java keystores and many CAs hand certificates over as a password-protected PKCS#12 bundle (RFC 7292). `openPkcs12` reads one locally — pure, DOM-free, WebCrypto from `globalThis.crypto.subtle` unless passed — for `x509.loadCertificates` (§5.2); the views never call it directly.
```js
export const PKCS12_ERRORS = ['NOT_PKCS12', 'BAD_PASSWORD', 'DAMAGED', 'UNSUPPORTED']
export class Pkcs12Error extends Error { code; detail; encryption }   // detail: 'mac'|'no-mac' for BAD_PASSWORD, what for UNSUPPORTED ('pbeWithSHAAnd128BitRC4', 'envelopedData', 'signedData', 'SHA-224', 'iterations', an OID; 'webcrypto': no WebCrypto at all; 'webcrypto-refused: <operation>': WebCrypto refused HMAC-SHA-256, PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256, a SHA-384 digest, …); encryption: the EncryptionInfo an 'iterations' count belongs to
export async function openPkcs12(bytes, password /* string, '' for none */, { checkKey = false, subtle } = {}) -> {
  certificates: Array<{ der: Uint8Array /* a copy */, friendlyName, localKeyId /* hex */ }>,   // certBag x509Certificate, file order
  keys: Array<{ encrypted, encryption: EncryptionInfo|null, unsupported: string|null /* why it cannot be decrypted here: a name / OID (encryption null) or 'iterations' */, friendlyName, localKeyId,
    check: null | { status: 'checked'|'unsupported'|'unsupported-encryption'|'failed', algorithm, certificates: number[] /* indexes it belongs to */ } }>,   // never the key
  mac: { kind: 'hmac'|'pbmac1', hash, iterations, kdf } | null, encryption: EncryptionInfo[], passwordVerified: boolean,
  passwordEncoding: 'bmp'|'none'|'utf8-bytes'|null, ignored: { crl, secret, other }
}   // throws Pkcs12Error only (a TypeError for a non-bytes input or a non-string password); never writes to the input
EncryptionInfo = { scheme: 'PBES2'|'PKCS#12', cipher: 'AES-256-CBC'|'3DES-CBC'|'2-key 3DES-CBC'|'RC2-128-CBC'|'RC2-40-CBC'|'DES-CBC'|…, kdf: 'PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256'|'PKCS#12 KDF (SHA-1)'|…, iterations, strength: 'ok'|'legacy'|'weak' }
export async function pkcs12Kdf({ hash = 'SHA-1', password /* BMPString */, salt, id /* 1 key, 2 IV, 3 MAC */, iterations, length, subtle }) -> Uint8Array   // RFC 7292 B.2
export function bmpPassword(password) -> Uint8Array          // UTF-16BE code units (surrogate pairs outside the BMP) + 00 00
export function passwordForms(password) -> Array<{ bmp, utf8, encoding }>   // the readings tried, in order
```
- **Structure:** PFX → AuthenticatedSafe → SafeContents → SafeBags with a BER reader (indefinite lengths, constructed OCTET STRINGs as Windows writes them; depth, bag count and lengths bounded). certBag certificates are returned; keyBag / pkcs8ShroudedKeyBag are counted and described; safeContentsBag nests; CRL, secret and other bags are counted. Public-key integrity (signedData) and privacy (envelopedData) modes are UNSUPPORTED. A file that starts like a PFX (SEQUENCE, INTEGER 3, SEQUENCE) and does not parse is DAMAGED (cut short), anything else NOT_PKCS12.
- **Integrity:** the RFC 7292 HMAC (key from the PKCS#12 KDF, id 3, over SHA-1 / 256 / 384 / 512 — SHA-224 and the SHA-512/t variants are UNSUPPORTED) or PBMAC1 (RFC 9579: PBKDF2 with the UTF-8 password, then HMAC). No reading of the password matching the MAC → BAD_PASSWORD 'mac'. With the MAC right, contents that do not decrypt or parse → DAMAGED. Without a MAC every reading is tried; the one that decrypts to SafeContents that parse wins, none → BAD_PASSWORD 'no-mac' (a damaged file looks the same). A bundle with neither MAC nor encrypted certificates opens with any password (`passwordVerified: false`).
- **Encryption:** PBES2 with PBKDF2 (HMAC-SHA-1 default, 256, 384, 512; the key length must fit the cipher) and AES-128 / 192 / 256-CBC through WebCrypto — lib/ciphers.js when WebCrypto refuses the key (Chromium has no AES-192) — or DES / 3DES / RC2-CBC (RC2 version → effective bits: 160 → 40, 120 → 64, 58 → 128); the PKCS#12 PBE (RFC 7292 C: key id 1, IV id 2, SHA-1): pbeWithSHAAnd3-KeyTripleDES-CBC, 2-KeyTripleDES, 128BitRC2, 40BitRC2. RC4 and PBES1 are UNSUPPORTED by name, before any password is used. `strength`: weak = a 40- or 56-bit key (RC2-40, DES), legacy = 3DES, RC2-128, the SHA-1 PKCS#12 KDF.
- **Passwords:** the PKCS#12 KDF takes the BMPString, PBKDF2 the UTF-8 bytes (as OpenSSL). The empty password is also tried as no bytes (writers that leave the terminator out), a non-ASCII one also as OpenSSL 1.0.x encoded it (each UTF-8 byte one character), like `openssl pkcs12` retries. Iteration counts above 10,000,000 (PBKDF2, native), 1,000,000 (the KDF in JS, which yields to the event loop every 20,000 rounds) or 200,000 (a SHA-384 / SHA-512 MAC key: one WebCrypto digest a round) are UNSUPPORTED 'iterations' — refused at once, never a hung tab.
- **Keys:** nothing decrypts a key without `checkKey`. With it, each key is decrypted (shrouded) or read (plain keyBag), imported with `importKey('pkcs8', …, extractable false)` (RSA as RSASSA-PKCS1-v1_5, EC P-256 / P-384 / P-521 as ECDSA), signs a fixed challenge, and every certificate whose SPKI has the same algorithm (and curve) verifies it; a key type WebCrypto cannot import (RSA-PSS, DSA, Ed25519, …, or the browser refuses it) is 'unsupported', never a mismatch. A shrouded key under an encryption this page cannot undo (RC4, PBES1, too many iterations) keeps its `unsupported` reason and is 'unsupported-encryption'; the certificates still open. The password bytes, the KDF's working buffers (and lib/sha.js's padded copy and message schedule), derived keys, key schedules and decrypted buffers are zeroed once used — best effort: the password string itself cannot be cleared and the engine may keep copies (WebCrypto's buffers, uncollected garbage); no message carries the password.
- `lib/ciphers.js`: `desEde3CbcDecrypt(key /* 24 = 3DES, 16 = two-key, 8 = DES */, iv, data, { padding = true })`, `rc2CbcDecrypt(key, effectiveBits, iv, data, …)`, `aesCbcDecrypt(key /* 16, 24, 32 */, iv, data, …)` → Uint8Array; `CipherError { code: 'length'|'padding'|'key' }` for partial blocks, bad PKCS#5 / #7 padding (the output is zeroed) or impossible keys; inputs are never written to, key schedules are zeroed after each call. Table-driven (DES: the S-boxes and P merged into one lookup per box on 32-bit halves, IP / FP by byte tables; AES: InvMixColumns by product tables): about 150 ms for 5 MB of 3DES, RC2 or AES. `cbcDecryptInSlices(decrypt, blockSize, iv, data, { padding = true, sliceBytes = SLICE_BYTES /* 256 KB */ })` → Promise: the same result a slice at a time, yielding (setTimeout) between slices — lib/pkcs12.js decrypts every JS cipher this way, so a large legacy bundle never freezes the page. Tests: FIPS 46-3, SP 800-17, SP 800-67, RFC 2268, FIPS 197 and SP 800-38A known answers plus node:crypto as an oracle, 64 KB of 3DES under 500 ms, slice boundaries, errors and the yield (`tests/js/ciphers.test.js`).
- `lib/sha.js`: `sha1(bytes)`, `sha256(bytes)` → Uint8Array, synchronous — x509's fingerprints without WebCrypto (plain http) and the PKCS#12 KDF, which hashes its own output thousands of times (SHA-384 / 512 go through WebCrypto there).
- Tests (`tests/js/pkcs12.test.js`): the KDF against OpenSSL's published vectors and `openssl kdf` cases, every fixture against `p12_expected.json`, wrong passwords (MAC / no MAC), the old-OpenSSL and empty-password readings, BER as Windows writes it, a refused AES-192, missing WebCrypto and a refused WebCrypto operation, crafted damaged / unsupported bundles (MAC computed in the test), a key under RC4 or too many iterations, a 700 KB 3DES bundle that never holds the event loop for long, random corruption (only Pkcs12Error escapes), the input left untouched and no key bytes in the result; `loadCertificates` warnings, key-check verdicts and the leaf paired by localKeyId.

### 5.48 `lib/estate.js` — the certificate estate of the CLI's reports
DOM-free (imports `domain.js` certCovers, `netinfo.js` normalizeIP, `export.js` toCsv). Nothing is sent: the Certificate estate view reads the report files in the browser and keeps them in the tab.
```js
export function readEstateReport(text, { name }) -> { ok: true, report: EstateReport } | { ok: false, error: REPORT_ERRORS, detail? }   // never throws: 'too-large' (> ESTATE_MAX_BYTES = 64 MiB),
                                              // 'not-json', 'not-report' (no "tool": "ssl_origin_scan"), 'version' (another major; detail: it), 'no-results'
EstateReport = { name, doc, startedAt: Date|null, finishedAt: Date|null, estate /* options.estate */, keyHashes /* every certificates{} entry has spkiSha256 */, id /* the same scan read twice */ }
export function mergeReports(reports) -> { doc, origin: Map<'ip|port', index>, sources: Map<'ip|port|sha256', index[]>, overlaps: string[] }
  // one report: as it is; several: names asked = the union, certificates{} = the union; on an ip:port several reports scanned, each probe (no SNI, or one name)
  // takes the newest report's answer (finishedAt; a tie → the later one), so a name only an older report asked keeps its answer, and a port the newest report found
  // closed or silent drops the older answers there; `origin` = the newest report of each endpoint, `sources` = the reports behind a certificate on an endpoint
export function estateOf(doc, { now /* default: doc.finishedAt */ }) -> Estate   // the CLI's estate_from_report, field for field (tests/fixtures/estate)
export function expiryBucket(daysLeft) / weakReasons(keyAlgorithm, keyBits, signatureAlgorithm) / keyLabel(keyAlgorithm, keyBits, curve)   // the CLI's rules
export function estateMatches(cert, filter /* ESTATE_FILTERS */) / estateFilterCounts(estate)   // attention: any flag, or expired / < 30 days; private: self-signed or private-ca
export function estateCsvRows(estate, { certificates, reportName }) / estateCsv(estate, opts)   // the CLI's --estate --csv rows (ESTATE_CSV_COLUMNS), plus `report` with several reports
export const ESTATE_BUCKETS = ['expired', '7d', '30d', '90d', 'later'], ESTATE_KINDS = ['origin-ca', 'self-signed', 'private-ca', 'other'],
  ESTATE_WEAK_REASONS = ['rsa-short', 'sha1', 'md5'], ESTATE_FLAGS = ['name-conflict', 'stale', 'shared-key', 'weak', 'covers-none'], WEAK_RSA_BITS = 2048,
  SHARED_KEY_MIN_HOSTS = 2, SHARED_KEY_WIDE_HOSTS = 5, ESTATE_FILTERS, REPORT_ERRORS, ESTATE_MAX_REPORTS = 20, ESTATE_CSV_COLUMNS
export function sharedKeyNeedsLook(group) -> boolean   // the CLI's shared_key_needs_look: 2+ certificates, or hosts >= SHARED_KEY_WIDE_HOSTS
Estate = { namesAsked, counts: { certificates, endpoints, openEndpoints, endpointsWithCertificate, expiry: {bucket: n}, kinds: {kind: n} },
  certificates: [{ sha256, subjectCN, subjectDN, issuer, issuerDN, serialHex, notBefore, notAfter, daysLeft, expiry, hostnames, keyAlgorithm, keyBits, curve, key,
    signatureAlgorithm, spkiSha256|null, kind, privateCa, isCA, weak, coversAsked, flags, endpoints: [{ servers, ip, port, defaultCert, names }] }],   // soonest expiry first
  nameConflicts: [{ name, certificates: [{ sha256, stale, endpoints: [{ servers, ip, port }] }] }],   // newest first
  sharedKeys: [{ spkiSha256, key, hosts, servers, addresses, certificates }], weakKeys: [{ sha256, reasons }], coversNone: sha256[] | null }
```
- Every handshake that returned a certificate counts (with SNI or without, covering the asked name or not: a fallback certificate is served too). `names` of an endpoint: the names asked that got this certificate there.
- A **name conflict**: a name asked that two or more endpoints serve with different certificates, each covering it (the SNI rows; coverage by `certCovers` on the report's `hostnames`). `stale`: another certificate of the conflict with the same key algorithm and kind family (public / Origin CA / private) was issued later (notBefore, then notAfter): the endpoints serving it were left behind; an RSA + ECDSA pair or an Origin CA certificate next to a public one has no stale side.
- A **shared key**: one SPKI SHA-256 served by `SHARED_KEY_MIN_HOSTS` or more hosts or carried by two or more certificates; sorted by hosts, then certificates. `hosts` counts distinct addresses (several inventory names of one address, or its ports, are one host); `servers` lists the inventory names (case-folded; the address when a row has none), `addresses` the addresses. Every shared key is listed, but only one that `sharedKeyNeedsLook` (in two or more certificates — a renewal that kept the key, one key for several sites — or on `SHARED_KEY_WIDE_HOSTS` or more addresses) flags its certificates `shared-key`: one certificate on the members of a load-balancer pool is the usual case. A report of an older CLI has no `spkiSha256`: no key is shared there (the view says key reuse was not checked).
- **Weak**: RSA under 2048 bits, a SHA-1 signature, an MD5 / MD2 one. **Covering none of the names asked**: `null` when no name was asked (coverage cannot be told).
- Kind: the report's `certificates{}.kind` (`--private-ca` applied by the CLI); a report without it: `self-signed` from `selfSigned`, else `other`. Days left count from `now`.
- Tests: `tests/js/estate.test.js` (the fixture reports' own estate sections, a week later, an older report, no names, merging both orders, a closed port, junk, filters, CSV and formula-safe cells), `tests/js/estate-view.test.js`.

### 5.49 `lib/chainfix.js` — the missing intermediate, and what the root faces in the root stores
The most common installation error is a server certificate installed without its intermediate: browsers often hide it, Android, curl, Java and API clients fail. The page cannot fetch the certificate's AIA caIssuers URL (plain http, no CORS; §3), so the site ships the CCADB list of public TLS intermediates (`assets/data/intermediates/`, built by `tools/build-intermediates.mjs`, rebuilt weekly by `.github/workflows/intermediates.yml`) and completes the chain from it. DOM-free; the network is `fetchImpl` (tests read the files from disk).
```js
export const DATASET_URL            // new URL('../../data/intermediates/manifest.json', import.meta.url): moves under v/<commit>/ with assets/
export const DATASET_FORMAT = 1
export const STORES = ['chrome', 'mozilla', 'apple', 'microsoft']        // display order
export const STORE_STATUSES = ['tls', 'other', 'not-before', 'removed', 'absent']   // a root's status in one store (roots.json)
export const LIFECYCLE_CODES = ['distrusted', 'renewal-distrusted', 'removed', 'not-for-tls', 'cut-off', 'not-included', 'root-expired', 'root-expires']
export const MAX_ADDED = 4                  // intermediates added to one chain at most
export function dnHash(dn) -> string        // the first 16 hex digits of SHA-256 over the DN as lib/x509.js writes it (the dn/ index key)
export function shardOf(key, digits) -> string|null   // a hex key's shard file name; null for anything but hex
export function datasetDate(value) -> Date|null       // 'YYYY-MM-DD' = the end of that day (UTC): "issued after 2024-11-11" is after 23:59:59.999
export function rootTable(json) -> { generated, roots: RootInfo[], bySki: Map, byDn: Map }
RootInfo = { sha256, name, owner, ski, dn, notAfter: Date|null, stores: { chrome|mozilla|apple|microsoft: STORE_STATUSES }, events: LifecycleEvent[] }
LifecycleEvent = { type: 'distrust-after'|'expiry', store: string|null, date: Date, basis: 'sct'|'notBefore'|null, url: string|null /* https only */, source: 'announcement'|'ccadb'|null /* what url is */ }
export function rootsIssuing(table, cert) -> RootInfo[]   // by the authority key id (the DN must agree where known) or, for a root without a key id (ski null: TWCA Global Root CA), by the issuer DN; by the issuer DN alone without an authority key id
export function rootsWithKey(table, cert) -> RootInfo[]   // the roots `cert` is a copy of (same key id and DN; the DN alone for a root or a certificate without a key id): a cross-signed root
export function rootEntryFor(table, rootCert) -> RootInfo|null   // a self-signed certificate of the file: by fingerprint, else key id + DN
export function issuedAt(cert, basis) -> Date               // 'sct': the earliest embedded SCT (Chrome's cut-offs), else notBefore
export function chainStanding(anchors, leaf, now) -> { stores: { [store]: { status, root } }, trusted: string[], warnings: LifecycleWarning[] } | null
export function rootStanding(root, leaf, now)               // chainStanding([root], …)
LifecycleWarning = { code: LIFECYCLE_CODES, severity: 'error'|'warn', stores: string[], root: RootInfo, date: Date|null, issued: Date|null, url: string|null, source: 'announcement'|'ccadb'|null }
export function createIntermediateStore({ url = DATASET_URL, fetchImpl, timeoutMs = 15000 }) -> { manifest(), roots(), bySki(ski), byDn(dn) }
  // each file fetched once (a failed fetch is forgotten: the next call tries again); bySki/byDn → [{ cert /* parsed */, owner /* CCADB CA owner */ }], a damaged entry skipped
export function fileChain(certs, leaf) -> { chain: Certificate[] /* leaf first, no root */, rootCert: Certificate|null }
export async function repairChain({ certificates, leaf }, { store, now, signal }) -> ChainRepair   // rejects when the dataset cannot be read
ChainRepair = {
  status: 'complete'|'repaired'|'not-found'|'self-signed'|'no-leaf', reason: 'missing'|'untrusted-root'|null,
  leaf, chain, rootCert, added: [{ cert, owner }], fullchain /* chain + added, never the root */, root: RootInfo|null, ownRoot /* where the file's own chain ends */,
  anchors: RootInfo[] /* the roots the chain can end at, nearest first */, standing, missing: { issuerDN, authorityKeyId }|null,
  alternatives /* ways to a root the dataset offered */, generated /* the dataset's date */
}
```
- **Dataset** (`assets/data/intermediates/`, format 1): `manifest.json` (date — kept when a rebuild changes nothing —, a digest of the data files and of the manifest without its date and digest, sources, licence, shard layout, counts, the lifecycle window; the skipped counts go to the build log only, so a change to them alone rewrites nothing), `ski/<xx>.json` × 256 (subject key id → `[{ owner, der /* base64 */ }]`, by its first two hex digits; ≈ 16 KB each), `dn/<x>.json` × 16 (dnHash → subject key ids), `roots.json` (`roots` and the lifecycle table `events`). Candidates: Mozilla's intermediate report, plus the certificate records' intermediates that are TLS capable, `Trusted` in at least one store, `Not Revoked`, unexpired and not in Mozilla's report (`extraIntermediateRecords`), their PEM from the PEM report of each notBefore year they need (`pemYears`), the fingerprint checked, de-duplicated by fingerprint, the owner from the record. Kept: CA certificates with a subject key id, valid on the build date, whose EKU allows TLS server authentication (none, serverAuth, anyExtendedKeyUsage) — ≈ 2,270 (≈ 275 of them from the records). Roots: every root CCADB marks TLS capable, the ones Mozilla includes for websites, the issuers of the listed intermediates and the roots of the lifecycle input; their status per store from CCADB (Mozilla: 'tls' only with the Websites trust bit; Apple / Microsoft: 'tls' when CCADB marks the root TLS capable; Microsoft NotBefore = 'not-before'; Removed / Blocked / Disabled = 'removed'); names shared by several roots get the DN's first OU (`GlobalSign (GlobalSign Root CA - R3)`). Lifecycle table: `tools/root-lifecycle.json` (hand-kept, each entry with its announcement: Chrome's Entrust / AffirmTrust cut-off, SCTs after 2024-11-11; Chrome's Chunghwa Telecom / Netlock, after 2025-07-31; Apple's Entrust, after 2024-11-15; Mozilla's Entrust announcement), Mozilla's `Distrust for TLS After Date` from CCADB (which wins over a hand-kept Mozilla date), and an `expiry` event for every root a store trusts that expires from January 1 of last year to December 31 of next year. A Mozilla date from CCADB carries `source: 'ccadb'` and the report's URL (the page labels that link "Source", a hand-kept announcement "Announcement"). The builder refuses implausible data (fewer than 500 intermediates or 100 roots) or a missing canary — a well-known current issuer (`CANARIES`: YE1, YR1, R12, Microsoft TLS G2 RSA CA OCSP 02, WR1, Amazon RSA 2048 M02, Sectigo DV R36, each until its own expiry) — and writes nothing.
- **Repair:** the file's chain from the leaf (x509 `issuedBy`); a self-signed issuer in the file is the root it holds (`complete`, never in fullchain). Otherwise the roots the file's last certificate names: `complete` when a store trusts this certificate through them (a cross-signed root inside the file counts as an anchor, so leaf + WE2 + the GlobalSign-signed GTS Root R4 is trusted through GTS Root R4); else the issuers are looked up — by the authority key id, or by the issuer DN when the certificate names none (a DN match under another key id would not be its issuer; only a root without a key id of its own, whose certificate has no subject key id extension, is matched by the DN under an authority key id) — valid now, never twice, up to MAX_ADDED, a root trusted by fewer than every store also looked past (its cross-signed copy may reach one more). Paths are ranked by the number of stores that trust the certificate through them, then reaching a known root, fewer warnings, fewer added certificates, the later expiry: so a cross-signed copy under a removed root loses to the one under the current root, and a file that ends at a root no store trusts is completed through a cross-signed copy of it (`reason: 'untrusted-root'`). Nothing found: `not-found` with the issuer looked for. Only the manifest, the roots table and the shards the key ids name are read.
- **Standing:** per store, the first anchor it trusts for this certificate ('distrusted' when a distrust-after event of that store is past the certificate's issue time, 'expired' when the root is); a store that trusts none is judged by the first anchor it knows. Warnings: distrusted (error; with the announcement), renewal-distrusted (warn: issued before the cut-off, the renewal is affected), removed / not-for-tls (error), cut-off (warn, Microsoft's NotBefore), not-included (warn, only while another store trusts the chain), root-expired (error), root-expires (warn: the table's expiry of a root a store trusts the chain through falls before the certificate's own).
- **UI** (`ui/chain-repair.js`, §6): one job per loaded file (a WeakMap on its parse result; a failed one starts again on the next call), `ChainRepairNotes(load, { lifecycle, onDownload })` and `ChainRepairChainPart(load)`, `onChainRepairEnd(load, fn)` (once, when a running job ends); pure helpers `repairApplies`, `repairText` (a Certificate Transparency load is titled "Intermediate found in the CCADB list", never "missing"), `notFoundText` (an expired certificate: its issuer may have expired or been withdrawn with it), `lifecycleText` (the store names as one or several stores), `lifecycleLinkLabel`, `storeList`, `trustText`, `repairedFullchain`.
- Tests: `tests/js/chainfix.test.js` (the fixture PKI over the test dataset: a leaf alone, a complete chain, the root in the file, the cross-signed alternatives, two levels, no authority key id, a root without a key id, not in the list, the lifecycle, an expired intermediate, scoring by stores, MAX_ADDED and a loop, a failed download retried, an unknown format, abort, a damaged entry), `tests/js/build-intermediates.test.js` (CSV, statuses, filtering, the records-only intermediates and their PEM, canaries, the lifecycle table, determinism and the manifest digest; every shard, index and root of both checked-in datasets; the site's dataset repairs a Let's Encrypt YE leaf through the Root YE cross-sign up to ISRG Root X2, and a TWCA Global EVSSL leaf up to TWCA Global Root CA, which has no key id, with or without the root's cross-signature), `tests/js/chain-repair.test.js` (the job and the sentences, EN + TR), `tests/e2e/chainfix.e2e.mjs` (offline).

### 5.50 `lib/fixes.js` — DNS change requests and fix-as-code
DOM-free, no I/O of its own (`readCurrent` / `countSpfLookups` take a DohClient). Loaded by the DNS change request view and, on the first "Show the fix", by Domain Health and Zone File (through `ui/fix-panel.js`: it brings the zone parser and linter).
```js
export const FIX_TYPES = ['A', 'AAAA', 'CNAME', 'TXT', 'MX', 'CAA'], FIX_MODES = ['is', 'has', 'none'], FIX_ACTIONS = ['add', 'replace', 'delete', 'ttl', 'rewrite', 'unchanged'],
  FIX_FORMATS = ['bind', 'route53', 'cloudflare', 'octodns', 'terraform-cloudflare', 'terraform-route53'], FIX_FORMAT_EXT, FIX_LANGS = ['en', 'tr'],
  FIX_LIMITS = { rrsets: 20, values: 40, valueBytes: 4000, ttlMin: 1, ttlMax: 2^31-1 }, DEFAULT_TTL = 3600, CLOUDFLARE_VARS = { token: 'CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN', zone: 'CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID' },
  TERRAFORM_VARS, CAA_FORM_METHODS, TXT_FAMILIES /* spf1, dmarc1, tlsrptv1, stsv1, dkim1, bimi1 */, FIX_CAS /* health CAA_ISSUERS minus distrusted, + ZeroSSL → sectigo.com */,
  FIX_FIELDS, CHANGE_TEMPLATES, TEMPLATE_IDS, CHANGE_LINT_CODES, HEALTH_FIX_IDS, LINT_FIX_CODES, ACME_TOKEN_RE, FIX_I18N /* fix.* EN + TR */
RRsetChange = { name, type, ttl, mode: 'is'|'has'|'none', family: string|null, values: Data[], before: Data[]|null, others: Data[]|null, beforeTtl, maxTtl, rewrite }
  // values in dnswire RR.data shape; `family` scopes a TXT set to one kind of record (the v=spf1 record, the v=DMARC1 record …): the other TXT
  // values at the name (`others`) stay; before / others null = not read. beforeTtl comes from a zone file only (a resolver's TTL counts down).
ChangeRequest = { v: 1, template, zone, rrsets, notes: FixText[], problems: FixProblem[], reads: { name, type }[] }
export function rrset(r), rrsetPlan(r) -> { add, remove /* null: not known */, keep, after, full /* the whole set afterwards, null when not known */, ttlOnly, rewrite, unchanged, complete },
  rrsetAction(r, plan) -> FIX_ACTIONS, changeRequest(spec), hasErrors(req), readPlan(req), readCurrent(queries, { dns, signal }) -> { 'name|TYPE': { status, values, ttl, cname } },
  currentEntry(res, name, type), applyCurrent(req, current), afterZoneText(req, current), validateChange(req, { current, spf }) -> FixProblem[] /* errors first */,
  countSpfLookups(req, { dns, signal }), renderFix(req, format) -> string, formatNotes(req, format) -> FixText[], changeInstructions(req, { lang, checkUrl }) -> string,
  editSpf(record, { add, remove, all }), mergeSpf(records), editDmarc(record, set), buildChange(templateId, input, { current }) -> ChangeRequest, templateInput(id, input),
  changeTemplate(id), unreadEdits(req) -> RRsetChange[], healthFix(check, report) -> Fix|null, currentFromReport(report), caaFixFromIssuers(fix, issuers), lintFix(finding, zone) -> Fix|null,
  absoluteName(input, zone), relativeName(name, zone), zoneName(input), txtChunks(text), txtText(data), normalizeValue(type, data), valueKey(type, data),
  valueText(type, data), parseValueText(type, text, zone), txtFamily(data), familyOf(type, values), mailtoUri(input), m365MxHost(domain), textIn(lang, key, params)
Fix = { id, kind: 'records'|'advice', request: ChangeRequest|null, template, input /* the builder form that makes the same change */, advice: FixText[], needsCt }
```
- **Templates** (`CHANGE_TEMPLATES`, form fields in `FIX_FIELDS`): `acme-txt` (the `_acme-challenge` TXT values, mode `has`; a value that is not 43 base64url characters is a warning; a `_acme-challenge` CNAME found by a read is an error: the TXT belongs at its target), `acme-cname` (the delegation; both take the record name an ACME client prints, `_acme-challenge.example.com`, for the certificate name it validates — the label is never doubled — with an info `fix.p.acme-name`), `m365` (MX `0 <domain-with-dashes>.mail.protection.outlook.com` unless given, the SPF include, Autodiscover, the selector1 / selector2 DKIM CNAMEs of a tenant, a p=none DMARC record when none is there — not read: `fix.n.dmarc-first-unread`, a record there stays), `google` (MX `1 smtp.google.com`, the SPF include, the DKIM TXT when pasted, the first DMARC record), `caa` (one issue value per CA, `accounturi` with exactly one CA, `validationmethods`, issuewild as issue / none / unset, iodef), `spf` (an include added before `all` / `redirect=` or removed, the `all` qualifier; a removal needs a read — without one it is an error, `fix.p.spf-remove-read`, and writes nothing —, an addition without one says what it cannot know, `fix.n.spf-unread`, and `fix.n.spf-new` only when a read found no SPF record), `dmarc` (the policy step-up keeping the other tags, `v` first and `p` second; pct empty keeps the record's own, 100 removes it; not read: `fix.n.dmarc-unread` names the tags the value leaves out and `fix.n.dmarc-to` names no policy it comes from), `ttl` (the listed sets with their read values and a lower TTL; `maxTtl` makes the check wait for resolvers' copies to expire within it), `record` (one A / AAAA / CNAME / TXT / MX / CAA set: set, add or delete; an SPF / DMARC value scopes the set to its family), `parked` (null MX, `v=spf1 -all`, `v=DMARC1; p=reject` — after a read, the DMARC record there with p=reject and its report addresses kept —, CAA `0 issue ";"` unless left out, optionally `*._domainkey` `v=DKIM1; p=`; a read that finds MX records, or an SPF record that lets servers send, is a warning: `fix.p.parked-mail`, `fix.p.parked-sends`). The templates that edit what is there (`needsCurrent`: m365, google, spf, dmarc, ttl) build from a read; without one the outputs say what they cannot know: a TXT family set such a template edits unread (`unreadEdits`: its note — `fix.n.spf-unread`, `fix.n.dmarc-unread`, `fix.n.dmarc-first-unread` — names it) is "Add or change" in the instructions (`fix.ins.action.set`, `fix.ins.editFamily`: add the value if the name has no such record, else follow the note) and in the set list, and every format carries `fix.fn.unread-edit` (it would replace a record there). What a template looks at in the current state is in `reads` too, so a read asks exactly that.
- **Validation**: the records afterwards, plus what a read found at the same names for other types, go through `lib/zoneparse.js` + `lib/zonelint.js` (as a partial zone); `CHANGE_LINT_CODES` of the change's names count (a CNAME next to other data, private addresses, SPF / DMARC syntax, CAA tags and flags …). Then the SPF budget (the record's own lookup terms; with `countSpfLookups`, the recursive count with the includes as published now — of the record it counted: `countSpfLookups` returns `{ record, count, exceeded, error }` per name, and a set the form changed since gets an info "read again" (`fix.p.spf-recount`) instead of the old count), the RFC 8659 / 8657 grammar of each CAA issue value (lib/health.js parseCaaIssueValue) and TTLs under 60 s or over a day.
- **Formats**: BIND (the changed sets afterwards relative to `$ORIGIN`, removals as `; delete:` comments; an unchanged set left out); a Route 53 change batch (UPSERT of the full set — the family's other TXT values included when read —, DELETE of a set with its values, `CURRENT_VALUE` when not read; TXT and CAA strings in Route 53's octal escape form); the Cloudflare API as a POSIX shell script (the token and zone ID read from `$CLOUDFLARE_API_TOKEN` / `$CLOUDFLARE_ZONE_ID`, never written; a record name in a list call percent-encoded, `!` too, and the zone's name in the `${VAR:?…}` message only as a plain host name, so no name from a zone file — `` ` `` `$` `"` `\` — reaches the shell; request bodies single-quoted ASCII JSON; one value for another is a PATCH in place so two SPF records never coexist; a deletion or TTL change lists the records first and uses `RECORD_ID`); octoDNS YAML (the names and every mapping in octoDNS's natural key order — `ttl`, `type`, `values` —, `''` for the apex, a TXT value as its raw text with `;` escaped — and a header line saying to load the entries with `escaped_semicolons: true` when one has a `;` —, in one more pair of quotes when it starts with `"` (octoDNS strips them as it loads it), `octodns: lenient: true` where octoDNS's own check refuses the text — outside ASCII, a `\` before a `;`; a deletion says which record goes from the name's list, never the name's whole entry); Terraform `cloudflare_record` (provider v4, one resource per value) and `aws_route53_record` (one per set, TXT over 255 bytes split with `\"\"`). `formatNotes` says what a format needs (a set not read in a whole-set format, a Route 53 DELETE that must match exactly, Cloudflare's TTL range, record ids). The Route 53 string escapes, the YAML quoting, the octoDNS TXT escapes and check and its key order are lib/zonetext.js's (`route53String`, `yamlString`, `octodnsTxt`, `octodnsTxtRefused`, `naturalCompare`, §5.68), which lib/zoneconvert.js writes whole zones with: the change request, its check page and "Show the fix" never load the zone converter.
- **Instructions** (`changeInstructions`): one numbered step per set — Add / Replace / Delete / Change the TTL / Rewrite, the name, the values, the TTL, what it removes or keeps — the template's notes and the check link, in English or Turkish whatever the UI language.
- **Fixes**: `HEALTH_FIX_IDS` — dmarc.missing (p=none with a report address to create), dmarc.policy-none (→ quarantine), dmarc.pct, dmarc.sp-none, dmarc.rua-missing, dmarc.multiple (advice), spf.missing (`-all` without MX, the platform include for Microsoft 365 / Google Workspace MX hosts, else advice only), spf.null-mx, spf.all-pass / all-neutral / all-missing (`~all`), spf.ptr, spf.after-all (both keep `exp=` and unknown modifiers; only a `redirect=` an `all` voids goes), spf.redirect-ignored, spf.multiple (one merged record, `mergeSpf`: the first `exp=` and every other modifier once), spf.lookups-exceeded / lookups-high (flattening advice naming the costliest includes, never a record), mx.none (the mail part of the parked lock-down; advice only when the domain's SPF record lets servers send: a send-only domain has no MX either), mx.null-mixed (advice), caa.missing (the CAs of the current certificates from Certificate Transparency, `needsCt`; `caaFixFromIssuers` keeps what the report read — no CAA set, so an add — and a CA the form does not list sends the edit link to the plain-record template with every value), caa.cert-denied (the current certificate's CA added to the property that decided, the listed CAs kept), caa.critical-unknown (the critical flag cleared on the unknown tags, as CAA_FLAGS), tls-rpt.missing. The DMARC fixes change only what they are about (p, sp, pct and rua otherwise as they are); for a policy inherited from the organizational domain (RFC 7489 §6.6.3) they change that record, at `_dmarc.<org>`, where the policy comes from — dmarc.policy-none then steps up its `sp` — and the advice says so (`fix.a.dmarc-inherited`). The CAA fixes change the set where the climb found it (the domain or a parent, `fix.a.caa-at`). Built over `currentFromReport(report)` (the TXT and MX of the domain, the CAA set at the name it was found, the DMARC record of `_dmarc.<domain>` and an inherited one at the organizational domain), so the whole-set formats are exact; a fix that changes existing records notes that its TTL is a default (`fix.n.report-ttl`: a resolver does not tell the zone's own TTL). `LINT_FIX_CODES` — TXT_STRING_TOO_LONG (the same text re-split, `rewrite`), LOCALHOST_RECORD (delete), CAA_FLAGS (flags 0), TTL_TOO_LOW (TTL 300), MULTIPLE_SPF (merged, modifiers kept): from the file, so exact, TTLs included; they carry no check link (`ui/fix-panel.js` LintFixPanel), since nothing about an imported zone reaches a URL or the current target. The views keep a copy of the id lists (`FIXABLE_CHECKS`, `FIXABLE_LINT`; a unit test keeps them equal) and load the panel on the first click.

Tests: `tests/js/fixes.test.js`, and the goldens of `tests/fixtures/fixes/gen-fixes-golden.mjs` (every template in every format and both languages, with and without a read; `--write` after a deliberate change).

### 5.51 `lib/changecheck.js` — "is it live?"
```js
export const CHECK_VERSION = 1, CHECK_RESOLVERS = ['cloudflare', 'google', 'dnssb', 'cznic'], CHECK_LIMITS = { rrsets: 20, values: 40, chars: 4000 },
  CHECK_VERDICTS = ['done', 'pending', 'wrong', 'error'], PENDING_REASONS = ['missing', 'old', 'other', 'partial', 'ttl', 'present'],
  CHECK_HEADLINES = ['done', 'done-partial', 'wrong', 'pending', 'no-answer', 'unknown'], CHECK_STOPS = ['done', 'timeout', 'cached', 'failed'],
  CHECK_TIMING = { base: 15 s, factor: 1.6, max: 5 min, stopAfter: 2 h, maxTtlWait: 24 h }
ExpectedSet = { name, type, mode, family, values, old: Data[]|null, maxTtl }
export function checkFromRequest(req) -> { zone, sets }, encodeCheck(check) -> { ok, query, length, reason }, decodeCheck(query | URLSearchParams) -> { ok, check } | { ok: false, error, detail },
  linkEncode(value), linkQuery(URLSearchParams) -> string, judgeAnswer(expected, response) -> { verdict, reason, seen, ttl }, pairKey(set, resolver),
  checkRound(check, { dns, resolvers, only, signal, now, onResult, timeoutMs }) -> PairResult[], checkState(check, latest) -> { headline, counts, sets, settled, answered, pairs },
  nextCheck({ latest, check, round, startedAt, now, errorRounds }) -> { stop: 'done'|'timeout'|'cached'|'failed'|null, at, pairs, cachedUntil }
```
- **The link**: `#/change/check?z=<zone>&r=<set>&r=…`, one `r` per set in presentation form: `<mode>[/<maxTtl>] <name relative to the zone> <TYPE> <values separated by |>[^<old values>]` (`has _acme-challenge TXT "…"`, `is/300 www A 192.0.2.10^198.51.100.5`); `|` and `^` inside a value are written `\124` / `\094`; `@ : / , ; =` stay readable (`linkEncode`). A TXT set's family is read from its values (or a deletion's old values); where that would read it wrong the type names it: `TXT:*` (every TXT record, e.g. a deletion where the read found only an SPF record), `TXT:spf1`. Nothing else is in it. `encodeCheck` refuses a set whose name is outside the zone (`outside`: the link writes names relative to it). `decodeCheck` refuses a link over `CHECK_LIMITS` — its length as written: the page passes the hash's own query, and a URLSearchParams counts as `linkQuery` writes it back, never as its own `toString()`, which percent-encodes the readable characters —, another version, a name written absolute (outside the zone), an unknown type or family or a value its type does not parse; `encodeCheck` / `decodeCheck` are exact inverses.
- **Verdicts** of one set on one resolver (what a CA or a mail server sees: through a CNAME chain for any type but CNAME; a family set looks at its own kind only; TXT compared by the joined text): `done` (exactly the values / at least them / none left, and a `maxTtl` set's copy expiring within it); `pending` with `missing` (no record), `old` (still exactly the old values, when there were any: an empty answer where a read found nothing is `missing`), `other` (another value while the old ones are not known), `partial` (a `has` set not complete yet), `ttl` (the new values cached with the old TTL), `present` (a deletion not seen yet); `wrong` (neither the new nor the known old values); `error` (no answer, or an rcode other than NOERROR / NXDOMAIN). `ttl` is what the resolver's copy has left — the negative TTL of the SOA for an empty answer (RFC 2308).
- **Timing** (`nextCheck`): after round n the wait is min(15 s × 1.6ⁿ, 5 min), and a pair is asked again only once its resolver's cached copy can have expired (its TTL after it answered, + 1 s, at most 24 h); a resolver that fails keeps being retried until three rounds in a row had a failure. It stops when every pair is done (errors aside), two hours after the start, when no pair can change before then (`cached`, with the time the last copy expires), or — `failed` — when what was given up leaves a set that no resolver answered (DoH blocked, offline, rate-limited everywhere, a SERVFAIL). `checkState` gives `done`, `done-partial` (done wherever a resolver answered), `wrong`, `pending`, `no-answer` (every pair answered or failed, and a set got no answer at all) or `unknown` (still asking). The check page (views/change.js) words each stop but `done`; Stop (Esc) during a round lets that round's answers show and schedules nothing after it; Check again starts over and asks every set on every resolver (a finished change can be checked again); an error verdict reads as its kind in words (server error, timed out, network error, rate limited, unreadable reply) or its DNS rcode. Offline a round does not run: the page says so, schedules nothing and runs it on the `online` event. While a round runs Check now is disabled and the keyboard focus it had moves to Stop; once the check stops, to Check again. Next to Copy link, **Copy summary** (§5.32 `changeSummary`, facts from `checkSummaryFacts`) copies the headline (`checkHeadline`, the same key and counts the page shows), each set by name and type with its state and how many resolvers see it, and why the check stopped, with the check link as its link; never a value.

Tests: `tests/js/changecheck.test.js` (the goldens' links round-trip, the family named where the values would read it wrong; limits, a near-limit link as URLSearchParams; every verdict; the schedule, a check no resolver answers stopping as `failed`).

### 5.52 `lib/nsparity.js` — the zone compared with the NEW provider's name servers (before the NS switch)
DOM-free. The Zone File's live check compares the file with public DNS, which the current provider still answers; this module asks the new name servers themselves, before the registrar's NS records change. Each (name, type) is one Globalping DNS measurement (§3, `dnsQueryRequest`) with `resolver` = the new server's host name, read back into the DohClient response shape lib/zonedrift.js reads (the answers parsed by lib/zoneparse.js one line at a time, `aa` from the dig flags line, a referral's NS records and glue from the authority / additional sections), so the whole plan and evaluation of §5.23 applies: names and types only, internal-looking names skipped by default, the hidden targets of proxied / flattened / alias records never queried, one query per unique (name, type), `queryTypes: PARITY_QUERY_TYPES` (CAA, TLSA … are `not-queryable`: the CLI's job).
```js
export const PARITY_MODES = ['first', 'all'], PARITY_MAX_PROBES = 100, PARITY_MAX_NAMESERVERS = 8, PARITY_CONCURRENCY = 4,
  PARITY_EXTRA_TYPES = ['A', 'AAAA', 'MX', 'TXT'], PARITY_WWW_TYPES = ['A', 'AAAA'], PARITY_QUERY_TYPES /* GP_DNS_TYPES without ANY */,
  PARITY_STATUSES = ['same', 'different', 'missing', 'unproxied', 'extra', 'skipped', 'error'], PARITY_SEVERITY,
  PARITY_REASONS = ['ttl-differs', 'ns-new', 'ns-mismatch', 'ns-by-address', 'extra-record', 'cname-kept', 'below-cut'] /* + DRIFT_REASONS */,
  NS_STATES = ['ok', 'refused', 'not-authoritative', 'no-zone', 'servfail', 'unreachable', 'failed', 'not-run'], NS_ISSUES, PARITY_STOPS,
  RUNBOOK_STEPS = ['ttl', 'fix', 'dnssec', 'switch', 'wait', 'after'], RUNBOOK_STATES, PARITY_TTL_LOW = 3600, PARITY_KEEP_OLD_HOURS = 48, PARITY_CLI = 'dns_parity.py'
export function parseNameservers(text, { fileNs }) -> { list, cliOnly, issues: [{ code /* NS_ISSUES */, value }] }   // host names (trailing dot dropped, IDN → punycode) or public IPv4 addresses → list (Globalping); private / documentation addresses → cliOnly + a 'private' issue, public IPv6 addresses → cliOnly + an 'ipv6' issue (a probe asks name servers over IPv4: Globalping's DNS ipVersion defaults to 4 and an IPv6 resolver is experimental there) — the CLI command carries them; ≤ 8 in both together; the file's own NS flagged 'in-file'
export function fileNameservers(zone) -> string[]
export function extraQueries(zone, { skipPrivate }) -> [{ name, type }]   // apex: PARITY_EXTRA_TYPES the file lacks; www: A / AAAA when no wildcard covers it; none at a CNAME, A / AAAA never at a proxied name
export function planParity(zone, { nameservers, mode, extras, skipPrivate, wildcardProbes, maxProbes }) -> { ok, why: null|'no-origin'|'no-nameservers'|'over-cap', mode, full, serial, cap,
  needed, probes /* the most the run sends */, capped, rrsets, checked, perServer, extras, driftBudget, skipped, targetsHidden, internalShare }
export function authoritativeFlag(rawOutput) -> bool|null ; export function digSection(rawOutput, 'AUTHORITY'|'ADDITIONAL') -> answers[]
export function dnsResponse(measurement, { name, type, resolver, origin, id }) -> { ok, rcode, aa, answers, authorities, referral, resolver, ede: [], error, errorKind, measurementId, probe, unparsed, failure }
export function nameserverState(soaResponse, origin) -> { state /* NS_STATES */, serial, aa, rcode }
export function createParityDns({ client, nameserver, origin, reserve, release, onMeasurement, onStop, stopped }) -> { query(name, type, { signal }), sent }   // a DohClient over Globalping, memoised
export function parityRow(driftRow, { ns, origin, hosts, autoTtl }) -> ParityRow
export async function runParity(zone, { client, nameservers, cliOnly /* never asked: an address among them makes the apex NS set ns-by-address */, mode = 'first', extras = true, skipPrivate = true, wildcardProbes = true, maxProbes = 100, dns, signal,
  onRow, onProgress, onQuota, onServer, labelFn, now }) -> { origin, mode, startedAt, finishedAt, nameservers: NsResult[], rows: ParityRow[], counts, spent, planned,
  notReached /* record sets a stop kept from a server to compare */, stoppedBy: null|'quota'|'unreachable'|'abort', resetAt, serials: 'same'|'differ'|'unknown', dnssec: { signedInFile, ds: 'present'|'absent'|'unknown'|null }, capped, measurementIds }
export function signedInFile(zone) -> bool ; export function paritySummary(result) -> { verdict: 'ready'|'fix'|'check'|'partial'|'blocked', counts, ttl, unchecked, badServers, compared, stopped /* stoppedBy or notReached > 0 */ }
export function parityRunbook(zone, result|null, { nameservers }) -> [{ id /* RUNBOOK_STEPS */, state /* RUNBOOK_STATES */, params }]
export function parityZoneFile(origin) -> '<zone>.parity.zone' ; export function buildParityCommand({ file, nameservers, shell, script }) -> { command|null, dropped }
```
Rules that are easy to get wrong:
- **Batching:** 'first' mode compares every record set on the first server and asks each other server for its SOA only (a provider's servers serve one copy; the serial shows they serve the same version; `serials: 'differ'` warns). A first server that does not serve the zone costs its SOA probe and the next one is compared instead. 'all' multiplies the cost and is refused past the cap (`over-cap`). Each server's SOA is asked first: REFUSED, no `aa`, no SOA, SERVFAIL or an unreachable name ends that server there.
- **The hard cap** (`PARITY_MAX_PROBES`): a zone that needs more is compared up to the cap in 'first' mode (`capped`; the rest `skipped` / `budget`, and the UI points at the CLI). The budget is reserved before each POST (`reserve` / `release` for a free 400), so parallel queries never overspend; the plan's `probes` is exactly what an uninterrupted run sends.
- **Stops:** a quota 429 (or three network failures in a row) refuses every later POST at no cost, while the measurements already created — paid — are still read; the caller's signal ends the run at once. The rows so far are kept, and `notReached` counts what the stop left out for each server to compare (the plan's record sets less the rows it got; a server stopped before its first row: what it would have compared or left to the CLI). `paritySummary`'s `unchecked` adds it to the record sets left to the CLI and those without an answer, so a stopped run's "not everything was compared: N record sets" is exact ("did not finish" when N is 0). Verdict order: `blocked`, `fix` (missing / different, a server that does not serve the zone), then `partial` for a run that stopped (`stopped`), left a server unasked or got no answer for a record set (an `error` row) — what it never got could hold anything, so unproxied or extra rows so far never make it a clean `check`; cli/dns_parity.py ranks its ERROR rows the same —, then `check`, then `partial` for a finished run that left record sets to the CLI (types, the cap), then `ready`. `blocked` needs every server asked: a stop before a server was asked is `partial`, never "no new name server serves the zone".
- **Statuses:** match / proxied-ok / flattened-ok / alias-ok / routing-ok → `same`; differs → `different`; missing-live → `missing`; origin-exposed → `unproxied` (a proxied record answered with its origin: at the switch it stops being proxied); occluded → `skipped` / `below-cut`. A flattened CNAME the new provider serves as a plain CNAME to the same target is `same` / `cname-kept`. `ttl-stale` is replaced by `ttl-differs` (any difference: an authoritative TTL is exact), never at a proxied name, an automatic TTL or the apex NS. The apex NS set is judged against the host names entered (`ns-new` when the new servers name exactly themselves, `ns-mismatch` otherwise, `ns-by-address` when any server was typed as an address, one only the CLI asks — `cliOnly` — included: the CLI's rule, with one address which names the set should hold is not known). Extra rows (`extra` / `extra-record`) come from `extraQueries`: a provider's parking address or default MX at the apex or www.
- **Runbook:** ttl (the apex NS TTL and the longest record TTL of the file against 3600 s; the TLD's own NS TTL cannot be lowered), fix (todo: missing / different / servers that do not serve it; warn: extra, unproxied, not compared, stopped, capped, serials out of step — after a stop `params.stopped` words it "nothing is missing or different so far, but the comparison did not finish", as the headline does), dnssec (todo when the file carries DNSKEY / RRSIG / NSEC* / CDS / CDNSKEY or the DS lookup finds a DS: remove the DS and wait for its TTL, or pre-publish the new provider's DNSKEY and DS when both support multi-signer, RFC 8901), switch (blocked while there is something to fix; warn — "compare again, or run the CLI, before you switch", with the count of record sets not compared — after a stop or when record sets were left out; todo otherwise), wait (48 h: the old zone keeps answering the cached delegation), after (check from outside, restore the TTLs).
- Tests: `tests/js/nsparity.test.js` (the live captures `d01`–`d14`, a fake authoritative Globalping with referrals, quota stops, budget, cancel and what a stop left out, a stop after unproxied rows (`partial`, the switch `warn`), host names mixed with an address and an address only the CLI asks, a public IPv6 server sent to the CLI, the runbook, the command with private servers), `tests/e2e/zone.e2e.mjs` (offline: the consent dialog, exactly the planned probes, nothing hidden sent, statuses, the runbook, redacted exports, the BIND download, a private server in the command, the privacy line with the internal skip off, Compare / Stop from the keyboard, the progress of a tab mounted during a run, a quota 429 while About is shown — the warning toast "stopped, N record sets not compared", the `partial` headline, the fix step's "so far" and the switch `warn` —, Forget during a run — no toast, no result —, the table inside its card at 1280 / 1440 px, 320 / 375 px).

### 5.53 `lib/origincompare.js` — the old and the new server of a name, side by side
DOM-free. Before DNS moves a name to a new address: one HTTPS GET of a path at each address with the name as SNI and Host (§3, `httpsGetAtRequest`), the second from the first one's probe (`locations: <id>`), compared field by field. Addresses a probe cannot reach go to the CLI (`buildCompareCommand`: `ssl_origin_scan.py --compare`, §7).
```js
export const COMPARE_FIELDS = ['reach', 'status', 'location', 'contentType', 'title', 'body', 'hsts', 'server', 'certSubject', 'certCovers', 'certTrusted', 'certIssuer', 'certExpires', 'certFingerprint'],
  COMPARE_VERDICTS = ['same', 'differs', 'broken', 'incomplete', 'unreachable'], COMPARE_NOTES, COMPARE_SHARED = ['cert-untrusted', 'cert-name', 'cert-expiring'], COMPARE_ISSUES, COMPARE_EXPIRY_WARN_DAYS = 14, COMPARE_BODY_LIMIT = 10000, COMPARE_PROBES = 2,
  COMPARE_CERT_NAMES = 3
export function checkCompare({ host, oldIp, newIp, path, port }) -> { ok, probeable, host, oldIp, newIp, path, port, issues: [{ code, value }], private: string[] }
export function compareRequest({ ip, host, path, port, locations }) -> body
export function pageTitle(body) -> string|null ; export function parseHsts(value) -> { raw, maxAge /* null unless valid */, includeSubDomains, preload, valid }|null   // as a browser reads it (RFC 6797 6.1, 8.1): the first of joined headers, exactly one max-age of digits, no directive twice, a valueless includeSubDomains, token syntax
export function readSide(measurement, { ip, host, now }) -> CompareSide   // { ip, ok, failure: verify.parseFailure, status, location, contentType, server, hsts, body: { sha256, length, truncated, title }, cert: { sha256, subjectCN, hostnames, covers, issuer /* its CN · O, all Globalping reports */, issuerCN, selfSigned /* the probe's DEPTH_ZERO_SELF_SIGNED_CERT, or an issuer CN that is the subject CN: an expired self-signed certificate reports CERT_HAS_EXPIRED */, notBefore, notAfter, daysLeft, authorized, error }, probe, measurementId }
export function certNames(cert, limit = 3) -> string|null   // the subject CN first, then the SAN host names not listed yet, three and '+N' (the CLI's cert_names_text)
export function compareSides(oldSide, newSide, { now }) -> { verdict, fields: [{ key, old, new, same, severity: 'ok'|'info'|'warn'|'error', note, shared }], differences, worst /* of the fields that count */, shared /* COMPARE_SHARED notes */ }
export function sideFields(side) -> [{ key, value }]   // one side alone (the old server after a stop or a failure before the new one was asked)
export async function runCompare({ client, host, oldIp, newIp, path, port, signal, onSide, now }) -> { old, new, comparison, spent, ids, at }   // a Globalping error after the first measurement carries err.spent and err.first
export function buildCompareCommand({ host, oldIp, newIp, path, port, shell }) -> string|null
```
Rules: the new server not answering (compared no further), answering 4xx / 5xx where the old one did not, or a certificate that does not cover the name or that the probe did not trust while the old one was is an **error** (`broken`); another status, redirect, content type or title, a lost HSTS header (a policy is on with one valid max-age above 0, `parseHsts`; none: `hsts-lost`; one browsers ignore — no single valid max-age, a directive twice, `max_age` —: `hsts-invalid`; `max-age=0`, which tells browsers to forget the policy: `hsts-off`; a shorter max-age, or without the old header's `includeSubDomains` or `preload`: `hsts-weaker` — an old header that is off or invalid is no policy to lose), a new certificate expiring within 14 days or an untrusted certificate after an untrusted one that does not fail alike from the same issuer (`cert-untrusted-other`: whatever trusts the old one — a CDN that knows its origin CA, clients that know an internal CA — may refuse it) is a **warning** (`differs`); another body (a page with a token or a time differs on every request), Server header, the certificate's names (subject CN and SANs), issuer, expiry or certificate is **information** (`same`). A certificate problem **both servers share** — an untrusted certificate, the same one, or one that fails alike from the same issuer (`sharedUntrust`: the same probe error — a chain that lost its intermediate reports another —, the new one valid by now, neither self-signed, the issuer compared by its CN and O only, case and spacing aside, as that is all Globalping reports: Cloudflare's RSA and ECC origin CAs have no CN and are both "CloudFlare, Inc."; an origin CA certificate behind a CDN, renewed or not, is the usual case), a certificate that covers the name on neither, both expiring within 14 days with the new one no sooner — is no difference: the field is `shared` (severity `warn`, no `differs` highlight when the values match), its note goes to `shared`, and the verdict is taken from the other fields, so two identical servers are `same`; the card says each shared problem apart under the verdict (`oc.shared.<note>`), the JSON download carries `shared`. An old server that does not answer makes the comparison `incomplete` (the new values are information); neither answering is `unreachable` (the reach row a warning), never `broken`: the probe's network may be the cause as much as the servers. The body hash covers what the probe returned (the first 10,000 characters, decoded): `body-cut` says so. An untrusted certificate shows why (`no: <the probe's error>`, as the CLI's `no: <reason>`). Certificate identity and coverage reuse lib/verify.js `servedCert` and lib/domain.js `certCovers`. Tests: `tests/js/origincompare.test.js` (the live captures `h01` / `h02` and edits of them), `tests/e2e/retire.e2e.mjs` (offline: the prefill from the form, the CLI block for a private address, the consent dialog, two probes from one probe, `differs` then `broken` then `unreachable` (the failure in TR and EN), the same untrusted certificate on both → `same` with the shared warning (TR and EN, the JSON), an origin CA certificate then a self-signed one → `differs`, each side's reason and the note (TR and EN), Compare / Stop from the keyboard, a stop after the old server keeps its answer, a trip to About during a run ends on the card shown on return, the JSON download, 320 / 375 px).

### 5.54 `lib/egresslog.js`, `ui/egress-meter.js` — what this page sent, counted
The product says everything runs in the browser; the ledger lets a user check it without developer tools. `ui/egress-meter.js` starts with the shell's boot (before the first view mounts) and feeds one page-session log from two witnesses: a wrapper around `window.fetch` (each request the page's code starts; a redirect it followed, by `response.url`; a request that got no answer — a network error, a response the browser blocked, a cancel) and a buffered `PerformanceObserver` on `'resource'` entries (every request the browser made for the page, the app's own files loaded before the boot included). The wrapper passes the same arguments to the browser's fetch (called on the window) and returns a promise with the same outcome, so an unhandled rejection is still reported as one. Nothing is stored; a reload starts from zero.
```js
export const EGRESS_VIA = ['fetch', 'resource', 'redirect', 'failed'], MAX_SIGNATURES = 400, OVERFLOW_PATH = '/…'
export const MAX_NOTES = 8
```
