```js
export function requestSignature(url, base?) -> { key, origin, host, path, query } | null
  // path: plain lower-case words kept, every other segment (a name, an address, a hash, an id) '*', and so is the one after
  // a word an API puts a value after (domain, ip, autnum, entity, nameserver, measurements, subdomains), at most 8 segments;
  // query: the sorted parameter names (odd ones '*'), never a value; null for anything but http(s)
  // 'https://crt.sh/?q=%25.example.com&output=json' → key 'https://crt.sh/?output&q'
export function requestCount(entry) -> max(fetch, resource) + redirect   // a completed fetch is seen by both witnesses
export function countRequests(entries, pageOrigin) -> { thirdParty, self, hosts }
export function noteRequest(url, note)   // the sender says what a URL cannot, just before its fetch: a fixed word
                                         // (/^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,31}$/), never a value; anything else is dropped
export function onRequestNote(fn) -> unsubscribe   // fn(url, note): the meter
export function createEgressLog({ now, maxSignatures }) -> {
  record(url, { via, base, from }) -> boolean,   // never throws; past maxSignatures new shapes of an origin share one '/…'
                                                 // entry; a 'redirect' takes the notes of its request `from`
  note(url, note, { base }) -> boolean,          // adds a note to the signature (at most MAX_NOTES)
  snapshot() -> { since, at, entries: Array<{ key, origin, host, path, query, notes, fetch, resource, redirect, failed, first, last }> },
  clear(), subscribe(fn) -> unsubscribe    // fn once per microtask burst, never per request
}
// ui/egress-meter.js (browser only): export const egressLog; startEgressMeter(win) -> { fetch, resourceTiming } (once; it
// also hears the notes); egressMeterStatus()
```
**Notes.** A URL does not always say what a request carried. `lib/globalping.js` notes each measurement it creates as `ip-target` (the body sends a public address with the host name and port: SSL Targets › Verify, Retire an IP › Compare the old and the new server, which sends the path too), `host-target` (a host name alone: Domain Health's MTA-STS check, Renewal readiness's HTTP-01 test) or `dns-query` (a DNS measurement: a name and a type asked at a name server, Zone File › New name servers) — the kind, never the value; `lib/rdap.js` notes each registry server it asks as `rdap` (one the IANA bootstrap named, or rdap.org, whose redirect takes the note to the server it reached).
Both modules are on the start route (the budget of §6 names them). The footer's **What this page sent · N requests to third parties** (`countRequests`, refreshed at most once a second) links to `#/about?section=sent`.

### 5.55 `lib/egress.js` — the registry of third parties and the ledger
Every third party the page can contact, what each endpoint receives, and the rows of About › What this page sent. Loaded with the About view.
```js
export const DATA_KINDS = ['appFiles', 'nothing', 'dnsQuestions', 'nameServers', 'domains', 'hostnames', 'ipNamePairs', 'ipAddresses',
  'asNumbers', 'certSerial', 'keyHash', 'measurementIds', 'apiToken']   // nameServers: the servers a Globalping DNS measurement asks (Zone File › New name servers);
                                                                         // apiToken: the user's own token, in a header, for one fetch (Zone File › Fetch)
export const EGRESS_ROLES = ['site', 'dns', 'ct', 'passive', 'ip', 'registration', 'probes', 'dnsHosting']   // dnsHosting: the user's own DNS provider
export const NEVER_SENT = ['certificates', 'keys', 'zone', 'inventory', 'workspace', 'tracking']   // egress.never.<id>, each with its exceptions
export const EGRESS_SERVICES: Array<{ id, name, role, hosts: string[], noteHost?: string,
  endpoints: Array<{ id, sends: DATA_KINDS[], path? /* '*' one segment, '**' any run */, param? /* a query parameter name */,
    notes?: { [note]: DATA_KINDS[] } /* what a request carried by its sender's note; `sends` without one */ }> }>
  // doh (hosts from lib/resolvers.js RESOLVERS and ECS_RESOLVERS — AliDNS's JSON form for the mainland China locations; the row named as the resolver), crtsh (search → domains, serial → certSerial,
  // spkisha256 → keyHash), certspotter, hackertarget (hostsearch → domains, reverseiplookup → ipAddresses), anubis, otx,
  // thc (subdomains → domains, lookup → ipAddresses), ripestat (each data call by name: announced-prefixes → asNumbers;
  // prefix-overview, maxmind-geo-lite, reverse-dns-ip → ipAddresses; no catch-all, so a new call is an unknown endpoint),
  // ipwhois (its one API, /<address>), rdap-bootstrap (data.iana.org → nothing), rdap (rdap.org, rdap.identitydigital.services — §5.14 RDAP_OVERRIDES —, and noteHost 'rdap': any other https host whose
  // requests lib/rdap.js noted — a path that merely looks like an RFC 9082 lookup is not enough — with the endpoint by path:
  // …/domain/<name> → domains, …/ip/<address>[/<length>] → ipAddresses),
  // globalping (limits → nothing; POST measurements → hostnames by the note host-target, ipNamePairs by ip-target (Verify,
  // Retire an IP's old and new server), dnsQuestions + nameServers by dns-query (a DNS measurement: Zone File › New name
  // servers), all four without a note; GET measurements/<id> → measurementIds),
  // desec (dnsHosting; /api/*/domains/*/rrsets/ → domains + apiToken), digitalocean (dnsHosting; /*/domains/*/records → domains + apiToken)
export const SELF_SERVICE        // the page's own origin: appFiles
export function getEgressService(id) -> service | SELF_SERVICE | null
export function classifySignature(sig /* with notes */, { origin }) -> { service|null, endpoint|null, sends, label /* resolver name */ }
  // an unknown endpoint of a known service: every kind the service can receive; an unknown host or plain http: service null
export function classifyUrl(url, { origin, notes }) -> the same, or null for a non-http(s) URL
export function serviceSends(service) -> DATA_KINDS[]
export function ledgerRows(snapshot, { origin }) -> Array<{ host, origin, kind: 'self'|'service'|'unknown', serviceId, name, role,
  requests, fetch, resource, redirect, failed, sends, endpoints: Array<{ id, requests, sends }>, first, last }>
  // one row per origin; unknown hosts first (a host is one service only when every request of it classifies as it), then the
  // services in registry order (a service's hosts by requests), the page's own origin last; an endpoint's kinds add up over
  // its signatures
export function ledgerTotals(rows) -> { requests, services, hosts, self, unknown, failed }   // requests / hosts / failed: third parties
```
**The guarantee** (`tests/js/egress.test.js`): the sources (`assets/js/**` and `sw.js`) are scanned (a small lexer: comments, strings, templates, regular expressions) for network call sites — `fetch(`, `fetchJson(`, `fetchText(`, `fetchWithTimeout(`, `fetchAndRead(`, a `fetchImpl` passed on, the global fetch touched — and every file that has one must be declared with the registry services it sends to (a transport such as `lib/util.js` or a forwarder such as `lib/scanner.js` with none). Every `http(s)://` literal of **every** file — not only the call sites: a URL constant can live in a module without a fetch call (`lib/sourceinfo.js`, `ui/globalping-gate.js`) and be fetched by one that imports it — must classify through the registry (in a call site, as one of its declared services), or be a link host declared for that one file (the repository on GitHub, the registries' WHOIS pages, the platforms' and resolvers' home pages, the SVG namespace …), or a reserved `.invalid` name; a URL whose host is data (a template's `${…}` there) is refused in a call site and elsewhere needs a declared reason (a name read through the URL parser, a probe's URL that is only shown). A self-test plants a new API constant in `lib/sourceinfo.js` fetched from `lib/sources.js` and must see it fail. Every RIPEstat data call the code builds (a literal path segment after `RIPESTAT_BASE`, or the literal first argument of `lib/ipintel.js` `ripeUrl()`, the one template that takes the call from a variable) must be a named endpoint, every named one must still be called, and ipwho.is keeps its one caller and request. Every registry service must have an endpoint in the code (DoH: every resolver of `RESOLVERS`); XMLHttpRequest, beacons, sockets, EventSource, workers and `new Image()` are refused outright; the senders' notes are checked against the real `lib/globalping.js` and `lib/rdap.js`. A new endpoint therefore cannot ship without a registry line — the ledger names it and says what it receives — and a host that is still unknown at run time (a browser extension's request) is listed first, flagged.
**UI** (`ui/egress-panel.js`, About › What this page sent, `#/about?section=sent`): the summary ("Since 14:02 · 42 requests to third parties on 6 hosts · 38 files from this site"), a warning for hosts not in the registry, the table (service with host and role, requests with "N got no answer", what it received — per endpoint with counts when a host got several kinds —, last time; labelled cards at ≤ 640 px), refreshed at most once a second; **Clear**; how it is counted; the never-sent list; the deploy line from the version file (§5.35: "This page runs deploy <version> · commit <12 hex>" linking `<REPO_URL>/commit/<sha>`, "built without a commit name" or "its version file could not be read" once the file answered — a file of another deploy counts as not read, never as "no commit" —, until then the deploy alone, `data-state="reading"`; "Development copy …" in the repository).

### 5.56 `lib/ctrelated.js` — related domains from the certificates a scan already has
Other registrable domains named in the same certificates as the scanned domain's hosts (SAN co-occurrence) — a forgotten brand or campaign domain often shows up only that way. Pure; the certificates are a scan's CT `SourceResult.certs` (lib/sources.js CtCert: `names` is every name a source gave), so nothing is asked again.
```js
export const SHARED_CERT_DOMAINS = 12, RELATED_NAME_CAP = 12, RELATED_CERT_CAP = 10, RELATED_MAX = 200
export function uniqueCerts(certs) -> Array<CtCert & { complete }>
  // exact keys folded; a crt.sh certificate folded into the Cert Spotter issuance with the same validity whose names include
  // all of its own (the complete ones indexed by validity: linear); complete: Cert Spotter reported it (its whole name list)
export function relatedDomains(certs, { domains, now }) -> {
  related: Array<{ domain, certs, names /* ≤ 12 */, moreNames, certificates: Array<{ key, sources, issuer, notBefore, notAfter,
    url /* crt.sh page, or a crt.sh search by SHA-256 */, ownNames, domains, shared, partial }> /* newest first, ≤ 10 */,
    latest, current, sharedOnly, platform /* netinfo provider name */ }>,
  more, certs /* certificates of the scanned hosts read */, withOthers, partial /* crt.sh-only: names may be missing */, shared
}   // a scope (shop.example.com) counts its registrable domain as its own; a certificate naming more than SHARED_CERT_DOMAINS
    // registrable domains is a shared one (usually a CDN's or a host's) unless most of its other domains carry a scanned
    // domain's label (example.de, example.fr … for example.com: one company's multi-brand certificate, never shared); a
    // domain only in shared certificates is sharedOnly and listed last, a platform's domain after the brands; order then
    // by certificates, newest, name
```
**UI** (`ui/related-domains.js`, loaded by the Subdomains view only with a run that asks crt.sh or Cert Spotter): Sources › **Related domains in the same certificates** — waiting / failed states; "N other domains share certificates with these hosts (M certificates read)" counting the brand domains; per brand domain its certificate count, Current / Expired, a platform badge, its names, **The certificates** (issuer, validity, the scanned names it carries, a crt.sh link, "N registrable domains" / "crt.sh only" flags) and **Scan too** (its accessible name "Scan too: <domain>"), which starts a new scan of the run's domains together with it and moves the keyboard focus to the new run's title (disabled while a scan runs; the hint says the passive sources are asked again); the shared-only domains in one Disclosure ("N domains appear only in certificates that name more than 12 registrable domains"), hedged — usually a CDN's or a host's certificate for many customers, which says nothing about ownership, but it can be one company's for its many brands — and each with **Scan too** as well (never a platform's domain); the crt.sh partial-names note. The card is drawn again only when the run's CT results change — a scan starting or ending only turns the buttons on or off — and the disclosures the user opened stay open.

### 5.57 `lib/keycontinuity.js` — was the key reused across renewals, or rotated?
Everything is said of crt.sh, not of CT: its coverage is incomplete and can lag by days or weeks (checked 2026-09-28: letsencrypt.org's leaf issued on 2026-09-04 with embedded SCTs, which Cert Spotter lists with its `pubkey_sha256`, came back `[]` from crt.sh by key and by serial, while the previous leaf's key was found). Only other certificates with the key on crt.sh are evidence (a reuse); only this one is "no earlier certificate with this key on crt.sh", never proof of a rotation; none at all is "logged, but not on crt.sh yet" for a certificate with SCTs or from a known public CA.
```js
export const CRTSH_BASE = 'https://crt.sh/', KEY_TIMEOUT_MS = 60000, KEY_RETRY_DELAY_MS = 4000, KEY_MAX_ROWS = 2000
export const KEY_STATUSES = ['reused', 'single', 'not-indexed', 'not-found']
export async function spkiSha256(cert, { subtle }) -> hex   // SHA-256 of spkiDer (x509.computeFingerprints: WebCrypto, else lib/sha.js)
export function crtshKeyUrl(hex) -> 'https://crt.sh/?spkisha256=<hex>&output=json'   // TypeError unless 64 hex digits
export function parseKeyRows(rows, { cert, maxRows }) -> { certs: Array<{ ids, serialHex, issuer, notBefore, notAfter, isThis, url }>, rows, truncated }
  // one entry per issuer + serial (the precertificate folded in), oldest first; isThis: the looked-up certificate's serial and issuer CN
export function keyContinuity(certs, { cert, now, publicCa }) -> { status, sctCount, expectLogged, total, others, thisLogged, before,
  after, current, firstSeen, lastUntil, days /* firstSeen → now, or → lastUntil once all expired */, issuers }
  // status: reused (crt.sh has others), single (only this one), not-indexed (none, but cert.sctCount > 0 or publicCa: it was
  // logged), not-found (none, and nothing says it was logged); publicCa: the issuer is a known public CA (health.caaIssuerInfo)
export async function lookupKeyContinuity(cert, { fetchImpl, signal, timeoutMs, retryDelayMs, now, subtle, publicCa })
  -> KeyContinuity & { spki, url, certs, rows, truncated, checkedAt }   // one crt.sh search, retried once on a server / network
                                                                       // error (never after a timeout); throws its failure or AbortError
```
**UI** (`ui/key-continuity.js`, Certificate › CT logs, per shown certificate, kept per certificate for the page session): the public key's SHA-256 (computed locally, with copy), **Look this key up in CT** (only on the click; "Sends only this hash to crt.sh"; `ctx.requireOnline`), then **Key reused across renewals** ("crt.sh lists this key in N other certificates: in use since at least …", how many days, how many issued before and after this one), **No earlier certificate with this key on crt.sh** (the key may be new, or crt.sh lacks the earlier ones), **Logged, but not on crt.sh yet** (the certificate's SCT count, or its public CA) or **crt.sh has no certificate with this key** (no SCTs, not a known public CA: a private CA's certificates are never logged); for the first two, "crt.sh does not list this certificate itself" when it is missing, what it means for TLSA `3 1 1` records (a reused key keeps matching until a renewal changes it; a key that did change needs the next record published 2 × TTL first, or a `2 1 1` pin of the CA) and for HPKP-style pins, **Open the DANE / TLSA tab**, and the certificates with the key (crt.sh links, this certificate marked). A CA certificate picked in "Certificate shown" (`isCA`) is worded for a CA key: the same key in re-issued and cross-signed CA certificates is normal (**Key also in other certificates**), the consequences are a TLSA `2 1 1` record and a CA pin of that key (with a backup), none on crt.sh says crt.sh has most public CA certificates and a private CA's are never logged, and `publicCa` is not passed (a CA's issuer promises no log of it). A failure shows the error with Retry ("crt.sh is often busy"). The lookup runs on its own AbortController kept with the result per certificate, so leaving the view (to watch it in About › What this page sent), switching the language or loading another certificate does not stop it (its answer is kept for its certificate; another certificate's card never shows it); only a switch to another workspace or "Delete all local data" does (`cancelKeyLookups`).

### 5.58 `lib/zipread.js` — the containers mail reports arrive in
Reporters mail DMARC reports as `.zip` (Google) or `.xml.gz` (Microsoft) and TLS reports as `.json.gz`; a mailbox export is a zip of those. DOM-free, no dependency: gzip and deflate go through the platform's `DecompressionStream` (browsers, Node ≥ 21), the zip structure is read here.
```js
export const ZIP_ERRORS = ['not-zip','truncated','multi-disk','encrypted','method','crc','too-large','too-many','corrupt','overlap','nested','unsupported']
export const ZIP_LIMITS = { maxEntryBytes: 32 MiB, maxTotalBytes: 128 MiB /* inflated per dropped file, failed parts included */,
  maxEntries: 2000 /* per dropped file, the archives inside it included */, maxDepth: 2 /* a zip of .xml.gz */ }
export class ZipError extends Error { code /* ZIP_ERRORS */; detail; inflated /* bytes decompressed before it failed */ }
export function containerOf(bytes) -> 'gzip'|'zip'|null            // by the first bytes (1f 8b; PK 03 04 or an empty archive's PK 05 06), never by the name
export function crc32(bytes) -> number ; export function toBytes(input) -> Uint8Array
export function readZipDirectory(bytes, { maxEntries }) -> { entries: [{ name, method, flags, encrypted, directory, compressedSize, size, crc, localOffset }], total }
  // the end of central directory record (the last one; a comment may hold its signature), ZIP64 end record + locator + the 0x0001 extra field;
  // data descriptors (bit 3) need nothing more; UTF-8 names (bit 11, or valid UTF-8), else one character per byte. Throws not-zip, truncated, multi-disk
export async function extractZipEntry(bytes, entry, { maxBytes, signal }) -> Uint8Array   // stored or deflate only (method names deflate64, bzip2, lzma, zstd, xz, aes …);
                                                   // inflates no further than the declared size (one byte more is corrupt, found at once), checks the
                                                   // size and the CRC-32; throws encrypted, method, too-large, truncated, corrupt, crc
export async function inflate(bytes, 'gzip'|'deflate-raw'|'deflate', { maxBytes, signal, DecompressionStreamImpl }) -> Uint8Array   // stops as soon as the output passes maxBytes;
                                                   // an abort cancels the stream even while a read waits on it
export async function gunzip(bytes, { maxBytes, signal }) -> Uint8Array   // the trailer's ISIZE is not trusted (mod 2^32; a cut file has none): the stream is capped instead
export async function unpackFile({ name, bytes }, { maxEntryBytes, maxTotalBytes, maxEntries, maxDepth, signal }) -> {
  files: [{ name /* .gz taken off */, path /* 'outer.zip › dmarc/inner.xml.gz' */, bytes, via: Array<'zip'|'gzip'> }],
  problems: [{ path, code /* ZIP_ERRORS */, detail }],
  inflated   // what it cost: the bytes charged to maxTotalBytes (inflated, failed parts included, or a stored entry's size; 0 for a plain file)
}   // directories, __MACOSX/ and .DS_Store skipped; one broken entry never stops the others; rejects only with an AbortError
```
**A hostile archive** (anyone can mail a "report" to a rua mailbox) costs no more than the caps: an entry inflates only up to its declared size, every byte inflated is charged to the dropped file's budget whether its part then unpacks or fails (a lying CRC or size, a gzip whose trailer fails), and through `inflated` to the drop's (§5.59 `MAX_DROP_BYTES`), entries are counted across the nested archives, and entries whose bytes (local header and data) overlap — the trick of a small zip that unpacks to terabytes, where every central directory entry names one stream — are refused as `overlap` before any is inflated. A 50 KB zip of 1,000 entries naming one 40 MB stream takes milliseconds.
`tests/js/zipread.test.js` reads archives Python's `zipfile` and `gzip` wrote (`tests/fixtures/mailreports`, `gen_mailreports.py`: deflated, stored, a zip of zips and gzip files, data descriptors) and builds the broken ones byte by byte (ZIP64, encrypted, bzip2, a bad CRC, a lying size, several disks, truncation, the caps, overlapping entries, failed parts paid for, a read the abort ends).

### 5.59 `lib/dmarcreport.js` — DMARC aggregate reports (RFC 7489 Appendix C)
Who sends mail as a domain, whether it passes DMARC and what stands between the domain and `p=reject`. DOM-free. **XML:** a minimal reader in the lib, not DOMParser in the view — the reports are a small data-only subset of XML, the same code then runs and is tested in Node (which has no DOMParser) on real report bytes, and a reader that refuses a DTD internal subset cannot expand an entity (no billion laughs, no external entity).
```js
export function parseXml(text, { maxElements = 2e6, maxDepth = 64 }) -> XmlElement { name /* local, lowercase */, attrs, children, text }   // throws XmlError: unterminated,
                                                   // mismatch, unclosed, doctype (an internal subset), empty, limit; the five predefined entities, character references,
                                                   // CDATA, comments and processing instructions; xmlChild / xmlChildren / xmlText(el, name). Linear time: attributes are read
                                                   // by a forward scan, never a backtracking pattern (a megabyte tag with no '=' reads in milliseconds)
export function parseAggregateReport(text, { file }) -> { ok: true, report } | { ok: false, code: 'xml'|'not-dmarc'|'incomplete', detail }
AggregateReport = { kind: 'dmarc', key /* org|report_id: a report dropped twice counts once */, file, org, email, extraContact, reportId, begin: Date, end: Date,
  errors: string[] /* the reporter's <error> notes */, policy: { domain, p, sp /* default p */, np, pct /* default 100 */, adkim, aspf /* 'r'|'s' */, fo, testing /* DMARCbis t */ },
  records: [{ ip, count, disposition /* DISPOSITIONS: none, pass (DMARCbis), quarantine, reject */, dkim, spf /* policy_evaluated: aligned results */, reasons: [{ type, comment }], headerFrom, envelopeFrom, envelopeTo,
    dkimAuth: [{ domain, selector, result, human }], spfAuth: [{ domain, scope, result /* 'hardfail' read as fail */ }] }], messages, skipped /* records without an address or count */ }
  // Google-, Microsoft- and DMARCbis-style reports (a version, namespaces, np, discovery_method, no pct); dates in seconds (milliseconds read as such)
export function looksLikeAggregate(text) -> boolean   // a feedback document element after the XML declaration, comments and a DOCTYPE without an internal subset
export function decodeReportText(bytes) -> string   // BOM, then an XML declaration's encoding, else UTF-8
export async function readReportFiles([{ name, bytes }], { signal, limits /* unpackFile's + maxDropBytes */, onProgress }) -> { dmarc: AggregateReport[], tls: TlsReport[],
  problems: [{ path, code /* REPORT_PROBLEMS */, detail }], read /* plain files looked at */, files /* dropped files read */, inflated /* what the drop's archives cost */, stopped }
  // archives unpacked (§5.58); each file told apart by its content (XML with a feedback element / JSON with policies), never by its name; at most
  // MAX_REPORT_FILES = 2,000 plain files, the dropped files after that named (too-many), not unpacked; a plain file past ZIP_LIMITS.maxEntryBytes (32 MiB)
  // is named too-large, never parsed, whether dropped as it is or unpacked. One inflation budget for the whole drop: MAX_DROP_BYTES = 1 GiB across the
  // unpackFile calls (each still within its own maxTotalBytes), so 200 dropped gzip bombs inflate 1 GiB, not 200 × 128 MiB; past it an archive is
  // too-large ('the drop as a whole') and never opened, a plain report is still read. Before each dropped file and each plain file an archive holds,
  // the event loop gets a turn once READ_YIELD_MS = 50 ms of work have passed and the signal is checked: a Stop acts in the middle of a zipped mailbox
  // folder. A Stop (the signal's abort) resolves with what was read before it — the reports of the archive it cut too — and stopped: true; `files` then
  // counts the dropped files done and the one it cut once unpacked.
  // onProgress(done, total) counts files: a dropped file is one until it is unpacked, then the plain files inside it (at least one), so `total` grows
export function aggregateDmarc(reports) -> { domains: DomainAggregate[] /* most messages first */, duplicates }
DomainAggregate = { domain, reports, messages, pass, fail, begin, end, days, policy /* the latest report's */, policies /* every p seen, latest first */,
  reporters: [{ org, email, reports, messages, pass, begin, end }], sources: SourceRow[] /* most messages first */, errors, skipped }
SourceRow = { ip, version, private, messages, pass, fail /* DMARC: an aligned DKIM or SPF pass */, dkimAligned, spfAligned, dispositions: { none, pass, quarantine, reject },
  headerFrom, envelopeFrom, spfAuth / dkimAuth / overrides: [{ …, messages }], reporters, records, begin, end }
export function spfDomainsFor(agg) -> string[]   // the header-from and SPF-checked domains of the policy domain's organisation, by volume, at most SPF_MAX_DOMAINS = 10;
                                                   // a domain of another organisation a report names is never looked up (a crafted report picks no names)
export async function loadSpfContext(domain, { dns, signal, noCache }) -> { domain, status: 'ok'|'none'|'failed'|'multiple', record, tree, mxAddresses, error, at }
  // spfLookupCount (§5.15) + the addresses of its mx hosts (≤ 10); names and types to the DoH resolvers only; a lookup with no answer is 'failed', never "no SPF";
  // noCache: every query past the client's cache ("Check again"); rejects only with an AbortError
export function classifySources(agg, { spf: Map<domain, SpfContext>, index /* inventory.buildIpIndex */ }) -> Array<SourceRow & { cls, reason, detail, servers, spfNow, spfDomain,
  spfListed /* a permerror's strict: false verdict */, atRisk /* messages that passed through SPF alone while spfNow is a permerror */, fixes }>
export function dmarcOverview(agg, rows, { spfChecked }) -> { domain, messages, pass, fail, compliance /* null without mail */, byClass, verdict,
  enforced /* p=reject at 100 %, not t=y: what fails is refused now */, blockers, blocked,
  atRisk, atRiskMessages, spfError /* { domain, reason, sources }|null */, unknown, unknownFail, notes }
export function dmarcCsvRows(agg, rows) -> object[]   // DMARC_CSV_COLUMNS
export const SOURCE_CLASSES = ['yours','third-party','forwarder','unknown'], CLASS_REASONS, FIX_CODES = ['spf-permerror','dkim-sign','dkim-align','dkim-fix','spf-add','spf-align'],
  DMARC_VERDICTS = ['no-mail','enforced','ready','fix-first','spf-broken'],
  DMARC_NOTES = ['short-range','pct','testing','mixed-policy','spf-unknown','spf-permerror','quarantine','rejected-now','spf-all'], DISPOSITIONS = ['none','pass','quarantine','reject'],
  FORWARD_OVERRIDES = ['forwarded','mailing_list','trusted_forwarder'], REPORT_PROBLEMS /* ZIP_ERRORS + not-report, xml, not-dmarc, incomplete, not-json, not-tlsrpt, empty */,
  DMARC_CSV_COLUMNS /* … disposition_none, disposition_pass, …, spf_now, spf_now_term, spf_now_reason, … */, SHORT_RANGE_DAYS = 7, MAX_REPORT_FILES = 2000, READ_YIELD_MS = 50,
  MAX_DROP_BYTES = 1 GiB, XML_LIMITS
```
**Classes**, in order: an address of the server list is **yours** (`inventory`); one the current SPF authorizes (`spfEvaluate` for each domain of `spfDomainsFor`, the first pass wins) is **yours** when the deciding term is the domain's own (`spf`: an ip4 / ip6 / a / mx term, or an include of its own organisation) and an **authorized third party** through an include (or an a / mx host) of another organisation (`spf-include`, the first foreign domain on the path); when the SPF could not be checked (no context, a failed lookup, `unknown`, several SPF records), an aligned SPF pass in the reports counts as yours (`spf-report`). **A permerror** (a syntax error, more than 10 lookups or 2 void lookups before the address, …) is what receivers get, but it hides whom the record means: the same tree is then asked with `strict: false`, only to tell whose the sender is — **yours** (`spf-listed`) or an **authorized third party** (`include-listed`) when the broken record lists it —; such a known source gets the `spf-permerror` fix first, and `atRisk` counts its mail that passed through SPF alone (no aligned DKIM), which fails from now on. Then an aligned DKIM pass: a **forwarder** when the receiver says so (`forwarded`: an override `forwarded` / `mailing_list` / `trusted_forwarder`, or `local_policy` with `arc=pass`), **yours** when all its mail passed SPF aligned too (`dkim-signed`: forwarding breaks SPF; this happens when the current SPF passes every address or no longer lists it), a **forwarder** when it carries a selector the known senders sign with (`dkim-forwarded`: the original signature survived the relay), an **authorized third party** when SPF passes for a bounce domain of another organisation (`dkim-service`), else a forwarder (`dkim-only`). The rest is **unknown**: `spf-removed` (the reports saw an aligned SPF pass the current SPF no longer gives), `foreign` (it authenticates only as another domain), `none`. **Fixes** (a known source that fails DMARC, or has mail at risk), `spf-permerror` first when the current SPF gives it a permerror (the record, not the sender, breaks SPF), then the most robust first: no DKIM result for the organisation → `dkim-sign` (`dkim-align` when it signs as another domain), a signature that does not verify → `dkim-fix`; the organisation's SPF not passing (and not authorizing it now) → `spf-add`, SPF passing only for another domain → `spf-align`. **Verdict:** `no-mail`; with blockers (yours and third parties that fail, most failing messages first) `enforced` when `p=reject` is in force at 100 % and not in DMARCbis test mode (`t=y`) — mail refused now — else `fix-first`; `spf-broken` when none fails but known sources passed through SPF alone and the current SPF gives them a permerror (`atRisk`; with `enforced`, that mail is refused now); else `enforced` or `ready` (only unknown senders would be turned away). **Notes:** the reports span < 7 days, pct < 100, test mode (`t=y`: receivers apply the next lower policy), the policy changed in the period, SPF not checked, the current SPF gives a permerror (`spfError`: its domain, the reason of the most mail and how many sources), `p=quarantine` and ready (next: reject), known sources already rejected, an SPF record that passes every address (`+all`: a pass by `all` never classifies a source).

### 5.60 `lib/tlsrpt.js` — SMTP TLS reports (RFC 8460)
```js
export function parseTlsReport(textOrObject, { file }) -> { ok: true, report } | { ok: false, code: 'not-json'|'not-tlsrpt'|'incomplete', detail }
TlsReport = { kind: 'tlsrpt', key /* org|report-id */, file, org, contact, reportId, begin, end, success, failure,
  policies: [{ type /* 'sts'|'tlsa'|'no-policy-found' */, domain, strings /* policy-string */, mode /* an MTA-STS mode: line */, mx /* mx-host or the draft's mx-host-pattern */, success, failure,
    failures: [{ type /* lowercased */, known, sendingIp, mx, helo, receivingIp, sessions, reason /* failure-reason-code */, info /* additional-information */ }] }] }
export function summarizeTls(reports) -> { domains: [{ domain, reports, begin, end, success, failure, rate /* null without sessions */, orgs, policies /* by type and mode */,
  byType: [{ type, known, sessions, mx, orgs, receivingIps, reasons }], byMx: [{ mx, sessions, types }], failures /* TLS_CSV_COLUMNS rows */ }], duplicates }
export function tlsAdvice(type) -> { type /* 'other' for one RFC 8460 does not define */, area: 'starttls'|'certificate'|'dane'|'sts-policy'|'other', tools: Array<'health'|'tlsa'|'cert'> }
export function tlsCsvRows(summary) -> object[]
export const TLS_RESULT_TYPES /* the 11 of RFC 8460 §4.3 in its order */, TLS_POLICY_TYPES, TLS_REPORT_ERRORS, TLS_ADVICE_AREAS, TLS_CSV_COLUMNS
```
Counts sent as strings and result types in capitals are read; a report dropped twice counts once. The view words the advice (`rpt.tls.advice.<type>`) and links the tools: Domain Health (`#/health?domain=…&run=0`, the MTA-STS card and its policy check), the MX host's TLSA records in DNS Lookup (`_25._tcp.<mx>`, DNSSEC on) and its certificate in the Certificate view (`?host=<mx>&run=0`, then the DANE / TLSA tab).

### 5.61 `lib/zonefetch.js` — a zone read from the DNS provider's API (Zone File › Fetch)
DOM-free; I/O through the injectable `fetchImpl`. Only providers whose API a page can call with an `Authorization` header (§3).
```js
export const ZONE_PROVIDERS = [{ id: 'desec', name, api: 'https://desec.io/api/v1', format: 'desec-api', scheme: 'Token', tokenUrl, docsUrl },
  { id: 'digitalocean', name, api: 'https://api.digitalocean.com/v2', format: 'digitalocean-api', scheme: 'Bearer', tokenUrl, docsUrl }]   // the links are opened, never fetched
export const ZONE_FETCH_ERRORS = ['provider', 'domain', 'token', 'auth', 'forbidden', 'not-found', 'rate-limited', 'http', 'network', 'timeout', 'response']
export const DESEC_TYPES /* deSEC's manageable types, the common ones first */, MAX_RECORDS = 20000, DO_PAGE_SIZE = 200, MAX_WAIT_MS = 60000, DEFAULT_WAIT_MS = 20000
export class ZoneFetchError extends Error { code /* ZONE_FETCH_ERRORS */, params: { status?, detail? /* the provider's text, plain, ≤ 200 chars */, retryAfterS? } }   // never holds the token
export function getZoneProvider(id) ; export function cleanToken(token) -> string|null /* trimmed, 16–512 printable ASCII, no white space */ ; export function zoneName(domain) -> string|null
export function retryAfterMs(res) ; export function desecTotal(body) -> number|null   // "Pagination required … (N total)"
export async function fetchZone(providerId, domain, token, { fetchImpl, signal, onProgress, timeoutMs = 20000, sleepImpl } = {})
  -> { provider, domain, format, text /* the provider's JSON documents, one per line, for parseZone */, requests, records, total, partial, byType }
```
- The token is a parameter of one `fetchZone` call: sent only to the provider's API host in `Authorization` (never in a URL), never stored, logged, returned or put in an error. Requests: `credentials: 'omit'`, `referrerPolicy: 'no-referrer'`, `cache: 'no-store'`, `redirect: 'error'` (a redirect would carry the header elsewhere).
- deSEC: `/domains/{zone}/rrsets/`; over 500 RRsets (a 400 with the total) it reads `?type=` by `DESEC_TYPES` until the total is in, a type over 500 by its first page (`&cursor=`): the listing is then partial and zoneparse says so. DigitalOcean: `/domains/{zone}/records?per_page=200&page=N` until `meta.total` (or no `links.pages.next`), merged into one listing.
- Requests 150 ms (deSEC) / 100 ms (DigitalOcean) apart. A 429 waits for a readable `Retry-After` — neither provider exposes it to a page —, else deSEC's "Expected available in N seconds", else 20 s; past 60 s or a fourth 429 it fails `rate-limited`. 401 → `auth`, 403 → `forbidden` (DigitalOcean: no `domain:read` scope; deSEC: a login token that needs MFA), 404 → `not-found`, any other status → `http`, a body that is not a listing → `response`, transport → `network` / `timeout`; cancellation stays an AbortError.

### 5.62 `lib/dohjson.js` — the JSON form of DNS-over-HTTPS (AliDNS)
DOM-free, no I/O; loaded by lib/doh.js with the first question to a `format: 'json'` resolver (§5.8, §5.9).
```js
export const DNS_JSON = 'application/dns-json'
export function ecsSubnet(ecs /* 'a.b.c.d/24' | { subnet } | { address, sourcePrefix } */) -> string|null   // IPv4 host bits zeroed; a bare address → its /24 (IPv6 /56)
export function dnsJsonUrl(base, { name, type, ecs }) -> string   // ?name=<encoded>&type=<number>[&edns_client_subnet=<subnet>]; unknown type → DnsWireError
export function decodeDnsJson(json) -> message   // the shape lib/dnswire.js decodeMessage returns; not a DNS JSON answer → DnsWireError
```
Each record's presentation text (A, AAAA, NS, CNAME, PTR, DNAME, MX, TXT / SPF with `\DDD` escapes and several strings, SOA, SRV, CAA, DS, DNSKEY, TLSA — also as AliDNS's bare RDATA hex —, SSHFP, SVCB / HTTPS, NAPTR, HINFO, URI, RFC 3597 `\# n hex`) becomes dnswire `data`, is encoded and decoded again, so its `text` is exactly what a wire answer of the same record gives and Global DNS groups JSON and wire answers together; a record it cannot read keeps its presentation text as `data` and `text`. An echoed `edns_client_subnet` becomes `edns.ecs` with `scopePrefix: null`; no NSID, no EDE. Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/dns-json/alidns.json` (captured 2026-10-02, scrubbed).

### 5.63 `lib/portfolio.js` — the Domain portfolio: many domains, one row each
DOM-free; the view (§6) and the runner's `audit` (§9). Every network call takes the injected DohClient (through §5.46 `passportDns`: each question once per domain) or `fetchImpl`, and the caller's signal; only an abort rejects.
```js
export const PORTFOLIO_MAX_DOMAINS = 300, PORTFOLIO_CONCURRENCY = 4 /* domains' DNS questions at once */, RDAP_CONCURRENCY = 4 /* requests in flight */
                                                   // (to registry servers); a row's RDAP — its own, its name servers' domains' — is waited for outside its domain's place, and a lookup
                                                   // waiting for its registry's turn or 429 holds no request's place: a registry that turns requests away never slows another
export const PORTFOLIO_DKIM_SELECTORS = ['google', 'selector1', 'selector2', 'default', 'k1', 's1', 'dkim', 'mail']   // and one random label first: a
                                                   // `*._domainkey` wildcard (often "v=DKIM1; p=" of a domain that sends nothing) answers every selector, so its record is no key
export const PORTFOLIO_LOOKUPS = ['rdap', 'ns', 'ds', 'dnskey', 'caa', 'mx', 'txt', 'dmarc', 'spf', 'dkim', 'mtaSts', 'tlsRpt'], LOOKUP_SOURCES /* rdap → 'rdap', the rest 'doh' */
export const PORTFOLIO_CELLS = ['expiry', 'status', 'registrar', 'dnssec', 'ns', 'caa', 'spf', 'dmarc', 'dkim', 'mtaSts', 'parked'], CELL_LOOKUPS /* a column's lookups */
export const CRITICAL_STATUSES = ['serverHold', 'clientHold', 'redemptionPeriod', 'pendingDelete'], EXPORT_COLUMNS
export function parsePortfolioInput(text, { max }) -> { domains /* registrable (§5.46 passportDomain), in order, once */, reduced: [{ input, domain }], invalid, capped }
export function statusRisk(statuses) -> { flags, critical, transferLock: boolean|null, registryLock: boolean|null, transferCodes: string[],
  risk: 'critical'|'pending-transfer'|'hijack'|'ok'|null }   // RFC 8056 and EPP spellings. transferLock: any transfer prohibition — the registrar's
  // (clientTransferProhibited), the registry's (serverTransferProhibited: RFC 5731, transfer requests MUST be rejected) or RFC 9083's plain "transfer
  // prohibited" —, as Domain overview and Domain Health read it; registryLock: serverTransferProhibited; transferCodes: the statuses that prohibit it;
  // risk 'hijack' only with no transfer prohibition at all, 'pending-transfer' a transfer under way (no critical status). Every status (the flags, the
  // codes, the facts' `statuses`) is said in its RDAP spelling (RFC 8056 / RFC 9083: 'client transfer prohibited'), each once: EPP's
  // 'clientTransferProhibited' arrives lower-cased from §5.14
export function nsDomainsOf(hosts, domain) -> [{ domain, hosts, own }]   // a zone's name servers by registrable domain, its own first
export async function runPortfolioLookup(id, domain, { dns, fetchImpl, signal, raw, rdapFor, dkim, noCache })
export function createPortfolio({ domains, dns, fetchImpl, dkim = true, concurrency, rdapConcurrency, rdapOptions, onEvent }) -> run
  // run.start({ signal }): rejects with an AbortError on a stop — the rows not finished are 'stopped', what landed stays; run.retry(domain, lookups, { signal }): those lookups
  // again past the DNS cache (RDAP too when listed); run.retryRdap(domain): a domain's RDAP again, a name server domain's too (every row reading it is told);
  // run.domains(), row(domain), facts(domain, { now }), allFacts, pending(domain), affectedBy(domain) /* the rows reading a domain's RDAP */; events: { type: 'lookup', domain, lookup },
  // { type: 'rdap', domain }, { type: 'row', domain, state: 'running'|'done'|'stopped' }
export function portfolioFacts(raw, { now, rdap /* the run's RDAP results */, dkim }) -> facts   // pure: codes and values, never text
export function cellFailures(facts, column) -> [{ lookup, status /* §5.36 */, nsDomain? }]   // what a column's "⚠ n/a" says and its Retry asks
export function rowRisk(facts) -> 'critical'|'ns-unregistered'|'pending-transfer'|'expired'|'expiring'|'ns-expiring'|'hijack'|'warn'|'ok'|null   // the first that
                                                   // applies, worst first: a name server domain nobody has registered is as urgent as a critical status
export function unregisteredNsDomains(facts) -> string[]   // the name server domains the registry answers 404 for (not the own ones)
export function expiryBand(daysLeft)               // Domain Health's bands: < 0, < 30, < 60
export function expiryEvents(factsList) -> [{ domain, expires, daysLeft, registrar, portfolio, nsOf }]   // one per domain, the name servers' domains too, soonest first
export function expiryUid(domain) -> 'expiry-<domain>@domainscope' ; exportRow(facts) ; portfolioSummaryFacts(factsList, { at, stopped, notLooked, audit })
                                                   // exportRow's dmarc: 'p=none' | 'p=quarantine' | 'p=reject' | 'missing' | 'invalid' (p=none and no record never alike);
                                                   // its statuses: comma-separated (a status has spaces)
                                                   // portfolioSummaryFacts -> { …, nsUnregistered: [{ domain, of }], pendingTransfer: string[], dnssec: { validated, signed, broken, unsigned } }
```
- **Registration:** §5.14 `rdapDomain`: the registry's server from the IANA bootstrap, rdap.org only as its paced fallback. RDAP is asked once per distinct domain of a run — a portfolio domain and a name server domain alike (`rdapFor`, at most `RDAP_CONCURRENCY` in flight; a Retry of the NS answer never asks a known one again) — so a provider shared by every zone is asked once. `registration.state`: `ok` (expires, daysLeft and its band, the registrar, the statuses read for risk — a critical status `critical`, a pending transfer `pending-transfer`, no transfer prohibition at all `hijack` (any of clientTransferProhibited, serverTransferProhibited or a plain "transfer prohibited" locks; serverTransferProhibited is also the registry lock) —, the delegation's signed flag), `unsupported` (a TLD without RDAP; `whois` = §5.46 `registryWhois`: the registry's web WHOIS for .tr, .de, .jp, .ch, else the TLD's IANA page), `not-found` (the registry answers 404: not registered), `invalid`, `failed` (its §5.36 status) or `pending`.
- **Name servers:** their registrable domains (`own` when under the domain itself: they expire with it) with each one's registration from the run's RDAP and `minDaysLeft`. A name server domain the registry answers 404 for (`not-found`) is not registered: anyone can register it and answer DNS for every zone on those name servers — `unregisteredNsDomains`, the row's risk `ns-unregistered`, an answer and never a failed lookup. A row is done once its name servers' domains are read too.
- **DNSSEC:** DS at the parent (`unsigned` without one), `validated` when the DNSKEY answer carries AD, `failing` when the DNSKEY answer is an error rcode next to a DS (broken: validating resolvers refuse the domain; counted as `broken`, never as signed). **CAA** (§5.15 findCaa through §5.46 certsCard): its state, issuers and wildcard issuers. **Mail** (§5.46 mailCard): MX (`null` / `none` / `some`), SPF (one record, its `all`, the lookup count of §5.15 spfLookupCount once the TXT answer landed: `over` past 10, `partial` when a lookup in its tree failed), DMARC (policy, pct), DKIM (the selectors with a key, revoked ones, a wildcard; `off` when not asked, never "none"), MTA-STS (`v=STSv1` with an id) and TLS-RPT (`v=TLSRPTv1` with a rua): `present` / `none` / `invalid`. **Parked:** a domain without MX, or with a null MX, should say it takes no mail — null MX, `-all`, `p=reject` (`complete` true / false, or null while a part is not known).
- A lookup that failed is a §5.36 status on its part (`failure`), never "none"; `cellFailures` lists them per column (the name servers column its name server domains whose RDAP failed too, `nsDomain`).
- The run is the view's page-session state (§6 7c): a language switch or a visit elsewhere shows it again as it is, so nothing is rebuilt from a copy.
- Tests: `tests/js/portfolio.test.js` (the list, statuses read for risk, the name server domains deduped, a run over a fake DoH and RDAP registry — a stop, a Retry past the cache, DKIM off —, the facts, the calendar dates, the exports, and no dead API: every export is read and every method of a run called by the view or the runner).

### 5.64 `lib/policy.js` — a domain policy and its audit
Pure (no DOM, network, storage or clock beyond the facts); the view's policy tab and the runner's `audit` (§9).
```js
export const POLICY_VERSION = 1, POLICY_MAX_CHARS = 16384, POLICY_MAX_RULES = 40, POLICY_OPS = ['>=', '<=', '>', '<', '==', '!=']
export const POLICY_STATUSES = ['pass', 'fail', 'unknown'], POLICY_ERRORS = ['not-json', 'not-object', 'too-large', 'too-many', 'unknown-rule', 'outside-rules', 'bad-value', 'empty']
export const POLICY_RULES = [{ id, kind: 'number'|'ordered'|'bool'|'enum'|'list', op?, min?, max?, levels?, values?, area, example, health /* Domain Health check ids */ }]
  // expiryDays (number, >=) · transferLock (bool: any transfer prohibition, the registrar's or the registry's) · status.critical (bool) · registrar (list: a part of
  // the RDAP registrar's name) · nsExpiryDays (number, >=; the name servers' other domains: in-bailiwick ones expire with the domain, which expiryDays reads, so only
  // those pass it; one the registry does not know fails it — anyone can register it and take over DNS) · dnssec (ordered: unsigned < signed < validated; DS whose
  // keys cannot be validated fails every requirement: broken) · caa (enum: present —
  // deny-all counts — | deny-all) · caa.issuers (list: the only CAs CAA may allow) · spf (enum: valid) · spf.lookups (number, <=) · spf.all (ordered: +all < ?all < ~all <
  // -all; no "all" reads as ?all) · dmarc.policy (ordered: none < quarantine < reject) · dkim · mtaSts · tlsRpt · mx.null (bool)
export const POLICY_PRESETS = { baseline, 'strict-mail', parked /* the change request's "Lock down a parked domain": null MX, -all, p=reject, CAA deny-all */ },
  POLICY_PRESET_IDS, POLICY_I18N /* en, tr: rule names, statuses, presets, parse errors, the evidence */
export function parsePolicy(textOrObject) -> { policy: { name, rules: [{ id, kind, op, value }] }|null, errors: [{ code, rule?, value?, detail?, example? }] }
export function policyObject(policy) -> { name, version, rules } ; policyText(policy) /* indented JSON + newline */ ; presetPolicy(id) ; policyRule(id)
export function ruleValueText(entry) ; requirementText(entry) /* ">= 30", "true", a list between semicolons: a registrar's name has commas */
export function evaluatePolicy(policy, facts /* §5.63 */) -> [{ id, status, actual, evidence: { key, params }, required }]
export function auditPortfolio(policy, factsList) -> { name, rules: [{ id, required }], rows: [{ domain, cells, pass, fail, unknown }],
  counts: { domains, failing /* a rule failed */, passing /* every rule passed */, unknown /* the rest */, pass, fail, cells } }
export function auditCsv(audit, { t }) /* a row per domain, a column per rule: "FAIL · 20 days left (…)" */ ; auditJson(audit, { t, policy, app, version, at }) ; evidenceText(cell, t)
```
A policy is JSON, flat (`{ "dmarc.policy": ">= quarantine", "dnssec": "signed", "caa": "present", "transferLock": true, "expiryDays": ">= 30" }`) or `{ name, version, rules }` as the editor exports it. A number rule takes a number (its natural operator) or "OP N"; an ordered rule a level (at least it) or "OP level" (`p=reject` and a bare qualifier such as `-` read too); a bool true / false; an enum one of its values (true: the first); a list a string or a list, its entries between semicolons as the editor writes them (a registrar's name has commas; case-free duplicates dropped). An unknown rule, a value its rule does not take, or a key beside a nested `rules` object (`outside-rules`; such keys count toward the 40-rule limit) is an error and that rule is left out — the editor says so, the runner refuses the file —: a typo never passes. A rule whose facts could not be read (a lookup that failed, a TLD without RDAP, DKIM not asked, a lookup not landed yet) is `unknown`, never a pass or a fail, its evidence saying why; a domain that does not exist in DNS fails its DNS rules with that reason; a partial SPF count fails only when it is over already; DS whose keys cannot be validated (an error answer to DNSKEY: broken, validating resolvers refuse the domain) fails every dnssec requirement, while DS whose DNSKEY answer was not read (its lookup failed, or has not landed) is "not known" where signed and validated would differ. The evidence is a key and params in both languages, so the view and the runner word a cell alike; a value from DNS or the registry is a param (a code part in a summary). Tests: `tests/js/policy.test.js`.

### 5.65 `lib/ics.js` — an expiry calendar (RFC 5545)
```js
export const ICS_FOLD_OCTETS = 75, ICS_ALARM_DAYS = [30, 7], ICS_PRODID = '-//DomainScope//Domain portfolio//EN'
export function buildCalendar(events: [{ uid, date, summary, description?, alarm? }], { now, name /* X-WR-CALNAME */, prodId }) -> string
export function icsEscape(value) ; foldIcsLine(line)
```
CRLF line ends; lines folded at 75 octets of UTF-8, never inside a character (a continuation starts with one space); TEXT values escaped (`\\`, `\;`, `\,`, a line break `\n`; other control characters dropped). One all-day event per entry (`DTSTART;VALUE=DATE` on the date's local day — the day the table shows; a `YYYY-MM-DD` string as written —, `DTEND` the next day, `TRANSP:TRANSPARENT`) with a display alarm 30 and 7 days before. No `METHOD`: a published file, not an iTIP message (METHOD:PUBLISH would need an ORGANIZER, RFC 5546). The caller's UID is stable per subject (§5.63 `expiryUid`: importing a newer file updates the event instead of adding one), `SEQUENCE` grows with the date (a renewal is a later revision), and `DTSTAMP` (`now`) is the only part that differs between two files of the same dates. An entry without a usable date is left out. Pure. Tests: `tests/js/ics.test.js` (a golden file, folding, escaping).

### 5.66 `lib/zonediff.js` — two zones compared record set by record set (Zone File › Compare)
Pure, synchronous, DOM-free; nothing is sent or stored. A semantic diff: never a text diff.
```js
export function diffZones(a, b, { ignoreTtl = false, joinTxt = true, ignoreSoa = false, ignoreApexNs = false } = {}) -> {
  a, b /* About */, relative /* the origins differ */, options, rows: DiffRow[],
  counts: { added, removed, changed, same, ignored, total } }      // TypeError for a missing zone or one with a fatal issue
About = { origin, format, dialect, records /* no duplicates */, rrsets, partial /* an incomplete export */, changeBatch: { upserts, deletes /* a count */ }|null,
  guessed /* the origin is a low-confidence guess */, problems: { errors, warnings } /* parse issues, PARTIAL_EXPORT / RECORDS_TRUNCATED not counted again */ }
DiffRow = { key /* '<relative name>|<TYPE>' */, rel, name /* absolute */, type, status /* DIFF_STATUSES */, reasons /* DIFF_REASONS */, notes /* DIFF_NOTES */,
  a: Side|null, b: Side|null, added: string[] /* values only in b */, removed: string[] /* values only in a */ }
Side = { values: string[] /* the record's text, file order; a Route 53 alias "ALIAS <target>." */, ttl /* the lowest */, ttls, proxied: true|false|'mixed'|null, count }
export function recordSets(zone, { joinTxt, ignoreSoa }) -> Map<key, set> ; export function valueKey(record, { origin, joinTxt, ignoreSoa }) -> string
export function canonicalName(name) -> string ; export function relativeName(name, origin) -> '@' | labels | '<absolute>.'
export function diffFilter(row, filter /* DIFF_FILTERS */) -> boolean ; export function hasDifferences(result) -> boolean
export function diffCsv(rows, { redact }) -> string /* lib/export.js toCsv; DIFF_CSV_COLUMNS; values joined ' | ' */ ; export function diffJson(result, rows, { redact }) -> string
export function diffSummaryFacts(result, { max = 5, formatA, formatB }) -> { a, b /* { origin, format, partial, changeBatch, guessed, problems } */, relative, counts,
  options /* the ones on */, differences: [{ status, name, type, reasons }], more }
export const DIFF_STATUSES = ['added', 'removed', 'changed', 'same', 'ignored'], DIFF_REASONS = ['values', 'ttl', 'proxied', 'routing', 'soa-names', 'soa-serial', 'soa-timers'],
  DIFF_NOTES = ['ttl-ignored', 'txt-split', 'soa-ignored', 'soa-one-side', 'apex-ns', 'not-in-batch', 'batch-delete', 'partial', 'other-variants'], DIFF_DEFAULTS, DIFF_OPTIONS,
  DIFF_FILTERS = ['diff', 'added', 'removed', 'changed', 'same', 'ignored', 'all'], DIFF_CSV_COLUMNS
```
- **Matching:** records by owner and type; each record set is a set of values (order, duplicates and the file format do not matter). The first zone is the starting point: `added` only in the second, `removed` only in the first, `changed` in both with other values, a TTL (unless `ignoreTtl`), a Cloudflare proxy flag (only when both sides have flags: a provider without them never differs) or Route 53 / octoDNS routing (the variants, and the values each holds: values swapped between SetIdentifiers is a change of `routing`, a value changed inside a variant one of `values`).
- **Names** relative to each zone's own origin ('@', 'www', '*.apps'; outside the zone absolute with its dot), in RDATA too (a CNAME target, an MX exchange, an SOA's names …), so a copy under another name compares; lowercase, no trailing dot, `\042` = `*` (the parser has done most of it; an escaped dot is never a label boundary).
- **Values** by lib/zoneparse.js `rdataKey` (IPv6 compressed, MX / SRV fields not spacing, CAA tags lowercase) with: the SOA by every field (`ignoreSoa`: its mname and rname only — the serial, the timers and the SOA's own TTL become the note `soa-ignored`; a changed SOA says which of `soa-names` / `soa-serial` / `soa-timers`), a CAA issuer domain in lowercase, TXT by the bytes of its character-strings (lib/zonetext.js `txtBytes`: a byte that is not UTF-8 never equals its Latin-1 look-alike), joined with `joinTxt` (strings split differently, even inside a character: the note `txt-split`), a Route 53 alias by its target, an undecoded value by its whitespace-collapsed text. With `ignoreSoa` an SOA only one side has (a provider's export leaves it out) is `ignored` (note `soa-one-side`).
- **`ignoreApexNs`:** the NS set at the apex is `ignored` (note `apex-ns`): two providers each name their own.
- **A Route 53 change batch** on either side (lib/zoneparse.js `changeBatch`) is changes, not a zone: a set it does not name is `ignored` (note `not-in-batch`), never removed or added; a set its DELETE names is compared as gone (note `batch-delete`). It names routing variants by SetIdentifier: of the other side's set only those variants are compared (note `other-variants` when it has more; a DELETE of a variant it lacks leaves the set `ignored`). **An incomplete export** (`partial`): a set only the other side has, or a changed set's values it lacks, get the note `partial` (they may be in the part that is missing).
- **Record sets** of a zone object are kept per `joinTxt` / `ignoreSoa` in a WeakMap (made again when its records or origin are other ones): toggling the TTL or apex NS option compares again without reading the zones (20,000 against 20,000 records: about 70 ms instead of 350).
- **Rows** in order: the apex first, then by labels from the right, names outside the zone last; per name SOA, NS, A, AAAA, CNAME, MX, TXT, then the rest alphabetically.
- **Exports** (`diffCsv`, `diffJson`): the absolute name (a spreadsheet takes `@` for a formula), both TTLs, both sides' values, what was added and removed, the proxy flags, reasons and notes; `redact` maps every value list (the view hides proxied origins unless opted in).

Tests: `tests/js/zonediff.test.js` (the BIND zone of `tests/fixtures/zonediff/` against its move to Route 53 — names in another case, a relative target, a decimal-escaped wildcard, IPv6 spellings, CAA case, MX spacing and TXT chunks equal; a new, a gone and a changed set, TTLs, the SOA and the apex NS found — with every option, both directions, proxy flags, two zone names, routing and swapped variants, TXT bytes, a change batch and an incomplete export on either side, the record sets kept per option, filters, exports and summary facts); `tests/e2e/zone.e2e.mjs` (offline: the Cloudflare sample against the Route 53 sample — the rows the library finds, the options from the keyboard, redacted exports, Copy summary without values, Forget —, a one-change batch named after this zone, an incomplete Cloudflare page, a long owner that wraps, at 320 / 375 px).

### 5.67 `lib/zoneconvert.js` — a zone written for another tool or provider (Zone File › Convert)
Pure, synchronous, DOM-free; nothing is sent or stored. Nothing is dropped silently: a record a target cannot hold is left out (BIND and DNSControl keep it as a comment with the reason) and every such case, and every change of meaning, is a pitfall.
```js
export function convertZone(zone /* with an origin, no fatal issue (TypeError) */, target /* CONVERT_TARGETS (RangeError) */) -> {
  target, text, filename /* the first file's */, mime, written /* records (values) in all files */,
  files: [{ filename /* convertFilename */, text, written }] /* one; several change batches for a Route 53 zone over its quotas (batch-split) */,
  pitfalls: [{ code /* PITFALL_CODES */, severity: 'error'|'warn'|'info', count, names /* relative */,
    params: { target /* TARGET_NAMES */, types?, flags?, tags?, keys? /* lists */, ttl?, max?, records?, zone? } }],
  omitted: [{ id, code }] /* left out or commented out */,
  changed: [{ id, code }] /* written differently: 'cname-apex' (as ALIAS), 'alias-record' (an ANAME as ALIAS), 'proxied-mixed' */ }
export function convertFilename(origin, target, part = 0) -> '<zone>.zone' | '<zone>.route53.json' ('<zone>.route53.<part>.json' for one of several) | '<zone>.yaml' | 'dnsconfig.js'
export function pitfallKey(code, target) -> 'zconv.pit.<code>.<target>' (a code in PITFALL_VARIANTS for that target) | 'zconv.pit.<code>' ; export function pitfallKeys() -> string[]
export function route53Name(name) ; export { route53String, yamlString, octodnsTxt, octodnsTxtValue, octodnsTxtRefused, naturalCompare }
                                                    // the escapes, checks and orders of the formats: lib/zonetext.js's (§5.68; lib/fixes.js uses them too)
export const CONVERT_TARGETS = ['bind', 'route53', 'octodns', 'dnscontrol'], TARGET_NAMES, CONVERT_MIME, TARGET_TYPES, TARGET_BY_HAND, DNSSEC_TYPES,
  PSEUDO_TYPES = ['ALIAS', 'ANAME', 'URLFWD'], OCTODNS_SVC_KEYS, ROUTE53_ROUTING = ['weighted', 'latency', 'failover', 'geolocation', 'multivalue'],
  ROUTE53_BATCH_LIMITS = { records: 1000, chars: 32000, upsert: 2, setValues: 400 }, CAA_COMMON_TAGS, CONVERT_DEFAULT_TTL = 3600, ALIAS_ZONE_PLACEHOLDER, DNSCONTROL_PROVIDER = 'main',
  PITFALL_SEVERITY /* code → { target: severity } */, PITFALL_CODES, PITFALL_VARIANTS
```
- **Record types** (`TARGET_TYPES`, what this module writes; CAA, SRV, TLSA, HTTPS and SVCB everywhere): Route 53 its own list (HTTPS, SVCB, SSHFP and TLSA since 2024; DS, NAPTR, SPF); octoDNS its record types that lib/zoneparse.js reads back (DNAME, DS, NAPTR, SSHFP, HTTPS / SVCB since octoDNS 1.8, URI, OPENPGPKEY, SPF); DNSControl its record functions (DNAME, DS, NAPTR, SSHFP, RP, SMIMEA, OPENPGPKEY). A type the target has but this module cannot write from a file (`TARGET_BY_HAND`: LOC and URLFWD for octoDNS, LOC and DHCID for DNSControl — the parser keeps LOC as text) is `by-hand`, never `unsupported-type`. A provider's ALIAS / ANAME is `ALIAS(…)` in DNSControl at any name and an ALIAS record in octoDNS at the apex (`alias-record`); elsewhere a type no target has.
- **BIND:** RFC 1035 with `$ORIGIN` and `$TTL` (the zone's, else the commonest TTL), the SOA first, owners relative (absolute where a relative name would look like a whole domain name), RDATA as the parser prints it, the Cloudflare flags and routing as lib/zoneparse.js `bindComment` writes them; everything the parser decoded or a server reads as text. TXT from its bytes, every character-string at most 255 bytes (a longer one in the source is split, as RFC 1035 requires); HTTPS / SVCB with tls-supported-groups by number, `key9`, as its wire bytes (dnspython 2.8 and DNSControl 5.3 have no name for it: `svc-key`). Commented out with the reason: a CNAME at the apex, a Route 53 alias, a provider pseudo-type, an unreadable value, a duplicate is written once.
- **Route 53:** `{ Comment, Changes: [{ Action: 'UPSERT', ResourceRecordSet }] }` for `aws route53 change-resource-record-sets --change-batch`: one set per name and type (and routing variant of a policy in ROUTE53_ROUTING: SetIdentifier, Weight, Region, Failover, GeoLocation, MultiValueAnswer, HealthCheckId), the lowest TTL of its values, names and RDATA in Route 53's octal escapes, TXT strings from their bytes (at most 255 bytes each) and CAA values by `route53String`, aliases as `AliasTarget` (ALIAS_ZONE_PLACEHOLDER where the source names no hosted zone), tls-supported-groups as `key9`. AWS counts every ResourceRecord (an alias as one) and every character of the Value elements twice for an UPSERT (1,000 and 32,000 a request: 500 and 16,000), so a zone over that is written as several change batches (`files`, `<zone>.route53.1.json` …, `batch-split`), sent one after another: whole record sets in each, filled in order, the aliases last and an alias after any alias it targets (a same-zone alias needs its target first; one set too big for a batch alone is no reason to split); a set no batch takes (over 400 values, or over 16,000 characters of values) is `batch-size` (error). Left out: the SOA and the apex NS (the hosted zone keeps its own), an apex CNAME, DNSSEC records, types Route 53 lacks, names outside the zone.
- **octoDNS:** the zone's YAML (`<zone>.yaml`) as octoDNS 1.22's YamlProvider loads it as written: every mapping in its natural key order (`enforce_order`, the default `order_mode` natural: `naturalCompare` = Python `natsort_keygen()`, runs of digits as whole numbers of any size); a scalar PyYAML would read as a number, a date or a boolean (`0x1f`, `1_000`, `2026-10-02`, `on` …) quoted; `''` for the apex; `value` for one value, `values` for several; a string with a character outside printable ASCII double-quoted with every such character escaped (`\xHH`, `\uHHHH`, `\UHHHHHHHH`: PyYAML refuses C1 controls, U+FFFE and U+FFFF written raw and folds a raw NEL, LS or PS, and octoDNS reads its files in the system code page on Windows — the file is ASCII only); TXT as one text (`octodnsTxtValue`), its raw text with every `;` written `\;` (the provider splits it again), inside one more pair of quotes when it starts with `"` (octoDNS strips the first and last character of such a value as it loads it: `txt-quote-start`); the header says to load the file with `escaped_semicolons: true` (octoDNS warns over the default, deprecated, which becomes false in 2.0, and with false refuses `\;` as "double escaped ;"; `semicolons` lists the TXT values with a `;`); HTTPS / SVCB as `svcpriority`, `targetname`, `svcparams` (lists for mandatory, alpn and the hints, null for a key without a value) with the keys octoDNS has no name for — and `ech`, whose check fails on a valid value in octoDNS 1.8–1.22 — by number, `key<N>`, as their wire bytes in `\DDD` escapes (ech decoded from base64, tls-supported-groups as 16-bit numbers, dohpath as UTF-8: `svc-key`); URI, OPENPGPKEY; `octodns.cloudflare.proxied` (one flag per set) and `auto-ttl`; `octodns.lenient: true` where octoDNS's own checks refuse a record (`txt-lenient`: TXT outside ASCII or a `\` before a `;`; `name-lenient`: SRV / URI not at a `_service._proto` name); an apex CNAME as `type: ALIAS`; a CAA value with a `"` or a `\` flagged (`caa-quote`: octoDNS hands it to a provider between quotes, unescaped). Left out: the SOA, the apex NS, Route 53 aliases, DNSSEC records, types octoDNS lacks, a TXT with `" "` inside (`txt-quote`: octoDNS deletes it as it loads the file), a TXT that is not UTF-8 (`txt-bytes`), a record whose target needs escapes (`escaped-target`: octoDNS refuses the whole file).
- **DNSControl:** `var REG_NONE = NewRegistrar("none"); var DSP_MAIN = NewDnsProvider("main");` then `D("<zone>", REG_NONE, DnsProvider(DSP_MAIN), DefaultTTL(n), A(…), …, END);` — `TTL(n)` where a set differs from the default, `CF_PROXY_ON`, `CAA_CRITICAL`, a TXT value as one string from its UTF-8 text (DNSControl joins a list itself and splits the text again every 255 bytes), HTTPS / SVCB parameters with tls-supported-groups as `key9` and a space inside a value as `\032` (DNSControl splits them at every space, quoted or not), an apex CNAME as `ALIAS("@", …)`, a Route 53 alias as `R53_ALIAS(name, type, target, R53_ZONE(id)[, R53_EVALUATE_TARGET_HEALTH(true)])`, `DISABLE_REPEATED_DOMAIN_CHECK` on a name that repeats the zone name (`repeated-domain`: DNSControl refuses it otherwise); names relative, strings as JSON literals (U+2028 / U+2029 escaped). Comment lines: a CAA tag DNSControl refuses (anything but CAA_COMMON_TAGS — `dnscontrol check` would refuse the whole file), a record whose target needs escapes (`escaped-target`, the same), a TXT that is not UTF-8 (`txt-bytes`), types it has no function for (SPF, URI …) or `by-hand`. Left out: the SOA, the apex NS, DNSSEC records, names outside the zone.
- **Pitfalls** (severity per target): `cname-apex` (BIND warn, Route 53 error, octoDNS / DNSControl info), `cname-alone` (error everywhere: a CNAME next to other records at its name, or two CNAMEs — every target refuses it; its RRSIG / NSEC may stay), `r53-alias`, `alias-zone-id`, `alias-record`, `proxied` (Route 53 warn: the origin becomes public), `proxied-mixed` (octoDNS), `cname-flatten`, `txt-long` (how each format splits a TXT over 255 bytes), `txt-split` (octoDNS and DNSControl split the text again), `txt-bytes` (octoDNS / DNSControl: not UTF-8), `txt-quote` / `txt-quote-start` / `semicolons` (octoDNS), `caa-flags` (not 0 / 128), `caa-tag` (not in CAA_COMMON_TAGS; DNSControl warn, as a comment), `caa-quote` (octoDNS), `svc-key` (every target: octoDNS its keys by number, the others tls-supported-groups), `txt-lenient`, `name-lenient`, `unsupported-type`, `by-hand`, `unreadable`, `dnssec`, `routing` (variants merged where the target has no place for them; a CNAME keeps its first), `out-of-zone`, `wildcard-inner`, `escaped-name`, `escaped-target` (octoDNS / DNSControl), `repeated-domain` (DNSControl), `no-soa` (BIND), `soa`, `apex-ns`, `wildcard`, `duplicate`, `ttl-mixed` (the set gets the lowest), `ttl-default`, `batch-split` (Route 53: several change batches), `batch-size` (Route 53 error: a set no change batch takes). They list errors first, then warnings, then notes, each with its record names.
- **Round trip:** the BIND, Route 53 and octoDNS files parse back with lib/zoneparse.js into the same record sets lib/zonediff.js compares (octoDNS with `joinTxt`), and `dnsconfig.js` run against stub DNSControl functions does too (with `joinTxt`), less `omitted`, with `changed` as said, routing merged where flagged. Checked by hand against the tools themselves (2026-10-02, again after the review): every fixture's octoDNS file loads in octoDNS 1.22's YamlProvider, every `dnsconfig.js` passes `dnscontrol check` (5.3.0) and every BIND file loads in dnspython 2.8, but for a source whose own CNAME is not alone (`cname-alone`); on zones of hostile names, values and every record type, the BIND and Route 53 records equal dnspython's reading of the source byte for byte (a zone of 1,200 A and 40 DKIM records as four change batches within the quotas), and octoDNS's and DNSControl's too but for what the notes say (`txt-split`, `txt-bytes`, `txt-quote`, `escaped-target`, `caa-quote`, `caa-tag`, `caa-flags`) and a CAA value outside ASCII, which dnspython itself reads as Latin-1.

Tests: `tests/js/zoneconvert.test.js` — the goldens of `tests/fixtures/zoneconvert/gen-convert-golden.mjs` (a zone with every pitfall, a Cloudflare export, Route 53 JSON with aliases and routing, octoDNS YAML, each in every format with its pitfalls; `--write` after a deliberate change), the round trip of every zone fixture in BIND, Route 53 and octoDNS through the parser, and of `dnsconfig.js` run against stub DNSControl functions, octoDNS's HTTPS / SVCB parameters (by number as wire bytes), key order (digits as numbers past 2^53), quoting and TXT quotes, TXT bytes and 255-byte strings, Route 53's quotas and its several change batches, escaped targets, and each pitfall per target; `tests/e2e/zone.e2e.mjs` (offline: every format of the sample, its notes, the download read back, Copy all; a zone of 600 records as two Route 53 change batches, each downloaded and copied).

### 5.68 `lib/zonetext.js` — the text helpers the zone formats share
Pure, synchronous, DOM-free, no imports. lib/zoneconvert.js writes whole zones with them and lib/fixes.js a change request's entries, so the change request, its check page and "Show the fix" never load the zone converter.
```js
export function charStringBytes(text) -> Uint8Array[]      // a TXT presentation text's character-strings as bytes: quoted or bare, \DDD a byte, \X the character, else UTF-8
export function txtBytes(record) -> Uint8Array[]           // a TXT / SPF record's strings: from its text (exact), else its data as UTF-8
export function joinBytes(list) -> Uint8Array ; export function utf8Text(bytes) -> string|null /* not UTF-8 */ ; export function split255(bytes) -> Uint8Array[]
export function route53String(s | Uint8Array) -> string    // quoted, `"` and `\` escaped, every byte outside printable ASCII as Route 53's octal escape
export function yamlString(s) -> string                    // plain, single-quoted, or double-quoted with every character outside printable ASCII escaped (PyYAML reads it back)
export function octodnsTxt(data) -> string                 // the joined text, every `;` as `\;` and nothing else escaped
export function octodnsTxtValue(data) -> string|null       // octodnsTxt in one more pair of quotes when it starts with one; null with `" "` inside
export function octodnsTxtRefused(escaped) -> boolean      // octoDNS's chunked-value check refuses it (outside ASCII, `\` before `;`): written lenient
export function naturalCompare(a, b) -> number             // octoDNS's natural key order (Python natsort_keygen(), digits as whole numbers of any size)
```
Tests: `tests/js/zonetext.test.js` (YAML escapes read back by the parser's YAML subset, bytes of a presentation text, Route 53 bytes, the octoDNS TXT value); the formats through `tests/js/zoneconvert.test.js` and `tests/js/fixes.test.js`.

### 5.61 `lib/topology.js`, `ui/topology.js` — where TLS really terminates
The inventory's topology keys (§5.5) say where TLS ends: a load balancer and the servers behind it, the backends that never get the certificate, the address an HA pair shares, the public address of a server behind NAT. SSL Targets, its Renewal plan, the Verify pairs, the CSV exports, targets.txt and the CLI (§7) follow them; an inventory without them works exactly as before. `lib/topology.js` is DOM-free and pure; it loads with the scanner (`ENGINE_MODULES`), off the start route.
```js
export function inventoryTopology(servers) -> { any, lbs: [{ server, backends: Server[] }], backendOf: Map<Server, Server[]>, vips: [{ ip, servers: Server[] }],
                                                nats: [{ ip, server }], plain: Server[] /* terminates_tls=no */, ported: Server[] /* with ports= */ }
  // any: the inventory has a topology key at all (false: nothing changes anywhere)
export function applyTopology(groups, servers) -> groups   // §5.12: the backends a reached load balancer forwards to (entries with `lbs`); `topology` on the groups it concerns
GroupTopology = { terminatesTls, backends: [{ id, name, terminatesTls }], behind: string[] /* the load balancers of the scan it was reached through */,
  vips: [{ ip, servers: string[] /* every holder of a VIP a name answered with */ }], nats: [{ ip, addresses: string[] /* its own */ }], tlsPorts: number[] }
export function orderByLoadBalancer(groups) -> groups      // each load balancer (a VIP pair together) followed by what is behind it; the rest stays in order
export function topologyNotes(t) -> string[]               // the English CSV text: 'load balancer for web01, web02', 'behind lb01, lb02 (plain HTTP, no certificate)',
                                                           // 'behind lb01 (re-encrypts)', 'VIP 203.0.113.50 (lb01, lb02)', 'NAT 203.0.113.10 -> 10.0.0.30', 'TLS ports 443,8443'
export const TOPOLOGY_CSV_COLUMN                           // { key: 'topology', header: 'Topology', get } over rows carrying a GroupTopology
// ui/topology.js (h() only; styles in assets/css/views/topology.css, injected with SSL Targets and Servers)
export function TopologyNotes(topology) -> HTMLElement|null       // one line per fact, the action first (below)
export function noCertStatus(group) -> 'plain'|'passthrough'|null // terminates_tls=no: plain HTTP, or a load balancer passing TLS through to its backends
export function TopologyCard(servers) -> HTMLElement|null         // the Servers view's card; null without a topology key
```
- A load balancer that terminates TLS gets the certificate (its status says so: "Install the certificate"; its note "Load balancer → web01, web02"). Its backends say "Behind lb01, lb02 — plain HTTP, no certificate needed" (`terminates_tls=no`: status "No certificate needed", never counted as needing it) or "Behind lb01, lb02 — re-encrypts: install here too". A load balancer with `terminates_tls=no` passes TLS through (status "TLS passthrough"): its backends get the certificate.
- Every holder of a VIP gets it: "VIP 203.0.113.50 — install on both: lb01 and lb02" ("on all N" for more), and the holders are listed together. A name answered at a NAT address says "Reached at 203.0.113.10 (NAT) → 10.0.0.30"; for it the Verify tab's CLI card scans the server's own addresses (`cliTargets`). A server's own TLS ports: "TLS ports 443, 8443".
- SSL Targets › Servers (§6): an intro line, the notes under each server's name, a backend indented under its load balancer; each name says which load balancers it came through ("Through lb01, lb02" — both of a VIP pair), the details also VIP / NAT. The Servers CSV and the Renewal plan's work list end with a `Topology` column only when a row has a topology.
- The Renewal plan (§5.43): the server cells carry the notes; the servers with `terminates_tls=no` are in no row but listed under the matrix ("1 server the names reach needs no certificate"), and the renewal line does not count them.
- The Servers view (§6): the card shows each load balancer with its backends as a tree (does each get the certificate?), the VIPs with their holders, the NAT pairs, the servers outside a load balancer that never get the certificate and the servers with their own TLS ports (each section's key as written, never upper-cased).
- targets.txt (the Servers view, SSL Targets) writes the keys the CLI reads (`inventory.topologyTokens`; `ports=` as ip:port tokens).

Tests: `tests/js/topology.test.js` (a scan through an emulated DoH zone: the matches through the VIP and the NAT address, the backend groups, `lbs`, the order, the Renewal plan and its work list, the Servers CSV, the Verify pairs, targets.txt read back by the CLI's test), `tests/js/inventory.test.js` (the keys in every format, the shared fixture), the topology group of `tests/e2e/scan.e2e.mjs` (§8).

## 6. UI (views)

Hash routing `#/<view>?param=...` (shareable: e.g. `#/lookup?name=example.com&type=MX`, `#/global?name=www.example.com&type=A`); a view may have one-word sub-pages, `#/<view>/<page>?…` (`parseRoute().sub`, `ctx.sub`; `setParams` and `shareUrl` keep it; a sub-page never brings a kept result back): `#/change/check`. Navigation groups (`lib/shellnav.js` NAV_GROUPS, §5.29; a view's `group` in app.js `VIEWS`): Discover (subdomains, domain, zone), Certificates (scan, cert, renew, estate), DNS tools (global, lookup, bulk, change), IP addresses (ip, ptr, retire), Mail & domain (health, reports, portfolio), Setup & info (inventory, about; “Workspace” names the customer workspaces, §5.39); a view with an unknown group is listed under "More tools". Views load lazily; when a view's module fails to load because the page belongs to an earlier deploy (`confirmStaleModule` in app.js: a missing export, or a failed fetch while the browser is online and this page's own `v/<commit>/assets/js/app.js` answers 404), the page body offers **Reload page**. Offline or on a dropped connection the same fetch failure keeps the plain network error with Retry and never claims an update. A stale DoH / Globalping module, or a scan whose wordlist tier, locale pack or engine module failed to load in a page whose `v/<commit>/` is gone, shows the same hint once as a toast. Every "Reload page" goes through `ui/pwa.js` `reloadPage()` (a waiting service worker takes over first, §5.35).

**Loading.** `index.html` links `style.css` only and modulepreloads app.js's static graph. `VIEWS[]` (app.js) gives each view its stylesheets (`css`, paths under `assets/css/`): they are injected as `<link rel="stylesheet">` resolved against app.js (so they follow `v/<commit>/`), before the view mounts, kept for the page's life and ordered by `VIEW_CSS_ORDER` whatever the visiting order; a sheet that cannot load leaves the view unstyled, never unusable, and a view whose module fails shows its message only once the stylesheets have settled too.
