The discovery engine (`ENGINE_MODULES`: scanner, sources, doh, dnswire, permute) is imported when a scan starts and modulepreloaded once the browser is idle on Subdomains and SSL Targets (`VIEWS[].preload`; not offline or with Save-Data); `lib/ipintel.js` loads on the first "Look up owner". A module loaded on first use that fails to load (`views/subdomains.js` `loadOnFirstUse`, behind `runScanner` and `networkOwner`) calls `ctx.checkOutdated` first, so a tab left open across a deploy offers the reload instead of only the failed scan or lookup. zoneparse and x509 load with the views that need them (Zone File; Certificate / SSL Targets). `tests/js/start-route.test.js` holds the start route (index.html, boot.js, style.css, app.js's graph, the Subdomains graph and stylesheet) under a gzip budget of 370 KB (≈ 365 KB once the Copy summary builders and texts of every view but the start view moved to `lib/summary.js`, which loads with the first view that has one — `ui/view-summaries.js`, §5.32 —, with `lib/summarycore.js` left on the start route; ≈ 379 KB before that with all of wave 4: the request meter and log, which count from the first request, and the senders' notes, §5.54, the new tools' navigation entries, FileDrop's `maxFiles`, one-decimal shares and a view's sub-pages, DMARC & TLS reports' own builder already loading with its view — `lib/reportsummary.js` —; ≈ 368 KB with wave 3's Copy summary builders of Renewal readiness, Retire an IP and the Domain overview and their navigation entries; ≈ 359 KB with the customer workspaces: the workspace store, its first-run migration and IndexedDB backend and the header switcher load before the first view, which reads its inventory at mount; ≈ 334 KB with wave 2's shell, page session, Copy summary, job progress and Subdomains tabs; ≈ 258 KB before them, ≈ 368 KB before the lazy engine) and keeps the heavy modules out of it.

**Installable app and offline.** The Pages bundle registers `sw.js` (§5.35) and links a web app manifest in the UI language, so browsers offer to install DomainScope; the development checkout registers nothing. With the worker in control every view opens offline. Certificate (parsing, the sample), Certificate estate (the CLI's reports), Zone File (import, lint, origins, the command), the DNS change request (the form, its checks and every output; its Read and the check page say in place what waits for a connection), DMARC & TLS reports (reading and classifying the reports; the SPF line says it was not checked), Servers and About need no network (`VIEWS[].offline`); offline, every other view shows a note between its header and body ("You are offline", what it needs the network for, links to the tools that work offline), and `ctx.requireOnline()` — called by every network action (Scan, Look up, Check, Run, CT Load, CAA / CT / DANE checks, the Live check, Verify, the MTA-STS check, owner and reverse-IP lookups) — says so in a toast and sends nothing (one such toast at a time: another click replaces it rather than stacking copies). A check a view starts by itself (the Certificate view's CAA check, and its CT search for a public CA, when their tab opens) calls `ctx.requireOnline({ quiet: true })`: offline it sends nothing and the panel says so in place (`cert.caa.offline` / `cert.ct.offline`, `data-offline="auto"`), without the toast — the user clicked nothing; the panel's button then runs it (and warns) as usual. A run on arrival (Lookup, Global DNS, Domain Health, IP Intel: `start({ auto: true })`) — a shared link, or a link from another view that carries params, such as a host name's DNS Lookup link — is quiet the same way: offline it fills the form and sends nothing, and the page's note says why. A click on the view's own button, or a new query on the view already open (`update()`), still gets the toast. Toasts stay at four on screen: past that the oldest timed one goes, never a sticky one (timeout 0, such as "Update ready — Reload"). A new deploy is announced by "Update ready — Reload". About › Privacy says what the worker keeps and what it never caches. Views:
0. **subdomains** "Subdomains" (**default route**, `#/subdomains?domain=…[&tab=overview|hosts|origins|sources]`; the brand link and About's Start button open it): domain(s) in, every discoverable subdomain out. Options: wordlist level **Off / Small / Smart (recommended, default) / Large / Huge** with exact counts (`wordlistInfo()`), the locale packs the typed domain gets and time estimates (candidates ÷ 120 queries/s at the full sweep width of 24, scaled down for a lower Settings value), and a plan line (≈ queries per domain, custom / learned / pack shares, per-domain caps); under Advanced options: languages / markets (auto from the domain ending, a manual pack list, or none; remembered), custom wordlist (textarea + `.txt` file, accepted / rejected counts from `parseCustomWordlist`, this tab only, Clear), learned names (opt-in switch, off by default, with the count, Forget; never used at Off), permutations on/off with a budget (500 / 1,500 / 5,000; the switch also drives the recursive round), origin hints on/off, passive sources with quota notes, include expired, extra hostnames, DoH chain. A shared link (`run=1`) pre-fills the domain and asks before scanning. While running: a progress bar whose label names the stage, and stage pills in `SCAN_STAGES` order with per-stage counts. Results in four tabs (§5.31 `lib/subtabs.js`) under the run's header card (title, time, progress bar, the zone-file banner, the cancelled / failed notice), each tab label with a live count: **Overview** — the stat cards (a click filters the host table and opens Hosts with the keyboard focus on its tab), the summary alerts (nothing found, sources that failed with a link to Sources, dangling CNAMEs, the Cloudflare count with a link to Origins, wildcard parents, scanner warnings; the tab counts the warnings and errors), a technique bar ("how the names were found": DNS records / wordlist / permutations / deeper level / passive sources), a wordlist usage line from `options.wordlist` (served level, packs, custom / learned tried vs found, fallback) and a CTA that opens SSL Targets for the same domain; **Hosts** — copy all names / resolving only / `names.txt` / CSV / JSON and the host table (name → DNS Lookup link, origin badges, classification, IPs; on a phone each row is a card in which a host name wraps only after a dot — `hostNameNodes`: one unbreakable run per label, a `<wbr>` between — or inside a label too long for the line on its own, and an IP never breaks); **Sources** — the stage pills, the per-source status (`sourceHealthSummary`, quota explanations; a screen reader hears each new status line and the “still fetching” note from the run's header whichever tab is open) and the free limit of each source asked, then **Related domains in the same certificates** (§5.56, a run that reads CT); **Origins** — for proxied hosts an **Origin** panel ("Origin servers behind the proxy"), or why there is none (candidates are looked for at the end of the scan, no host hides its origin, the scan did not finish): origin networks (/24 · /48) with their DNS-only hosts, per proxied host its resolver-leak answers ("answered by <resolver>") and history ("seen by <source> · last <date>"), read from the structured reason fields, other SPF / MX / sibling hints, and the sweep command for **Linux / macOS** or **Windows PowerShell** (built from `cliTargets` / `cliNames` with `buildSweepCommand`, dropped entries counted; over 200 names it reads `-n proxied-names.txt`, offered as a download next to the command, and a target list still too long for one line reads `-t proxied-targets.txt`, offered the same way) with copy and a download link for the CLI. Hosts is the automatic tab once a host is listed (Sources before that while the run is live, Overview when it ended empty); an automatic move waits while the keyboard focus is inside the tabs (a focused tab, a tapped panel) and happens once the focus leaves them. A tab the user picks (a click, also on the tab already shown, ←/→/Home/End with a roving tabindex, a stat card, a link) stays for that run, goes into the URL as `tab=` next to `domain` (replaceState; after a return through the nav link, which has no `domain`, the run's domains are written with it) and comes back after a language switch or a visit to another view (the page session keeps it too); a new scan starts on the automatic tab and drops `tab=`, and an edited `tab=` opens that tab without a re-mount (`update`). Every panel is built up front, so a live run fills the hidden ones as well. At ≤ 480 px the four tabs share the row in equal columns with the count under the label.
   Later additions:
   - **Live rows:** while the wordlist, permutation and recursive stages run, `onFound` hits appear at once as "resolving…" rows and are replaced by the full record at the resolve stage.
   - **Stage pills:** they show which passive sources were still fetching when the sweep started (`sourcesStillRunning`).
   - **Plan line:** a query range from `estimateQueries`.
   - **Origin panel:**
     - per proxied host, its `originCandidates` in order: zone file, resolver leak, sibling domain ("same name as <sibling>"), history, then only the related networks;
     - per network, whether the command sweeps the whole /24 or only its addresses (and why), a **shared hosting / cloud** badge with a warning, and **Look up owner** (`describeNetwork`, one RIPEstat request on click);
     - an **Exclude addresses** box (→ `buildSweepCommand({ exclude })`). It is kept per run for the page session, so it survives a re-mount. The JSON export's `origin.cliSuggestion` is the panel's POSIX command with these exclusions (`originExport`), and `origin.exclude` reports what they did;
     - a tip to scan sister domains together;
     - on each IPv4 network, **Reverse DNS sweep**: a link to `#/ptr?target=<network>&focus=<registrable domain of its first host>` that only fills the Reverse DNS form (the user presses Sweep); on a shared cloud / hosting network (whose other addresses are other customers') **Reverse DNS of its N addresses**, with only the network's own IPv4 addresses as the target.
   - **Names from a reverse DNS sweep** (`state.session.namesScanIntent` = `{ v: 1, target: 'subdomains', source: 'ptr', names, domains, label, mode: 'exact', at }`, one-shot, memory only, honoured for `NAMES_INTENT_MAX_AGE` = 60 s): `namesFromIntent` re-validates the names (they come from PTR records; at most `NAMES_HANDOFF_MAX` = 4,000), the box gets the domains, and a chip ("N names from the reverse DNS sweep of <label>", `NamesChip`) offers Scan exactly these names (default) / Include in discovery / Leave out (`ZONE_MODES`), Back to Reverse DNS and Remove. Nothing starts by itself. Exact mode (`handoffScanOverrides`) runs `runScan({ extraNames: typed + names, exact: true, sources: [], bruteforce: 'off', … })` for one run (never stored), the plan line says so, and the results carry a banner; discover adds the names as extra names. The names apply only to a scan of their own domains (`handoffForDomains(handoff, domains)`: the names equal to or under a typed domain, like the Zone File chip's `zoneForDomains`): with another domain in the box the chip says "Not used for the domains in the box: these names are under <domains>", with some of them how many this scan uses, and the plan line, exact mode and the extra names follow. The chip stays for the page session until removed ("Delete all local data" drops it, whether or not the view is mounted).
   - **Zone File "Scan now" during a scan:** the zone scan waits for the running one (`zoneStartAction`). A prompt offers **Cancel it and scan the zone** or **Don't start**. Leaving the page drops the request; the box and the zone mode stay filled in.
0a. **domain** "Domain overview" (`#/domain?name=…`; §5.46): one page per domain. A route — a shared link, a carried target (`run=0`), a nav link — only fills the box (a host name is reduced to its registrable domain, and the results header says so) with "Opened from a link: press Build overview … Nothing has been sent yet."; nothing is sent before **Build overview** (Ctrl/⌘+Enter; Esc stops). The seven cards fill as their lookups land (a spinner while pending), each with **Open in <tool>** carrying the name: Registration → Domain Health (registrar with IANA id, registered, expires with a days-left badge, the transfer lock — "Off" explains `clientTransferProhibited` —, the status flags, DNSSEC delegation, the registry's name servers; a TLD without RDAP says so and links the registry's WHOIS: TRABİS, DENIC, JPRS, SWITCH, else the TLD's IANA page), DNS hosting → DNS Lookup (provider badges, "name servers of N providers" when several, the registry's delegation when it differs from the zone, SOA primary / serial with its date / contact, DNSSEC), Mail → Domain Health (the MX platform — a gateway, forwarding or sending service says which —, MX hosts, SPF and DMARC in one line each, the services SPF names), Web → Global DNS (apex and www with their `KindBadge`, addresses linking to IP Intel, "alias of", the HTTPS records with their ALPN), Certificates → the Certificate view's "No file?" box (filled, `run=0`) (the CAs CAA allows, restricted ones marked, the issuewild values on their own row; "any CA" when CAA has no issue property, and an error when an unknown tag is marked critical — read as Domain Health reads CAA, so it never contradicts the CT verdicts; **Look up issuers**: one Cert Spotter request, on the click only, the issuers of the current certificates with CAA's verdict — "not allowed by CAA" warns that the next renewal will fail, naming the cause: a critical unknown tag, or the issue property when only issuewild names CAs (`ctNoteKey`), else CAA), SaaS verifications → DNS Lookup › TXT (vendor badges with a count; the tokens are never shown), Health → Domain Health (traffic-light dot, score, counts, the worst three problems). A lookup that failed is "⚠ n/a" with the reason in its fields (`lookupStatus`, §5.36) and the card's **Retry** asks only its failed lookups again, past the DNS cache, at once even while the rest of the build runs (Stop ends the build, not a Retry); the keyboard focus stays on the card (a card redrawn under it keeps it, and so does Copy summary when the head is redrawn as a Retry lands), the answers on screen stay ("Updating…") until the new ones land, and a DNS Retry runs the health checks again on the cache. A stopped build keeps what landed; each card not looked up says so with **Look up** (`RetryButton({ first: true })`: its accessible name and tooltip say "Look up" too). Web and Certificates say NXDOMAIN like DNS, Mail and SaaS for a domain that does not exist (Certificates still offers the CT lookup). **Copy summary** (§5.32 `domainSummary`) and **Copy link** share the overview on screen (`name`); the print stylesheet hides the form and the card links and prints one card per block. The finished overview is kept for the page session (`result()` / `snapshot()`, CT issuers included). At ≤ 600 px each card's links sit on their own row under the title; at ≤ 1100 px the cards are one column.
0b. **zone** "Zone File" (`#/zone[?tab=overview|records|origins|problems|live|parity|compare|convert]`; only `tab=` ever goes in the URL):
   - **Import:** drop, choose or paste, with format / dialect auto-detection. The zone name override and a Confirm step apply when the name is only guessed; there are 3 built-in samples and "How do I export my zone?".
   - **Fetch from deSEC or DigitalOcean** (`ui/zone-fetch.js`, a disclosure in the importer; §5.61): provider, zone name and the API token in a password field (`autocomplete="off"`, password managers told to ignore it). Nothing is sent before Fetch zone; the field is read once and emptied at that moment, and the token then lives only in the running fetch. Offline (Zone File itself works offline) Fetch zone reads nothing and says the work needs the network (`ctx.requireOnline`): the token stays in its field for when the connection is back. The privacy line says where it goes, that it is never saved or logged and that the field is emptied; the advice and two links per provider say how to make a read-only token (deSEC: a policy without write permission; DigitalOcean: Custom scopes › `domain:read`). Progress counts record sets / records and requests; a 429 shows the wait; Stop imports nothing. Errors name the provider, the HTTP status (a 401's causes per provider), the provider's own words and the permission a 403 lacks, and say the field was emptied whenever a token had been pasted (a bad zone name too). The panel keeps the keyboard focus through every repaint: on the same control, on Stop while a fetch runs (progress updates only the status line), on the token field after a fetch error, on the zone field after a bad name, on Fetch zone after Stop. A fetched listing is imported like a dropped one with the zone name confirmed, so lint, origins, the live check and New name servers all work on it. Only the provider and the zone typed are kept for the page session, never the token; Forget, another workspace, "Delete all local data" and leaving the view stop a running fetch.
   - **Summary bar:** a format badge and counts ("39 records · 26 names · 10 proxied"), with Forget. Alerts are pinned for an incomplete export and a mostly-internal zone.
   - **Overview:** stat cards and next steps:
     - Scan these names: exact mode or discovery, with "Leave out names that look internal", on by default;
     - Sweep the real origins;
     - Find certificate targets;
     - Compare with live DNS: shows the planned query count;
     - Moving to another DNS provider?: opens the New name servers tab;
     - Another copy, or another provider?: opens the Compare or the Convert tab.
   - **Records:** type groups, proxied only, search, CSV / JSON, copy names; an expanded row shows its comment, tags and problems.
   - **Origins & servers:** one row per proxied name (`proxiedOriginMap`), a by-address table (`addressMap`; both re-matched when the Servers list changes) and the sweep command (`zoneSweep` + `cmdline.buildSweepCommand` with the zone opt-ins). The command has a proxied-only / everything scope, POSIX / PowerShell, an "at least N TLS handshakes" estimate and the two-file form with `zone-names.txt` / `zone-targets.txt` downloads. It uses exact tokens only, never a /24.
   - **Problems:** parse issues and lint findings, errors first; a click jumps to the record.
   - **Live check** (`planDrift` / `driftZone`): nothing is sent until you click Check.
     - The card shows the record sets, the query count, the resolvers and what is sent.
     - Internal names are skipped by default, and wildcard probing is optional.
     - Progress, Cancel and Re-run; the check keeps running while you are on another view.
     - Banners: serial, name servers, a zone that does not exist. Status chips filter the rows.
     - CSV / JSON exports redact origins unless opted in, also inside a value (an SPF `ip4:` / `ip6:` / `a:` term, an MX or SRV target).
   - **New name servers** (`ui/parity-panel.js` over §5.52; confirmed zone name only): the new provider's name servers (issues under the box: not a server, a private address or a public IPv6 one — the CLI command below carries it —, more than 8, one of the file's own servers), what to ask ('first': every record set on the first server, the SOA serial on the others; 'all': every record set everywhere, disabled past the cap), look for extra records at the apex and www (shown only when the zone has something to ask), skip internal-looking names, and the plan line ("32 record sets → 35 Globalping probes (at most 100 per check)", the record sets of types Globalping cannot ask, the capped note). **Compare** goes through `gateProbes` (purpose `'ns-parity'`, its own privacy text — a second one when the internal-name skip is off, saying those names are sent —, again above 50 probes) and `runParity`; keyboard focus moves from Compare to Stop while it runs and back after it (or after Stop); the progress follows the tab on screen (a return to Zone File during a run mounts a new one); the job belongs to the Zone File module (it keeps running on another tab or view, and a toast offers the result, worded from the verdict — "ready, nothing to fix" only for a finished clean run, "N problems to fix", "stopped, N record sets not compared" after a stop (a warning), "N record sets not compared" for a partial one —; Forget / a new import / another workspace stop and drop it — `stopParity` marks the holder dropped: no result, announcement or toast for the zone that is gone —; a new import keeps the servers typed in). Results: the verdict (a `check` one adds the record sets it left to the CLI), a warning when proxied records are answered with their origins, serials out of step, each server (role, state, serial, where the probe asked from, the measurement link), status chips, the table (status, name — with the server under it when several were compared —, type, the file's and the new values as a diff with a highlighted "TTL file → new" line where the TTL differs, the note; six columns that fit a 1280 px screen) with CSV / JSON exports that redact origins unless opted in and add the server and both TTLs of every row (export-only columns, `DataTable` column `display: false`); labelled cards below 640 px. **The move, step by step** (`parityRunbook`, before and after a run) and **Every record, from your own machine (CLI)**: the zone as canonical BIND text (`toBindText`, `<zone>.parity.zone`), `cli/dns_parity.py` and the command for Linux / macOS or PowerShell (opened by itself when the check was capped or left record sets out).
   - **Compare** (`ui/zone-tools.js` over §5.66, loaded on the first of the two tabs with a spinner until then; modulepreloaded when the view is idle; styles in `zonetools.css`): a second zone by drop, file, paste (Compare, Ctrl/Cmd+Enter in the box) or one of the samples, in any format Zone File reads; its zone name is the one typed for it, else the file's own, else this zone's when the file names none or only guesses one from its records (and a change batch's DELETE names) and every name in it fits under this zone's (`readOther`: a Route 53 change batch of one `www` record is no zone named `www`); a new first zone reads the kept second file again. The second zone is held on the view's module session like the first (a new import or analysis of the first keeps it and compares again; "Forget the second zone" drops it alone; Forget, another workspace and "Delete all local data" drop both). Shown: the other zone (name, format, records, files) and what the parser said of it — a name guessed from its records (type it if it is wrong), an incomplete export (the "This export is incomplete" alert with its issue: a set missing from it shows as removed, though it may only be in the part that is missing), a Route 53 change batch (changes, not a whole zone: how many sets it creates or updates and deletes; only those are compared, the rest of this zone shows as ignored, "not in the change batch", never as removed), its parse problems (errors and warnings, the first 20 under a disclosure) —; when this zone's own file is a change batch or incomplete, the result says so too; a note when the two zone names differ, the headline ("N differences: a added, r removed, c changed; s record sets the same", ignored ones counted) with **Copy summary** (§5.32 `zoneCompareSummary`: names and types, never a value, and what the bar says of either file; a bare `#/zone` link; its group named “Summary of the comparison”, apart from the zone’s own), the four options (TTL differences, TXT strings split differently, the SOA serial and timers, NS at the apex; the keyboard focus stays on a toggled one), the legend ("added" is only in the other zone), status chips (Differences — the default —, Added, Removed, Changed, Same, Ignored, All) and the table (status, name — wrapping, so a long owner keeps the notes on the card —, type, this zone's values, the other zone's with + / − lines, the note: reasons, what an option hid, a change batch's or an incomplete export's note, with both TTLs and proxy flags; labelled cards below 640 px). CSV / JSON exports (`diffCsv` / `diffJson`, the rows the filter and search show) hide the origin addresses of either zone's proxied records unless "Include origin addresses in exports" is on.
   - **Convert** (`ui/zone-tools.js` over §5.67; confirmed zone name only): the format (BIND zone file, Route 53 change batch, octoDNS YAML, DNSControl), what it is for, how many records are written and how many are not written as they are, the notes (each pitfall worded for the target, with its record names and count, errors first), **Download** (`<zone>.zone`, `<zone>.route53.json`, `<zone>.yaml`, `dnsconfig.js`), **Copy all** — for a Route 53 zone over AWS's quotas the change batches instead, listed in the order to send them, each with its records, Download and Copy (named by its file for a screen reader) —, the reminder that the file holds every record, origins included, and a preview (the first 400 lines of the first file, and which file it is). Nothing is sent.
   - **Hand-off contracts:**
     - `state.session.zone` = `zoneScanInput(...)` + `{ label, counts, records, internalNames, originInternal }` (`records`: §5.22 `referenceRecords`; `originInternal`: the origin looks internal by its name, `looksInternalName`; `internalNames`: every name `privateLookingNames` flags, whatever the "leave out names that look internal" toggle put in `names` — Retire an IP reads both and never sends one of those names). It is published only once the zone name is confirmed, and cleared by Forget and "Delete all local data".
     - `state.session.zoneScanIntent` = `{ v: 1, target: 'subdomains'|'scan', domain, mode: 'exact'|'discover', autostart, at }`, one-shot. Exact mode maps to `runScan({ zone, exact: true })` and is never written to the stored options.
   - **Privacy:** nothing about the zone (or a second one compared with it) is persisted (no storage key, no URL data), and no network request is made before a click; Compare and Convert make none at all.
1. **scan** "SSL Targets": one requirement line above the steps — "A certificate or at least one domain is required" — turns into a check once met (`formProgress`, §5.27; no step is labelled optional), and a completed step shows a check in place of its number (plus "(done)" in its heading for screen readers). A loaded certificate the scan cannot use — a CA certificate, or one without DNS names — leaves step 1 open with a warning sign, a warn badge ("CA certificate" / "no DNS names") and "(needs attention: …)" in its heading; while nothing else meets the requirement the line adds "This certificate has no DNS names: enter at least one domain.", and Start's error says the same instead of "load a certificate"; steps — (1) drop/paste certificate → shows parsed summary (a PKCS#12 bundle asks for its password first and adds its note with the key check and Download fullchain.pem under the summary, as in the Certificate view below; a lone server certificate gets the Certificate view's "Missing intermediate found" note with Download fullchain.pem — not the root-store warnings — for one certificate, not in a renewal of several); without a file, the same "No file?" block as the Certificate view (a host name's certificate from CT, or **Try a sample**); loading either only fills step 2 like a dropped file and never starts a scan; a CT certificate carries the caveat and points at the Verify tab, which checks what each server really serves; a scan that ends while a lookup still runs hands the busy flag back to it (a language switch waits for the lookup instead of aborting it); (2) target domain(s) (auto-filled from cert via registrableDomain); (3) inventory (uses shared inventory from state; link to inventory view); (4) options, collapsed into one Disclosure line (the Subdomains › Advanced options component, its summary an `<h2>` via `Disclosure({ heading: 2 })` like the other step titles) whose summary lists what differs from the defaults (`optionChanges`) or "recommended defaults"; its open state lasts for the page session, and an invalid extra hostname opens it (sources checkboxes w/ notes about quotas, include expired, wordlist Off/Small/Smart/Large/Huge (default Smart) + permutations, DoH chain; a vocabulary line shows the languages, custom wordlist and learned names shared with Subdomains › Advanced options, with a link there — SSL Targets has no separate controls for them, but records learned labels after its scans too); a run bar with Start/Cancel, the plan line (≈ DNS queries and time; the zone's names in exact zone mode) and a summary — in the flow on wide screens; at ≤ 1100 px it is `position: sticky; bottom: 0` inside the form (edge to edge at ≤ 900 px, side safe-area insets; while it floats (`barStuck` → `data-stuck`) a shadow and the bottom safe-area inset, none of it at rest; no transition with reduced motion), rests at the form's end so it never hides the last field, and the view publishes its height as `--scan-runbar-h` on the root for `scroll-padding-bottom` so a focused field is never behind it (at ≤ 600 px Start goes full width and the summary gives way to the plan line); Run/Cancel with per-source status + progress (the keyboard focus moves from Start to Cancel and back; a cancelled run keeps its streamed hits in Hosts, and the hosts CSV / names.txt exports include them, with certificate coverage). Results: stat cards; tabs Hosts / Servers / Behind CDN / Verify / DANE / Sources / CT: **Hosts** (table: name, origins, status/classification badge incl. 🟠 Cloudflare, IPs, CNAME, cert coverage ✓/✗, matched servers; filters: covered only / resolving only / hide wildcard suspects / by kind; search), **Servers** (grouped: server → hosts; "needs cert" first; unmatched IPs group; with the inventory's topology keys (§5.61) an intro line, each server's notes, "No certificate needed" / "TLS passthrough" for `terminates_tls=no`, each load balancer — a VIP pair together — followed by its backends, indented, the load balancers each name came through, VIP / NAT badges in the details, and a Topology column in the Servers CSV), **Behind CDN** (proxied hosts + origin hints + explanation + ready-to-run CLI command with the same POSIX / PowerShell toggle & download buttons for names.txt/targets.txt + link to `cli/ssl_origin_scan.py`; one shell choice drives the quick sweep, step 3's command and the Verify CLI card), **Verify** (only with a certificate; `ui/verify-panel.js` over §5.18–§5.19: every covered (public IP, name) pair of the scan checked from the internet through Globalping, with the CLI's verdicts (a Cloudflare Origin CA certificate and a self-signed one are labelled as such — ORIGIN_CERT / PRIVATE_CERT — never "Old certificate"; info behind a CDN, a warning where visitors reach them directly). Nothing is sent when the tab opens, not even `/limits`. Start and "Check again (n)" read the free quota and show the consent + cost dialog on the first send of the page session (consent is never stored; "Delete all local data" resets it); a partial run is offered when the quota does not fit, and a 429 stops cleanly with the reset time. Private, reserved and unaccepted pairs and checks past the 500 cap are listed as skipped and, with every pair the internet could not answer (TIMEOUT / CLOSED), go to a validated CLI command (`--cert new-cert.pem --json verify-cli.json`, POSIX / PowerShell) with a new-cert.pem download; CDN-edge pairs are listed as skipped too but stay out of it, because the CDN serves its own certificate there. Origin pairs (an inventory origin IP with a proxied name, from an origin hint or the zone file) are opt-in, off by default, and the first batch with one always shows the dialog with the origin sentence. The headline counts servers; the tab badge reads `live/total`; warnings carry tooltips; CSV (the CLI's 17 columns first) and JSON exports; the scan's full JSON gains `verification`. The job lives on the scan run: it survives navigation and language changes, toasts when it finishes in the background ("Show results" reopens this tab), and a new scan cancels it), **DANE** (only with a certificate; `ui/dane-panel.js` over §5.25, the panel of the Certificate view's DANE / TLSA tab. Nothing is sent when the tab opens. "Check TLSA records" looks up the MX hosts of the certificate's domains, its concrete names and the covered, resolving hosts the scan found under its wildcard names, with the file's other certificates as the chain. The summary points to the tab when the scan mined in-domain mail servers (`dns-mine:MX`). The tab badge counts the endpoints that would break or fail (error) or need a closer look (warn). The job lives on the scan run like Verify's: Stop ends it ("cancelled"), a new scan cancels it, and the scan's full JSON gains `dane` (`domainscope.dane/1`)), **Sources** (per-source status, counts, errors, timing), **CT certificates** (crt.sh/certspotter certs: issuer, validity, names; highlight the uploaded cert's serial and certs expiring ≤30 days). Exports CSV/JSON.
   **Several certificates at once** (a renewal week, §5.43, `ui/renewal-panel.js`). Step 1's loaders take several files (multi-select, drag & drop), a folder where the browser has `webkitdirectory` (FileDrop `directory`: only files whose extension `accept` lists; "Choose a folder") and several PEM blocks pasted at once (`CertLoader({ multiple, folder, onLoads })`); a single certificate has "Add certificates" next to Details / Remove (a hidden multi-file picker, after step 1's own drop zone so that one stays the step's first file input). Several files, or one file with several leaves, show the sets instead of the certificate summary: names, and per certificate its key type, validity, expiry, files, Details (the Certificate view shows that certificate: the shared current certificate becomes a file of it, and the renewal stays while it is one of its leaves; any other certificate chosen elsewhere replaces step 1's files) and Remove; files that add nothing are listed with why (a private key, a CSR, a PKCS#12 bundle, CA certificates only, no DNS name) and can be removed; a file that gave certificates but could be read only in part (a damaged PEM block next to good ones) gets a warning that a certificate may be missing, with the parser's details (`bundle.unread`); a certificate another loaded one probably replaces (`bundle.replaced`) gets a warning above the list — a server still serving it would count as updated, so remove it if it is the one being replaced — and a "maybe the old one" badge on its row; a drop zone for more and Remove all. Step 1's description says "Their names…" once several certificates are loaded. The step badge counts the certificates, the run bar says "with N certificates", step 2 is filled from every set's registrable domains. One scan covers the union of the names (`runScan({ cert: first, certs })`); the streamed hits are covered by the union too. With two or more certificates (`run.config.certSets`): the Hosts tab's Certificate column names each host's set; a **Renewal plan** tab after Servers shows the sets (key types, hosts, servers, expiry, files), the server × set matrix (rows = your servers, then addresses outside the inventory; cells = the names each needs from each set, an origin hint marked; on a phone labelled cards, `DataTable({ cellLabels })` + `td::before { content: attr(data-label) }`, a set a server needs nothing from left out) with the **Work list (CSV)** (`workListRows`; with the topology keys the server cells carry the notes, the work list ends with a Topology column, and the servers with `terminates_tls=no` are listed under the matrix instead, §5.61), and the hosts no certificate covers (labelled cards on a phone too); the summary's renewal line (with Renewal plan; `renewalSummaryText`: one set is named by its key types and "needs it", several "need one of them") stands in for "N servers need the new certificate", and the Verify line speaks of the certificates; Behind CDN's CLI card offers one download per certificate (`cliCertFiles`) and one `--cert` each in the command; Verify runs one queue for every set (one cost), with a Set column when there are two sets or more, the set sentence, `other-set` badges and the same `--cert` files in its CLI card; DANE checks one certificate at a time with a picker (a job holder per certificate on `run.daneHolders`, the tab badge over all of them); Copy summary names the sets; the full JSON gains `certificateSets`, `renewal` (assigned sets, plan rows, uncovered names) and a `dane` report per certificate checked. One file with one certificate is the classic flow, unchanged.
2. **cert** "Certificate": full parsed details (subject, issuer, validity with days left, SAN list, key, fingerprints, chain order, warnings), CAA check against issuer for each base domain (Allowed / Allowed, with restrictions — each distinct RFC 8657 combination and what it means for the next renewal / Blocked, with each unusable value and why; the Result column comes before Records, and below 600 px the record set moves under the name so the verdict stays in view), CT lookup of this serial via crt.sh and, below it, **Key continuity** (§5.57: the key's other certificates in CT, only on a click), a **DANE / TLSA** tab and a "find targets for this cert" button that opens scan pre-filled. The DANE / TLSA tab (`ui/dane-panel.js` over §5.25) checks the leaf of the file and its chain, whatever certificate is selected, and only on a click. While it runs, Stop takes the place of "Check TLSA records" and ends it ("cancelled"); the keyboard focus moves from the run button to Stop and back, never to `<body>`. It shows the headline, a "Publish before you install" card with the exact records to add and the 2 × TTL wait (error styling when an endpoint would break, warn styling when every item is a warning), the endpoints worst first (DNSSEC state, published records, an expandable explanation), CSV / JSON exports and this certificate's own TLSA values, computed locally. The job is kept per certificate across navigation and language changes. **No file?** (`CertAlternatives`, shared with SSL Targets step 1): a host name field that loads the newest valid certificate covering it from Certificate Transparency (`lib/ctcert.js`, §5.26; only on a click, Cancel while it runs, the outcome kept across a re-mount; any certificate change — the sample, a file, a hand-over, Remove — goes through `setCurrentCert`, which aborts a lookup still running, so its requests, the busy flag and a deferred language switch end at once), and **Try a sample**, which fetches `assets/data/sample-cert.pem` relative to the module (so it moves under `v/<commit>/` in the Pages bundle) and sends nothing to a third party. A CT load is a `CertLoad` with `source: 'ct'` and `ct: { host, provider, issuance, precertificate, newerPrecertificate, truncated }` (`ctCertLoad`); the sample has `source: 'sample'`. Both show a badge in the overview / step-1 summary and a note (`CertSourceNote`): for CT "Loaded from Certificate Transparency — the server may serve a different one", the issue date, a newer precertificate or a partly read list, **Check servers in SSL Targets** (the Certificate view hands the certificate over; the scan there leads to Verify) and the crt.sh page; the Chain tab says a log holds the leaf only instead of blaming a file. A crt.sh-only find (`manual`) lists the `?d=` download links and why the page cannot load them itself (one link when crt.sh holds only one id, and then no "use the other link"); every outcome says why crt.sh was asked (Cert Spotter's hourly limit with the time it is asked again, no answer or a refusal, a partial list, an unreadable copy; `ctOutcomeMessage`). A not-found says flatly that nothing is logged (and that internal names and private CAs never are) only on a complete answer: a list cut at the page cap, or crt.sh leaving some or all of its searches unanswered (`ctCrtshIncomplete`; its literal search misses the `*.parent` certificate when only that search failed), gives a hedged text, with Try again in the crt.sh case; a crt.sh find with a search unanswered adds that a newer certificate may be missing. A precertificate gets a warning (another fingerprint than the served certificate); loaded from CT, its note adds that no final certificate is logged yet, so it has to come from the server or the CA. After a load from the block (Enter, Load, Try a sample) the keyboard focus moves to the new source note (`focusLoadedCert`, tabindex -1) instead of falling to `<body>`; "Try again" hands its focus to Load (then Cancel); the hint below the field is tied to it with `aria-describedby`. The PEM & OpenSSL tab's copy-ready `openssl s_client` line (`sClientCommand`) uses only a certificate name that passes `cmdline.validateNames` unchanged (a `*.x` wildcard as `www.x`, else `example.com`): SAN bytes are not validated by the parser, so a hostile name is skipped, never pasted into the command. **PKCS#12 (.pfx / .p12)** (`CertLoader`, so the Certificate view and SSL Targets step 1 alike; `ui/pfx-import.js` over `x509.loadCertificates`, §5.47): a dropped, picked or pasted bundle opens a password dialog (a Modal labelled by its title; the focus in the password field; "Leave it empty if the file has no password"; "Check that the private key matches the certificate", off by default, with what it does to the key; Enter in the field or on the checkbox opens). A wrong password keeps the dialog open with the reason under the field (`aria-invalid`, the text selected): "does not match the file's integrity check — if you are sure of the password, the file is damaged", or, without a MAC, "no integrity check to tell the two apart"; Cancel / Escape loads nothing, keeps the certificate shown and toasts "<file> was not opened"; the field is emptied when the dialog closes. An opened bundle loads like any file (the chain in server order, SSL Targets step 2 filled from the leaf) and the keyboard focus moves to the note about it (`CertPfxNote`, tabindex -1): what it held ("3 certificates and 1 private key. Private keys are never shown or kept."), the friendly name, the certificates' and the key's encryption (cipher · KDF · iterations, a "weak" / "legacy" badge with a tooltip), the integrity check or "none", a warning for a plain keyBag or a password nothing checked, the key check ("The private key matches this certificate" with the algorithm; an error callout "does not match this certificate … cannot complete a TLS handshake", naming the certificate of the bundle the key belongs to; "not checked" with how to check it; no key; a key type the browser cannot check; a key whose encryption this page cannot undo — its name in the Private key row with "not supported here", and "cannot be checked" whether or not the check was asked; a key that did not decrypt) and **Download fullchain.pem** (`<cn>-fullchain.pem`: the leaf and its intermediates in chain order, no root, no key; the overview then drops its own "Download full chain"). A bundle that cannot be opened shows its reason as an alert — damaged, or an algorithm / mode this page does not support (named, with the OpenSSL command, `-legacy` for the old PBE algorithms), no WebCrypto outside https / localhost, or an operation this browser's WebCrypto refused (named) — and the keyboard focus moves to that alert (`pfxFocusTarget`: the note, else the first warning, else a drop zone on screen). **Missing intermediate** (`ui/chain-repair.js` over §5.49, `CertChainNotes`): for a server certificate (not a CA, a self-signed or a precertificate) the view looks its chain up in the bundled CCADB list when it renders (files of the site only; nothing is sent). Above the overview: "Missing intermediate found" ("N missing intermediates found") saying the file holds only the server certificate (or stops at an intermediate whose issuer is not in it; a CT load: "Certificate Transparency logs hold the server certificate only"), each added intermediate (name, issuer, CA owner, expiry), the source ("From this site's copy of the CCADB list (date). Your certificate was not sent anywhere." with ccadb.org) and **Download fullchain.pem** (`<cn>-fullchain.pem`: the leaf, then the intermediates, never the root or a key); a file whose chain ends at a root no store trusts gets "A current root is reachable" (warn) with the cross-signed certificate; a certificate from Certificate Transparency is titled "Intermediate found in the CCADB list" (a log holds the leaf only: nothing says the server lacks it); a lone server certificate whose issuer the list does not hold gets an info line naming the issuer ("a private CA, newer than this copy of the list, or expired or withdrawn"; an expired certificate: the list keeps only intermediates valid today, so its issuer may have gone with it); when the list cannot be read (offline: the service worker keeps only its manifest and roots table), a lone server certificate says so with Retry (which moves the keyboard focus to the notes that replace it). **Root store warnings** (error or warn callout) list each LifecycleWarning as a sentence with its date (a calendar day, UTC), the announcement link ("Announcement"; "Source" for a Mozilla date taken from the CCADB report) and "Root store data: CCADB and the stores' announcements, as of <date>". The Chain tab adds "Added from the CCADB list" (the added intermediates as chain items, "Not in the file") and where the chain ends with the stores that trust it (grouped by the root they trust it through when a cross-signed root is in the chain); the overview's and the Chain tab's "Download full chain", a PKCS#12 note's Download fullchain.pem and the PEM & OpenSSL tab's fullchain.pem (shown again when a lookup still running as the tab opened ends) include the added intermediates once found. The containers carry `data-chainfix` (running | done | error | none), the notes `data-chainfix-note` (repaired | not-found | failed) and `data-lifecycle`.
   **Does this CSR match?** (PEM & OpenSSL tab, below the key-match card): paste a CSR and **Compare** (Ctrl/⌘+Enter in the box): `parseCertificateRequest` + `csrMatchesCertificate` (§5.2) compare its public key with the selected certificate's, in the browser. A match says the certificate was issued for this key (from this CSR or another one made with it), and lists the names the CSR asked that the certificate lacks and those a CA added; a mismatch says it was made for another key (both keys named) — not issued from it, or re-keyed since. A private key is recognised the moment it lands in the box (`looksLikePrivateKey` on every input: its label, or bare base64 of a key's shape), never decoded or kept — the box and the module's copy are emptied at once, Compare or not, and the view says why; a certificate or other text says what it is. The pasted text lives in the module's memory only and is forgotten with the page session (another workspace, "Delete all local data").
2b. **renew** "Renewal readiness" (`#/renew?names=a,b&ca=<RENEWAL_CAS id>&challenge=http-01|dns-01|tls-alpn-01`; `unknown`, the default, is left out): will the next ACME renewal of these names validate (§5.44 `lib/renewal.js`)?
   - **Form:** the names (one per line, `*.` for a wildcard, up to 50; what is not a host name, and a wildcard directly under a public suffix, is said and left out); **Take the names from a certificate** — the Certificate view's loaders (a file, a host name's certificate from Certificate Transparency, the sample), sharing the page session's certificate with Certificate and SSL Targets — which fills the names and sets the CA from the issuer (`caForIssuer`; an issuer outside the list leaves "Not known" and the hint says so); the CA (Not known, or a `RENEWAL_CAS` entry) and the ACME challenge (HTTP-01, DNS-01, TLS-ALPN-01, Not sure). **Check readiness** runs `checkRenewal` with `noCache` (a check right after a fix asks the resolvers again); Stop keeps the previous report. A check started while an HTTP-01 test runs stops the test: the test card drops it at once (its buttons off while the check runs) and says the probes it had used were not read.
   - **Results:** a summary card (the worst verdict as the headline — "At least one name could not be checked — check again" when a name could not be checked —, counts per verdict, the CA and challenge, when and which resolvers, Copy summary, CSV, JSON) and one card per name, worst verdict first then as entered (a `<details>` with the name as an h3, its verdict and error / warning counts; open unless ready, all open for one or two names; the user's open / closed choice survives re-renders), its findings grouped by area with the data they rest on: the CAA record set and where it was found, each resolver's answer (only when they do not all agree), a delegation target, the name servers, a CNAME chain, the probes of a test.
   - **HTTP-01 test** (a card when the challenge is HTTP-01 or not known; a per-name button too): the names that can be tested (`http01Plan`: not a wildcard, a public address, a host Globalping accepts; at most 10), one measurement per name and address family (IPv6 too for a name with AAAA records) from Europe, North America and Asia, through `gateProbes` (purpose `'renew-http01'`: its own privacy text naming the hosts and the path, the cost in probes); every measurement is created before any is read. The results merge into the report (`applyHttp01`) and can change a verdict. A quota that cannot cover the test says when it resets; measurements already paid for that could not be read (an error, Stop, or the view was left) are read again for free. Once the probes are being sent, **Stop** ends the test ("stopped": the paid measurements can be read again; with nothing paid yet, as before the click). A test that stopped before all its measurements were created is partial: a planned family not measured this time keeps what the name's earlier test measured for it, with that time ("IPv6 · tested <earlier time>"), else reads "not tested" (`http01Families`) — less evidence never improves a verdict —; a name with none keeps what it had, and the done note says the test is incomplete.
   - **Keyboard:** the focus follows Check readiness ⇄ Stop; a test button keeps it while its test runs (busy through `aria-disabled`, not `disabled`), after the consent dialog closes (Escape too) and after the test; a Stop or "Read the results again" that goes away hands it to the next action of the card.
   - **Links and session:** Certificate (overview actions) and SSL Targets (step 1's certificate) link to `#/renew?names=<the certificate's names>&run=0` (`RenewalLink`): the names are filled in and nothing is sent. The CA comes from the shared certificate when the box holds exactly its names and the link names no CA — on arrival, when a carry has just replaced the box (with a kept report too, and in `update()`), or when the certificate is not the one the CA was last set from (a CA chosen by hand for the same names and certificate stays). A carry replaces the box while it is empty, still holds what the last check ran (every name, past the cap of 50 too) or what a carry or a certificate put there — never a draft; the CA and challenge a `run=0` link names are set only when the box took its names, and names other than those of the certificate that set the CA drop that certificate's CA hint (the CA stays). A shared link without `run=0` runs on arrival (quiet offline, like Domain Health), and in `update()` sets the CA and challenge it names, else none chosen and "Not sure". The current target fills the box (`TARGET_ROUTES.renew`); the finished report — with its tests — is kept for the page session (`result()` / `snapshot()`), and `update()` takes a new link while the view is open.
2c. **estate** "Certificate estate" (`#/estate`; nothing goes in the URL; §5.48): one or more `ssl_origin_scan.py --json` reports (`--estate` or not) as one inventory of every certificate the servers serve.
   - **Import:** a drop zone for `.json` files (several at once, one per site or jump host; '/' focuses it; Ctrl+V pastes), **Paste a report** (the JSON `--json -` prints; Ctrl/⌘+Enter reads it; a pasted text that is not a report stays in its open box), **How to make a report** (the `--estate` command, open while nothing is loaded). What is not a report is listed with the reason (`REPORT_ERRORS`: the `--csv` file is named as such); the same report again is a duplicate (a toast), never a second copy; at most `ESTATE_MAX_REPORTS`. The reports list shows each file, its scan time (UTC), servers, endpoints and certificates, a `--estate` / "without --estate" badge (only the `-n` names were asked) and a remove button; **Forget all**.
   - **Privacy:** the reports are read in the browser and kept in the module's memory only — no storage, no URL; a reload, Forget, another workspace or "Delete all local data" drops them; nothing touches the network.
   - **Notes:** an endpoint in several reports ("for each name, the newest report's answer is used"; reports of separate networks sharing private addresses one at a time), reports of an older CLI (key reuse not checked), no names asked (coverage cannot be told).
   - **Numbers:** tiles that filter the certificate list (pressed state, the focus stays on the tile), each a count of certificates, its hint what is behind them: certificates (on how many endpoints), expiring (expired or within 30 days), in a name conflict (hint: how many names), with a shared key (flagged `shared-key`; hint: how many keys need a look), weak, covering no name ("no names asked" when none were); the expiry buckets and the kinds as badge lines.
   - **Tabs:** *Certificates* — a filter select (`ESTATE_FILTERS` with counts, "Needs a look" among them), search, a DataTable of labelled cards below 640 px, soonest expiry first: the certificate and its name count, the expiry date and days left, the issuer with its kind, the key with its weak reasons, where it is served (two endpoints, then "+N more"), its findings as badges with what each means; a row's details: SHA-256, public-key SHA-256, serial, validity, signature, names, the names asked it covers, and every endpoint (servers, `ip:port`, "default" for the no-SNI certificate, the names it was served for, with several reports the reports behind it); **CSV** of what the filter shows (`estateCsv`, the CLI's `--estate --csv` columns, plus `report`). *Name conflicts* — per name, its certificates newest first (days left, kind, key, issuer, endpoints), "older" on a stale one. *Shared keys* — per key, its addresses and certificates (warn badges from 2 certificates or `SHARED_KEY_WIDE_HOSTS` addresses), "needs a look" when `sharedKeyNeedsLook`, its public-key SHA-256, certificates and servers; "no certificate" when no server returned one, "not checked" for reports of an older CLI.
   - **Copy summary** (above the tiles; §5.32 `estateSummary`, facts from `estateSummaryFacts`): the counts, what expires first and the names served with different certificates — certificate names only, never an address or a server of the reports — and the bare `#/estate` link; its tooltip says the link carries no file contents.
   - Tests: `tests/js/estate-view.test.js` (import, duplicates, errors, the cap, every generated string key, the summary's facts); the offline `estate` E2E suite (§8).

3. **global** "Global DNS": name + type → table of all resolvers (answer, TTL, rcode, AD, latency) + geo table via ECS (flag, country, ISP, answer, scope) + consistency groups with color coding + answer IP classification (CDN badge). Re-run button; share link. Every answer group names the operator of its addresses, and the summary gives `propagationVerdict` (§5.10): "Differs by design: CDN / GeoDNS edges (<operators>)" as info (naming the names before the CDN that steer to the same CDN names, if any; "No <type> records anywhere — the CNAME chains differ by design (<operators>)" when only the chains differ), or a warning with one line per finding (the groups' letters, the sources — joined with "; ", as a location name has a comma of its own — and whether it looks like propagation or a misconfiguration: SERVFAIL as a resolution fault, REFUSED and other rcodes as a refusal, a provider move when the queried name points to different operators, a CNAME that differs as a change, GeoDNS / weighted records or name servers that disagree), or an error "No source could resolve the name" when every answer is an rcode; a SafeSearch rewrite of a filtering resolver is named in the summary and not counted as a difference; the Distinct answers stat warns only when the verdict does.
   **Mainland China** (2026-10-02): the three locations asked through AliDNS (§5.8) are a row group of their own under the location table — a heading with a decorative flag, a description (AliDNS on behalf of 3 mainland ISPs; the types asked; the control question; no ECS scope; no DNSSEC validation) and a table with a Resolver column ("AliDNS (ECS)"), its own CSV / JSON export (`global-dns-china`). They take part in the answer groups, the IP table and the verdict like every location; a CNAME only they get while every other source agrees on its branch is "by design" with "<name> sends <locations> to <CNAME>, unlike every other source: the name's own DNS answers the resolvers there from a line of its own (typically for a CDN inside mainland China), not a change" (`glb.sum.designGeo`; as a finding next to a real problem, `glb.find.cnameGeo`), and an operator only they get is not called multi-CDN steering. Without a confirming control the text gives both readings and the TTL of the CNAME they got (`glb.sum.designGeoUnsure`, `glb.find.cnameGeoUnsure`), and the title and the Copy summary say "most likely" (`glb.sum.designTitleUnsure`, `glb.sum.nodataTitleUnsure`, `glb.sum.geoTitleUnsure` with `glb.sum.geoBodyUnsure`; `sum.global.*Unsure`). A `geo` verdict with a split tells it too. The description also says AliDNS is asked once more for A and AAAA, on behalf of a US subnet, to tell a China line from an older answer. For a type AliDNS is not asked for, their rows read "Not asked" (muted, with the reason), the Answered stat counts the rows that were asked and its hint how many were not, and the summary names them (`glb.sum.notAsked`). A SERVFAIL only they give is never blamed on DNSSEC (`glb.find.servfailNoDnssec`). Finding sources are listed in table order.
4. **lookup** "DNS Lookup": name + type(s) (checkbox group or "ALL common"), resolver select, DNSSEC toggle → sections per type with parsed records (SOA fields, MX priority, CAA tags, SVCB params …), flags (AD), raw presentation text, copy buttons. Density (`lookupLayout`, §5.37): a plain NODATA type gets no card but a place in the summary's "No records: AAAA, CAA, …" line (a disclosure with what NODATA means, the negative-caching time and the raw answers); the resolver that answered ("answered by …"), its PoP and the header flags are said once in the summary, and a card repeats only what differs (a failover to another resolver, other flags) plus a failover note or a cache hit; on wide screens the cards flow in CSS columns (`column-width: 480px`), and once every answer is in the first card of each further column is pinned there (`break-before: column`, made again after a Retry or when the width fits another number of columns), so opening a card's raw answer lengthens its own column instead of moving cards between columns; on phones the SOA timers sit two to a row and the TXT list shows four records (SPF / DMARC / DKIM first) before "+N more". The "No records:" label is in the text font, the type list in mono. The summary is updated in place as answers and Retries come in: the line keeps its node while its types do not change (and is drawn anew with the same open state, raw answer included, and keyboard focus when they do). A query that got no DNS answer keeps its card: which resolver failed (or "Every resolver tried") and why (`dohStatus`, §5.36), the attempts de-duplicated, and a Retry that asks that type alone again — at once, also while other types of the lookup still run; a new lookup cancels it (the keyboard focus returns to the card).
5. **bulk** "Bulk Resolve": paste hostnames (hundreds; a text / CSV list, or JSON: an array, name-like fields of objects, JSON lines) → table: name, status, CNAME chain, IPv4, IPv6, classification, PTR (optional), ASN (optional; its PTR queries use the run's resolver too), inventory match; progress (a cancelled run shows unfinished IP lookups as "not looked up"); CSV/JSON export. A PTR or ASN lookup that could not be made says "⚠ n/a" with the source and the reason (§5.36) — with PTR only, the reverse lookup's own failure (`ptr(ip, { throwOnError })`: e.g. "Reverse DNS: answered SERVFAIL"), never a silent empty cell.
6. **ip** "IP Intel": paste IPs → PTR, ASN, holder, prefix, country, provider/CDN, private flag, inventory server, reverse-IP domains (button per row; quota note). No silent dashes (§5.36): a PTR, network, prefix or location cell that a failed source left empty says "⚠ n/a" (tooltip and screen-reader text: each source and why, e.g. "RIPEstat: rate limited — try again in a few minutes"); a chip per service (RIPEstat, ipwho.is, reverse DNS) says how many rows it answered or where it failed; Retry — per row under the address, or per chip for every row its service failed on — asks only those sources again (`retry()`, §5.13) and the cells fill in; a Retry belongs to its run: a new lookup, Stop or leaving the view cancels it, and it never draws over a later run's row for the same address; after Stop, the rows never looked up leave no chip "asking…" (no chips at all when nothing was asked); a reverse lookup answered SERVFAIL / REFUSED says so ("Reverse DNS: answered SERVFAIL"); the row details list each failure in words, the JSON export gains `unavailable` (field → sources) and `sourceErrors`, and the CSV writes "n/a" in every UI language (a stable token for scripts). Stat cards whose count is zero (CDN / proxy, your servers — only with a saved server list —, private) fold into one muted sentence ("None of these addresses is behind a CDN / proxy."; `foldZeroStats`, §5.37).
6b. **ptr** "Reverse DNS" (`#/ptr?target=…&focus=…`; a link only fills the form and says it waits for a click — "Nothing has been sent yet"; the target is read with `targetTokens`, so `192.0.2.10 - 192.0.2.20` stays one range; the target and focus the page last wrote (`routeTarget` / `routeFocus`) are its own URL, so a reload or a re-mount keeps the form, while a link with the same target and another focus domain — or one opened by a hash change while the form holds a draft — fills the form; a link opened while a sweep runs waits until that sweep ends or is stopped — the form keeps the running sweep's target and focus and says which target waits — and then fills the form): the reverse DNS of every address of an IPv4 network up to a /22, a range, a list of IPv4 / IPv6 addresses or an AS's prefixes, forward-confirmed (§5.28).
   - **Form:** the target (the parsed count and each `parseSweepTarget` issue live under it: why IPv6 networks are not swept, a network over the cap with **Use <its first /22>** — or, wholly private or reserved, why it is not swept at all —, private addresses left out), an optional focus domain, Sweep / Stop (keyboard focus moves between them), the Settings parallelism, one example (AS3333), and what is sent. An AS turns the button into **List prefixes**: one RIPEstat request, then a picker (`parseAnnouncedPrefixes`): IPv4 prefixes with their size, ticked up to 1,024 addresses together (the running total, **Sweep selected**, Clear), a larger prefix disabled with **Use <its first /22>** (fills the form), a wholly private or reserved prefix disabled with why ("private space: not swept"), IPv6 prefixes listed but not swept, "no longer announced" marked (on a phone these notes sit under the prefix, their column hidden). **Sweep selected** follows a running sweep (disabled while one runs) and, when the picked prefixes hold nothing to sweep, says why instead of doing nothing. While RIPEstat answers, Stop cancels the lookup, and changing the target to anything but that AS drops it (no prefix list turns up later for an AS the form no longer holds); the busy state follows the sweep and the lookup together. A typed network drops an earlier AS's list. A finished sweep or lookup is announced once, when it ends, never again on a re-mount.
   - **Sweep:** `runPtrSweep` through the shared DohClient with `min(64, 2 × Settings concurrency)` addresses in flight and balance mode; the job belongs to the module (like Bulk Resolve): it keeps running on another view, a toast offers the results, a language switch keeps them. The URL gets `target` (the form's `targetTokens` joined with commas, so a range typed with spaces or an en dash is one `192.0.2.10-192.0.2.20` token; ≤ 400 characters) and `focus` (a focus edited later replaces it, an invalid one never goes in); Copy link in the header reads the focus at the click.
   - **Results:** progress, six stat cards (addresses, with a PTR name / generated, forward-confirmed / not resolving back or could not be checked — "every name resolves back" only when there are names and each does, "no PTR name to check" when there are none —, no reverse DNS (NXDOMAIN · empty), lookup failed (SERVFAIL), names under the focus domain — or the matched servers without one), clickable as filters; the table (`sweepRows`): focus rows first with an accent bar, then named hosts, pattern rows ("8 addresses", the template with an IP chip, "8 of 8 confirm"), addresses without a name, failures; the forward check as a badge with its explanation, the operator (`KindBadge`, from netinfo ranges or a PTR name under a provider's domain), the matching server; a Show filter (`SWEEP_FILTERS`; every sweep starts at "With a PTR name", and one that ends without any PTR name shows all its addresses unless the user picked a filter meanwhile; "Under your domain" is disabled without a focus domain, and clearing the focus while it is shown goes back to "With a PTR name") and **Expand patterns**; the filter and the search judge a pattern's addresses one by one (`sweepRowResults`): a pattern row stays while one of them passes and then says "1 of 8 match"; on a phone the forward check sits under the address (its column hidden); row details: the reverse name, each PTR name's forward answer, an RFC 2317 delegation, the template, the resolver, links to IP Intel and DNS Lookup; a pattern's details list its members.
   - **Exports and hand-offs:** CSV (`SWEEP_CSV_COLUMNS`, one line per address the rows shown stand for: of a pattern row only the members that pass the filter and the search) and JSON (`domainscope.ptr-sweep/1`: the same addresses, with the table's filter and search recorded and the summary of the whole sweep); names.txt (`sweepNames`, generated names left out); **Add names to a scan** (`scanHandoff` → `namesScanIntent`, then `#/subdomains?domain=…`, the user presses Scan); **Add to Servers** (`inventoryAdditions` against the editor's unsaved draft, else the saved text, so a second click adds nothing; `inventoryDraft` writes the hosts in that text's own format as `state.session.inventoryDraft`, then `#/inventory` with a toast that names the group — "in a new reverse_dns group" or "under your reverse_dns group": the user reviews and saves; nothing is saved here. A format it does not write, or an addition that would not read back as expected, leaves the editor alone: a dialog says why and shows the hosts as `name ip` lines with Copy, Download and Open Servers).
6c. **retire** "Retire an IP" (`#/retire?ips=…&domains=…`; a link fills the form and says nothing has been sent yet — a check sends hundreds of DNS queries, so a link never starts one; the params the page wrote for its own last check are no new link): before a server is switched off or renumbered, what still points at its address (§5.45).
   - **Form:** the addresses (one or more, or networks up to a /24; the count and each `parseRetireTargets` issue under it) and the domains to check. An empty domain box is filled in from the page session — the last Subdomains / SSL Targets scan (`state.session.scanHosts`), the imported zone's origin, unless that name looks internal (sessionZone `originInternal`, zoneorigins `looksInternalName`: a label or suffix, never a private address; `prefillDomains().internal`, said in a note while the box does not hold it) — and says where from; a domain typed into the box is checked as typed; a carried target (`run=0`) goes into its box (an address into the addresses, a domain or host name into the domains) only while that box is empty or holds the last check or what it took from a carry before, never over a draft. A disclosure lists, per domain, the host names checked besides its own records (from the last scan, the imported zone, passive hits asked to be checked, a discovery; a name the imported zone marks as looking internal is left out whichever source brings it, whatever the Zone File hand-off toggle says); a domain with none gets **Discover host names (Small wordlist)** OFFERED (with the query estimate written out next to it, not in a tooltip; the discovery engine with the Small wordlist, no passive source, no permutations), never run by itself — after it the check runs again. Check references / Stop (the keyboard focus moves between them); what is sent.
   - **Check:** `runRetireCheck` through the shared DohClient, two domains at a time, then the zone's candidates verified; the job belongs to the module (it keeps running on another view, a toast offers the result, a language switch keeps it). `ctx.runStarted` with a single address makes it the current target.
   - **Results:** the head card (the verdict — how many records break something, or nothing to clean up, or nothing points at it — with what is never covered; "nothing points at it" (green) only for a settled check (`retireGaps`): after a failed lookup, a "cannot tell" or a stop it reads "nothing found, but not everything could be checked" (warn) with the failed lookups, the "cannot tell" results and the domains not checked, and a verdict that lists records gets the same "the list may be incomplete" note; a domain card names the lookups that failed (MX, NS, the SPF record, the HTTPS record, host names) instead of "nothing in it points at the address", and a domain that does not exist (NXDOMAIN, no name servers) says so ("a typo?") and keeps the verdict off the green "nothing"; a chip of a source nothing reached reads "not checked"; Copy summary, CSV, JSON; progress; a status chip per evidence source: public DNS, SPF, zone file, your servers, passive reverse IP; the owners from the server list with their other addresses), stat cards (must change, breaks mail, zone file only, cannot tell, unverified — the zero ones left out but the first), then one card per group, each record with its severity, current value (and the addresses it reaches), what to change and the evidence (verification, the chain or include path, the qualifier, roles, the zone file line, the sources, the domain it was found for); on a phone each record is a card of labelled lines. Passive names that no longer point here are listed apart.
   - **Compare the old and the new server** (`ui/origin-compare.js` over §5.53), a card under the results and a shortcut form of its own (`data-shortcut-scope`: Ctrl/Cmd+Enter in its fields compares, Esc stops its run, never the view's check): host name, old address (the one address of the form while nobody has typed there), new address, path, port. **Compare (2 Globalping probes)** goes through `gateProbes` (purpose `'origin-compare'`, its own privacy text) and `runCompare`; the result is a verdict and a table of the fourteen fields (old · address / new · address, the certificate's names among them, the severity icon and note of a difference, a failure in the page's language with the probe's own line after it, labelled cards below 600 px), the probe's location, the two measurement links and a JSON download (`domainscope.compare/1`); a certificate problem both servers share is a warning under the verdict, not a difference. **Stop** ends it (focus moves Compare ⇄ Stop); a run outlives its card: leaving Retire an IP or switching the language mounts a new one, and the run ends on the card shown then; the old server's answer, measured before a stop or a failure (a quota 429) kept the new one from being asked, stays on screen alone. A private, documentation or reserved address, or a port a probe cannot ask, gets the `ssl_origin_scan.py --compare` command (Linux / macOS or PowerShell) instead of the button. The form and the last comparison are kept for the page session; "Delete all local data" and another workspace drop them.
   - **Passive lookup:** only on a click (**Find other names on the address**, at most 8 public addresses; the button sits under the chips with its request count and HackerTarget's daily quota written out next to it, linked by `aria-describedby`): HackerTarget and ip.thc.org per address; an address whose ip.thc.org list was cut off (one page of 100 names: `truncated`, with `total` when the service counts them) gets a note that the passive list is incomplete. The names are "unverified until checked" in their own card, whose **Check these too** (`checkTooPlan`) adds their registrable domains to the list while it has room — every domain in the box counts, not only the first 25 a check takes —, the names under a domain that is checked then as known hosts, and checks again; it is offered only when that makes progress, and the domains that cannot be added are named ("the domain list is full"). A name the check already resolved joins its row (or is gone).
7. **health** "Domain Health": domain → checks list grouped with severity icons + record panels + RDAP (registrar, expiry countdown) + DNSSEC + email security. An RDAP lookup that got no answer (`rdapStatus`, §5.36) marks the card's registrar, dates, status and registry name servers "⚠ n/a" with the reason written out above them, and the card's Retry asks RDAP alone again: `applyRdap` (§5.15) puts the registration data, its checks and the new score on screen without a DNS query, and the keyboard focus stays on the card. The CAA card lists every issue / issuewild value: usable, restricted by RFC 8657 (only these methods / one ACME account, with what that means for the next renewal from `caaRestrictionNotes`), unsatisfiable or malformed. The **MTA-STS policy** card (shown when the domain has MX hosts or an `_mta-sts` record) sends nothing until **Check the policy (1 Globalping probe)**: `ui/globalping-gate.js` `gateProbes` (one free `/limits` read; nothing sent when the quota is used up; the consent + cost dialog on the first send of the page session, with its own privacy text: the host name `mta-sts.<domain>` and the path go to Globalping and the result, policy included, is public by id for about six months), then one measurement (`create` + `poll`) read by §5.24 with the report's MX hosts, `_mta-sts` and `_smtp._tls` records (`mtaStsContext`: a failed lookup is passed as not known, and a `_mta-sts` set the report flags as mta-sts.invalid as `txtInvalid`, the card's TXT row then marked "not valid: senders ignore it"): headline, mode, max_age, HTTP answer, certificate, an MX host → pattern table, the findings worst first, the policy file, the probe and a link to the measurement. A failed MX lookup reads "lookup failed" in the DNS card too, as do a failed NS, SOA, A, AAAA, TXT or HTTPS lookup (`failedLookups`) and a wildcard probe no resolver answered (never "No"); in the "Domain resolves" check a family whose lookup failed reads "IPv6: lookup failed" (`LOOKUP_FAILED_PARAM`, worded by the view like the 'yes' / 'no' of boolean parameters), never "IPv6: —". A paid measurement that could not be read is read again for free ("Read the result again"), also across a language re-mount; a new health check drops the result; "Report (JSON)" gains `mtaStsPolicy`. The check never changes the score. The Email security card ends with **Reverse DNS of the mail servers (FCrDNS)** (`report.mailIdentity`, §5.15): MX host (a provider's marked), address (with a Reverse DNS link to `#/ptr?target=<ip>&focus=<domain>`), PTR name (a generic one marked) and the forward check (on a phone it sits under the address, and long names break, so the table fits the card); the mail-identity checks count in the score like any other.
7b. **reports** "DMARC & TLS reports" (`#/reports`; no param ever: the reports never go into a URL; §5.58–§5.60; offline-capable): a privacy note ("Everything stays in this browser"), then a drop zone (up to `MAX_REPORT_FILES` = 2,000 files at once — FileDrop `maxFiles`, bytes only —, a folder, "Choose files" — the view's Ctrl/Cmd+Enter —; up to 50 MB a dropped file, a plain report up to 32 MiB whether dropped or unpacked) that takes .xml, .xml.gz, .zip (a zipped mailbox folder too), .json and .json.gz, each told apart by its content. While it reads, a bar counts the files read ("3 / 10"; a dropped archive counts as the reports inside it once unpacked: "164 / 2,000") and **Stop reading** (Esc) ends it, between two reports, in the middle of an archive too: what was read before Stop stays, the reports of the drop and the archive it cut included, and the toast says how many ("Reading stopped. The 164 reports read before it are kept." / "Reading stopped. No report had been read yet."), the focus goes back to the drop zone; files dropped or chosen meanwhile join the read (announced) and the bar's total. A line counts the files, the DMARC and TLS reports, the reports dropped twice and what could not be used, listed with its path inside the archive and the reason (`rpt.problem.<code>`; the first 200, then "+N more"). Dropping more adds to what is read; **Forget reports** (offered once anything was read, files that were no report alone too) drops everything (and stops its lookups). The reports live in the module's memory: a language switch or a visit elsewhere keeps them (the page header then says "Reports read at <time>", without Run again), another workspace and "Delete all local data" drop them. A drop makes the busiest reported domain the current target. Then the DMARC tab looks up the current SPF of the shown domain (§5.59 `loadSpfContext` for each of `spfDomainsFor`; offline: `requireOnline({ quiet: true })`, no toast, the SPF line says so), and a **Check again** asks past the DNS cache. Results head: "Reports for example.com" / "Reports for 2 domains" with Copy summary (§5.32 `reportsSummary`: the DMARC domain on screen and the TLS summary of the same domain). **DMARC tab:** a domain picker when the reports name several (busiest first, with its message count); the head card (period and reporters; compliance — one decimal when it has one, never 100 % unless all, the number Copy summary says (`headlineShare`) —, messages, sending addresses and failing messages; the published policy as code chips (`t=y` too); the verdict as an alert — `data-verdict`, `data-look` (`verdictLook`: with `p=reject` in force, `enforcedLosing` when known sources fail and `enforcedSpfBroken` when mail that passed through SPF alone meets a broken SPF record, both errors) —;
