## 9. Headless runner `tools/ds.mjs` (Node 22+, no dependency)

A closed browser tab monitors nothing: `tools/ds.mjs` runs seven of the app's checks under Node over the DOM-free libraries (and a few pure helpers of the views, DOM-free at import: `views/subdomains.js` countHosts / subdomainsSummaryFacts / scanConcurrency, `views/renew.js` localParams, `views/zone.js` redactValues / originSecrets and its drift labels, `ui/dane-panel.js`'s strings), for cron and scheduled CI. Node only: `tools/` is not in the Pages bundle. English output (the Python CLI's language); nothing goes to Globalping.
```
node tools/ds.mjs COMMAND TARGET... [--list FILE]... [--json FILE] [--md FILE] [--baseline FILE [--fail-on-change]]
    [--resolver ID[,ID]] [--concurrency N] [--show-all] [-q] [--no-color] [-h] [--version]
  health DOMAIN...                                   lib/health.js domainHealth, one domain at a time; the summary: lib/summary.js healthSummary
  subdomains DOMAIN... [--level off|small|smart] [--sources a,b] | [--exact FILE]
                                                     lib/scanner.js runScan per domain (level default small; permutations and the recursive round as the app;
                                                     sweep concurrency views/subdomains scanConcurrency); --exact: exact mode with the file's names under each domain;
                                                     a stage of 1,000 names or more prints its progress in tenths (`stageProgress`)
  drift ZONEFILE [--origin ZONE] [--max-queries N] [--include-origins]
                                                     lib/zoneparse.js parseZone + lib/zonedrift.js driftZone (default budget 2,000, ≤ 10,000); a low-confidence
                                                     origin needs --origin (exit 2), a fatal parse is exit 2
  ct DOMAIN... [--days N] [--sources crtsh,certspotter]
                                                     lib/sources.js fetchAllSources (current certificates of the domain and its subdomains), grouped by
                                                     lib/passport.js issuerName of the issuer DN alone; "recent" = notBefore within N days (default 30)
  renew NAME... [--ca ID] [--challenge ...]          lib/renewal.js checkRenewal (noCache), in batches of RENEWAL_LIMITS.names; never the HTTP-01 test (Globalping)
  dane CERT.pem                                      lib/x509.js parseCertificates + lib/dane.js checkDane (noCache); a PKCS#12 bundle is exit 2 (give PEM),
                                                     a private key in the file is ignored with a warning, never read or printed
  audit DOMAIN|FILE... --policy FILE | --preset baseline|strict-mail|parked [--no-dkim]
                                                     lib/portfolio.js createPortfolio over each target's registrable domain (DKIM at the 8 common selectors unless
                                                     --no-dkim) + lib/policy.js auditPortfolio; a positional path or name ending .txt / .csv / .list / .lst is a
                                                     --list (a URL with a scheme is a target, never a file); the policy file (UTF-8 or UTF-16, as the app exports
                                                     it) is read and checked before anything is sent: an unknown rule or a value its rule does not take is exit 2
                                                     naming them; exit 4 while a rule fails, also one that could not be checked and failed when last checked
```
- **Command line** (`tools/ds/args.mjs`, pure): `parseCommandLine(argv) -> { command, targets, options, help, version }` over `node:util` parseArgs, a `UsageError` for anything refused (an unknown option or command, an option of another subcommand, a value out of range, `-` for a file, `--fail-on-change` without `--baseline`, `--json` = `--md`, `--baseline` = `--md`, `--json` or `--md` = a `--list`, `--exact`, zone or certificate file the run reads). Targets on the command line must be valid (domains through `domain.normalizeHostname`, a URL gives its host; renewal names through `renewal.parseRenewalNames`); a `--list` file's invalid entries are warnings (`parseListText`). Also `COMMANDS`, `COMMAND_SPECS`, `EXIT = { OK: 0, FAILED: 1, USAGE: 2, WRITE: 3, CHANGED: 4, INTERRUPTED: 130 }` (3 wins over 4; 4 is also an `audit` whose policy a domain fails — a rule that could not be checked is no failure, unless the report carries it as failed: it failed when last checked), `USAGE`, `DS_TOOL = 'domainscope-ds'`, `DS_VERSION`.
- **DoH:** one `DohClient({ chain, concurrency, fetchImpl })` per run over Node's own fetch, which speaks HTTP/1.1 only. `NODE_UNREADABLE` (quad9, quad9-ecs, cznic: HTTP/2 only, measured 2026-09-28 with Node 22 and 24) are refused by `--resolver` and left out of `NODE_CHAIN` = DEFAULT_CHAIN minus them (cloudflare, google, dnssb). The concurrency is the app's Settings default (12; `--concurrency` 1–32) and the balance pool follows the chain as in the app. renew compares CAA on CONSISTENCY_RESOLVERS minus NODE_UNREADABLE, whatever `--resolver` says, since the app asks them by name.
- **Before anything is sent** (`tools/ds.mjs` main): the `--list` / `--exact` / zone / certificate files are read (UTF-8, UTF-16 with a BOM, UTF-16LE without one: `decodeText`), the `--json` / `--md` paths checked (`checkOutputPath`: an existing directory that takes a new file, not a directory, a writable file — nothing truncated or left behind) and the baseline loaded (`loadBaseline`: JSON, then `diff.baselineProblem`). A baseline that does not exist is a first run only when it is also the `--json` file (`baseline: { file, missing: true }`), else exit 2. The invalid entries of a `--list` / `--exact` file are warnings: the first 5 quoted (`summary.cleanText`, at most 80 characters), then how many more (`skippedWarnings`). Reports are written whole or not at all (a temporary file renamed over the old one), after the summary; a write that fails is an error line and exit 3. Ctrl-C (or `timeout -s INT`) aborts the libs' signal: exit 130, nothing written, and the program leaves 200 ms later even while a request nobody waits for is pending (lib/rdap.js' shared bootstrap download is bound by its own timeout, not by the run's signal). The entry runs as a program also through a symlinked path (`realpath` of `argv[1]`).
- **Report** (`--json`): `{ tool: 'domainscope-ds', version, command, startedAt, finishedAt, options, warnings?, targets: [...], baseline?, changes? }`, one target per domain / name / zone / certificate (`tools/ds/commands.mjs`):
  - health `{ target, checkedAt, score (summary.healthScore), light, summary, failedLookups, checks: [{ id, severity, titleKey, params, title }], carried?: [{ area, from, checks }], report /* the view's Report (JSON) */ }`. `carried`: for each area whose lookup failed this run, that area's checks as the last run that read it found them (`from`: its `checkedAt`, null when no run read the area);
  - subdomains `{ target, mode: 'discover'|'exact', level, sources: [{ source, state, names, error, skipped? }], finishedAt, counts (views/subdomains countHosts), warnings /* codes */, seeded, hosts }`. The scanner's warnings (TRUNCATED, DNS_UNREACHABLE, BRUTEFORCE_TRUNCATED …) are also run warnings (stderr, the report's `warnings`) and "Warning:" lines of the summary, in the Subdomains view's words (`sub.warn.<code>`, `scanWarningParts`; the detail a code part). Hosts are `export.scanHostRows` without the TTL, the resolver, the certificate and inventory columns, addresses sorted. A discovery run seeds the previous report's resolving hosts, dangling aliases and hosts whose lookup failed (`baselineSeeds`, origin `baseline`), so a host is never gone, nor dropped from the watch, only because a source was down or one lookup failed, and lists (`reportHosts`) the hosts that resolve, dangling aliases, failed lookups and the seeded names; the rest — names the sources know that resolve to nothing (tens of thousands for a busy domain), wildcard look-alikes — are only counted. An exact run lists every name of its file. A host whose lookup failed (SERVFAIL, REFUSED, ERROR) keeps its last answer: `lastGood: { at, status, kind, provider, providerId, hidesOrigin, dangling, ipv4, ipv6, cnames }` (the baseline's, or the one it carried);
  - ct `{ target, days, readAt, sources: [{ source, state, ok, truncated, errorKind, error, skipped?, lastFullAt }], complete, answered, recent, issuers: [{ name, count, intermediates, newest }], names, certificates: [{ id, ca, intermediate, issuer, notBefore, notAfter, names, sha256, sources, carried? }] }`. Names are those under the domain (Cert Spotter lists every SAN); `id` = `ctCertId`, a digest of the validity, the names and the intermediate's CN, so crt.sh's and Cert Spotter's rows of one certificate are one; `ca` = `issuerName(issuer DN)` (the known CA, else the DN's O, else its CN), never Cert Spotter's friendly name, so a night on which only one source answers names the same issuers. `lastFullAt`: when the source last read the domain in full (this `readAt`, else the baseline's). `certificates` also holds the baseline's current certificates this run did not list while a source that listed them was not read in full (`carried: { from }`: when they were last read); `issuers` and `names` cover them, `recent` and the summary count this read (the summary says how many are kept for the next comparison);
  - drift `{ target /* origin */, file /* base name */, format, records, queries, planned, aborted, finishedAt, preflight, counts, rows: [{ key, name, type, status, reasons, file?, live?, added?, removed?, resolver?, rcode? }] }`. Values only for rows that need a look (warn, error, could not check), redacted with the Zone File view's rule (origin addresses behind proxied names → `[origin hidden]`) unless `--include-origins`;
  - renew `{ target, wildcard, verdict, findings: [{ id, severity, unchecked?, params, title }], detail /* renewalExport's entry of the name */ }` (options: ca, challenge, resolvers, chain);
  - dane `{ target /* subject CN */, serialHex, notAfter, headline, endpoints: [{ key: 'service|host', qname, service, port, host, status, severity }], domains, detail /* daneExportJson */ }`;
  - audit `{ target /* the registrable domain */, checkedAt, pass, fail, unknown, rules: [{ id, status: 'pass'|'fail'|'unknown', required, actual, evidence /* English */, key, params, last? }], row /* lib/portfolio.js exportRow */ }` (options: policy — the policy file's object, `policyObject` —, policyFile, preset, dkim, resolvers). Warnings: a TLD without RDAP, an RDAP lookup that failed (a name server domain's too), a DNS lookup that failed: the rules that read them could not be checked; a name server domain not registered ("anyone can register it and take over DNS"); a rule not checked this run that failed when last checked ("it still counts as failed": the run ends with exit 4).
- **Carried** (`tools/ds/carry.mjs`, pure; used by commands.mjs to write a report and by diff.mjs to read a baseline): what a run could not read, its report carries from the last run that read it, so the next run compares with that read and not with the gap. Health (`carryHealth`): per area — a check id's first part (`checkAreas`; `ns.rdap-mismatch` also needs RDAP) — whose lookup failed (`failedAreas`: the lookup-error checks and `failedLookups`, an MX failure also hiding `mail-identity`, a DMARC one `bimi`; `txt` feeds `spf`, `a` / `aaaa` `apex` / `ipv6`), the area's checks as last read (the lookup errors and the ids this run found left out); an area that failed in the baseline too goes on with its `from`. CT (`carryCt`): each source's `lastFullAt`; a certificate stays known until every source that listed it has read the domain in full without it, or it expires. Subdomains (`carryHosts`): `lastGood` of a host whose lookup failed. Audit (`commands.mjs auditTarget`): a rule not checked this run carries `last: { status, evidence, from }`, the status the last run that checked it found, while the requirement is the same.
- **Sources a run stops asking** (`createSourceBreaker`; ct and subdomains discovery over several domains): Cert Spotter after a rate limit (HTTP 429, or a readable X-RateLimit-Remaining of 0; about 10 full-domain queries an hour per IP address, shared on GitHub's runners) until its Retry-After, at most an hour (`ctcert.createCtCooldown`, CT_COOLDOWN_MS); crt.sh for the rest of the run once it failed as a service (unavailable, rate limited: up to three minutes of retries per domain) or timed out on 2 domains in a row. The domains after that list the source as `skipped` with its state ("not asked"); one warning says when and why.
- **Summary / Markdown** (`tools/ds/render.mjs`): English strings from i18n.js (`setupStrings` registers SUMMARY_I18N, HEALTH_I18N, RENEWAL_I18N and POLICY_I18N, and imports the Zone File view and the DANE panel for theirs). Each target is a lib/summary.js SummaryDoc — the app's own builders for health, subdomains and renew, the same model for ct, drift, dane and audit (one for the run — the policy, the counts, the rules still counted as failed (not checked this run, failed when last checked: why the run ends with 4 on a night no rule failed), its rules, the domains that meet every rule —, then one per domain with a failed or unchecked rule, FAIL before NOT KNOWN, each with its evidence and a carried fail saying so) — rendered with `renderPlainText` (stdout) and `renderMarkdown` (`--md`). With `--baseline` both open with "Changes since the baseline (FILE, run of …): N" in the CLI's words (at most 50 lines without `--show-all`; the Markdown at most 200, `MAX_MARKDOWN_CHANGES`), a tag per change, the not-counted note (the audit's names its own: rules not checked this run, a domain or rule added that meets the policy, a rule taken out of it) and `baselineNotes` (what the two runs did differently: the resolvers, the level / sources / mode, the CA or challenge, the budget, the certificate's serial). Untrusted values are code parts (`summary.textParts` for finding titles, `summary.renderParts` per line): a code span in Markdown, so no mention survives into an issue.
- **Changes** (`tools/ds/diff.mjs`, pure): `diffReports(command, before, after, { t, localParams }) -> Change[]`, the ones that count first (`orderChanges`); `Change = { tag, tone: 'bad'|'good'|'info'|'quiet', counts, target, item, kind: 'appeared'|'disappeared'|'changed', before, after, parts }`, in the report with its `text`. Tags: NEW, GONE, WORSE, BETTER, CHANGED, FAILED (to a failure state), RECOVERED, FAILING (failure to failure), SCORE, ISSUER, NAME, CERT, EXPOSED, DANGLING. Listed but never counted (no `--fail-on-change`, no nightly issue): FAILING moves; CT sources that could not be read (FAILED once, RECOVERED: the source's outage, not the domain's change); what a failed lookup may hide (a health finding gone while its area's lookup failed — said once, not again while it stays carried —, a finding "new" in an area no earlier run read, the score moved while a lookup failed in either run; a host missing from a discovery run's result); a renewed certificate from a known issuer for known names (CERT); an issuer or a name "new" that none of its certificates shows surely new (surely new: a source that lists the certificate now has read the domain in full before — ok, not partial, not truncated, at its `lastFullAt` — or the certificate was issued after the first of those full reads); an issuer gone; drift rows skipped this run or gone from the file. A baseline that could not read something carried the last read of it (Carried, above): the run is compared with that. RECOVERED's tone is the new state's (a move out of a failure into "will fail", a warn / error status or a host that no longer resolves is red). Per command:
  - health: warn / error findings by id (a repeated id by its worst severity; the baseline's carried checks included) new, gone, worse or better; the score; domains checked or no longer checked;
  - subdomains (wildcard suspects left out): a new resolving host (NEW) or dangling alias (DANGLING), one no longer resolving (GONE), a lookup failing (FAILED / FAILING), a proxied host now direct (EXPOSED), another kind or provider, a direct host's addresses (CHANGED; a CDN's rotating addresses are no change), a name taken out of an `--exact` file (GONE, counts). A host answering again after a failed lookup is compared with its `lastGood` (EXPOSED, CHANGED … "compared with its answer of DATE, before the lookup failed"), else RECOVERED;
  - ct: CT not read this run (FAILED, nothing compared), read again (RECOVERED, compared with the last read the baseline carried), ISSUER, NAME, CERT, an issuer gone (only after a complete read);
  - drift: a row's status by DRIFT_SEVERITY (WORSE / BETTER / CHANGED), to or from `error` (FAILED / RECOVERED), a problem row whose live values changed, a problem row new in the file, the name servers' match (`same` / `overlap` / `disjoint`);
  - renew: the verdict (fail < unknown < warnings < ready; to `unknown` is FAILED), warn / error findings within one verdict (unchecked ones left out), names new or gone;
  - dane: each endpoint (service and host) by DANE_SEVERITY, to or from `error`, endpoints new or gone; compared whatever the certificate's subject (a note says when the serial differs);
  - audit: per domain and rule, pass → fail WORSE, fail → pass BETTER (the evidence alone moving — a day fewer left — is no change); to "not known" FAILED (listed only), and checked again compared with the status it carried (no change when it is the same; RECOVERED when no run had checked it, counted when it fails); a rule new to the policy or with another requirement NEW (counted when it fails), a rule gone GONE (listed only), a domain new (counted when a rule fails) or gone. The evidence's values (a registrar, CAA issuers) are code parts. Notes: the policy differs from the baseline's; DKIM checked in one run only.
  `baselineProblem(doc, command)`: not this tool, another major version, another command, or a list the comparison walks that is damaged (with its index) → exit 2 naming it.
- **Nightly template** (`docs/examples/nightly-domainscope.yml`, `docs/examples/README.md`): a scheduled workflow for a private repository with a `domains.txt`. It checks out that repository and DomainScope (`ref:` to be pinned to a commit SHA, or a release tag once there is one), both with `persist-credentials: false` so no token sits in `.git/config` while the checks run (the commit step hands the token to its `git pull` / `git push` alone through `-c http.<server>/.extraheader`; the issue step's gh reads `GH_TOKEN`), runs `health` and `ct` (subdomains, an exact host list, drift, renew and the policy audit — exit 4 while a rule fails, so the issue stays open until every rule passes — are commented out) with `results/NAME.json` as both `--baseline` and `--json`, `--md results/NAME.md` and `--fail-on-change` (exit 4 = changed, any other non-zero = failed), commits `results/`, then keeps one issue labelled `domainscope` through the gh CLI (created, or edited and commented, when something counts; closed on a night with none on which every check completed; left open with a comment on a night a check did not complete, which compared nothing) and fails the job when a check did not complete. Each check runs under `timeout -s INT` for at most `CHECK_MINUTES` (20), and all of them within `RUN_MINUTES` (45) of the job's `timeout-minutes` (60), so a slow night is a failed check (124) and the commit and issue steps still run. The template and its README say that more than about 10 domains exhaust Cert Spotter's quota and offer `--sources crtsh`. Its header says: use it in a PRIVATE repository, never commit inventories or zone files unless you mean to, no secret is needed (the GITHUB_TOKEN with `contents: write` and `issues: write` only).
- **Tests:** `tests/js/ds-runner.test.js` — the command line, every diff, baseline validation and notes, the renderers, what a run carries (health areas over three nights, CT over an outage and a partial read, a host's last answer), offline runs of `main()` with an injected fetch (the fake DoH of `tests/js/ds-fake-doh.mjs`: the Zone File e2e suite's fake live zone of the Cloudflare export fixture; crt.sh and Cert Spotter answered in the test: a 429 and a 503 over a list, a night with crt.sh down, four nights of `ct --list` across a crt.sh outage with a new CA issued during it; health over four nights with a TXT SERVFAIL; a seeded host through a night of SERVFAIL to a new address), the source breaker, the progress lines and the scanner's warnings, a spawned runner whose fetch is that fake (`node --import tests/js/ds-fake-doh.mjs`, DS_FAKE_DOH=1), a spawned runner stopped with SIGINT on a network that never answers (DS_FAKE_DOH=hang; POSIX only), a run through a symlinked checkout, every `ds` line of the template and every example of the README and `--help` parsed, and the template's time limits and failed-night issue handling. `audit`: its command line (a policy or a preset, a list file as a target, a URL never one, registrable domains), the diff with carried statuses (a domain added that meets every rule listed, not counted), four offline nights over the portfolio's fake zones and RDAP registry (`portfolioZone`, `createPortfolioFetch`: RDAP once per domain from the registry the bootstrap names, rdap.org only when it fails, .tr not known; a name server domain renewed; a night RDAP fails and the night after, with no change), a policy file the app exported (exit 0), DKIM off, a file with a typo or a key outside `rules` (exit 2, nothing sent), a registry lock and a name server domain gone (its warning), a rule carried as failed (exit 4, said why), and a spawned run with `DS_FAKE_DOH=portfolio` (exit 4, then 0).
